# 220: The Plump Cat, Mew, and the Lost Soul

The rain that had fallen throughout the night stopped the next morning, but the temperature had dropped significantly.

For Johan, though, a slight chill like this was nothing.

Between Gengar's frequent licks, which seemed to make him immune to paralysis, and his natural resilience to cold, Johan believed his resistance to cold weather was his true superpower.

That said, given winter's approach, Johan still threw on a hoodie before dragging his Pokémon out of bed.

"Gengar, lead the team—brush your teeth! Let's go!"

"Ka-kah~~"

Gengar gurgled and spit out its mouthwash, flashing its sparkling white teeth in the mirror.

With a thumbs-up to itself, it admired the glint of its teeth.

"Ka-kah!"

During breakfast...

"If there's nothing else today... we'll focus on training."

Moves like Thunder and Overheat could use a little more fine-tuning.

The Pokémon naturally had no objections.

After all, the final and toughest Gym in Kanto awaited them—they had to win!

All of Johan's Pokémon were full of energy and determination.

"But since the Weather Institute isn't fixed yet, let's head to the beach today!" Johan declared.

The Pokémon all perked up at the mention of the beach.

Truth be told, despite spending so much time in Rustboro City, Johan had rarely visited the local shores.

However, plans were made to be broken.

Just as Johan packed everything for the day—lunchboxes, picnic blankets, fruit juice, and Moomoo Milk—a little visitor appeared at the café.

It was an Eevee.

And not just any Eevee—it was a slightly chubby Eevee!

"Eevee~~"

The Eevee curiously padded around the café, stepping on the soft cushions before hopping onto the counter. It pawed at the glass jars and utensils, producing a series of crisp, clinking sounds.

The noise caught Gengar's attention.

Poking its head through the ceiling like a phantom, Gengar peered down and froze.

"Ka-kah?"

When it saw the chubby Eevee on the counter, it hesitated.

"Eevee~~(｡･ω･｡)"

The Eevee, completely unbothered, waved cheerfully at Gengar.

Gengar scratched its head, a faint sense of familiarity tugging at it.

At that moment, Johan also walked downstairs to check the commotion.

When he spotted the Eevee licking its paws atop the counter, his heart skipped a beat.

That feeling...

"Eevee~~"

Before Johan could speak, the Eevee took two bounding steps and leapt straight into his arms.

Feeling its soft weight, Johan smiled wryly.

"You've gotten chubbier, haven't you, Mew?"

"Eevee?"

The chubby Eevee looked surprised that Johan had recognized it.

But it soon giggled, covering its mouth. A flash of white light surrounded it as its form shimmered and transformed back into its true self—Mew.

"Mew~ Mew~~"

Sure enough, it was Mew.

Thanks to his Aura powers, Johan could sense the familiar energy radiating from it.

Mew's ability to transform wasn't limited to appearances—even its Aura signature could change.

If not for the fact that Johan's Aura had awakened through Mew's help, he might not have recognized it.

However...

Johan raised an eyebrow as he stared at the slightly plumper Mew.

Have I really turned Mew into a housecat?

A tender smile crossed Johan's face. Holding the rounder, fluffier Mew, he couldn't resist pressing his face into its soft belly.

The plush little Mew was even more huggable now.

Despite its smooth appearance, Mew was covered in a fine layer of fur, making it incredibly satisfying to pet.

"Mew~ Mew~!"

Blushing slightly, Mew squirmed in Johan's arms before giving up and settling down.

"W-wooo?!"

Vulpix appeared at the top of the stairs, stunned by the scene.

"Mew~~"

"Woo~~"

Soon, Mew was tumbling and playing with Vulpix, Tinkatuff, and Dragonair.

Meanwhile, Gengar was busy downloading movies and games for Mew on her phone, handling the process with the skill of a tech professional.

Mew had come here not only because it missed Johan, Gengar, and the others, but also because...

It had run out of games to play and shows to watch.

After all, the World Tree didn't have Wi-Fi.

Feeling Vulpix's occasional jealous glances, Johan rubbed his temples.

Caught red-handed cuddling Mew.

"Ahem." Johan cleared his throat. "Gengar, download this episode for Mew, too."

He handed Gengar his phone, which contained the unreleased first episode of Meowth and Pichu.

"Ka-kah!"

Gengar nodded confidently.

"It'll take a while to download, so let's go to the beach while we wait," Johan suggested.

Maybe the little ones can even learn a trick or two from Mew—that'll save me some money.

"Mew~~"

Mew's eyes lit up with excitement as it nodded eagerly.

"Woo~~"

Vulpix huffed, turning its head slightly.

Johan chuckled, stepping forward to scoop up the sulking Vulpix and ruffle its fur affectionately.

"Come on, Vulpix."

Vulpix squirmed briefly before relenting, its fur now a tangled mess.

"W-Woo~~(((//Д//)))"

"But Mew, you'll have to change forms."

While most people wouldn't recognize Mew, Johan didn't want to risk unnecessary trouble.

"Mew~~"

Mew playfully winked but didn't transform. Instead, its body became transparent.

A moment later, Johan felt a soft, warm weight settle onto his shoulder.

Rustboro Beach.

The morning sunlight reflected off the sea's surface, casting rippling waves of light.

A gentle breeze carried the faint scent of saltwater, and Wingull soared back and forth in search of breakfast.

Compared to Vermilion City's bustling shores, Rustboro Beach was quieter, especially as winter approached.

There were still some visitors, however, along with food carts and small stands.

Johan and his Pokémon strolled around, buying snacks before settling on a quiet stretch of beach.

Mew, now visible again, floated cheerfully nearby.

After laying out the picnic blanket and setting down snacks and toys, Johan turned to Gengar.

"Ka-kah?"

Gengar emerged from the shadows, already wearing swim goggles and a swimsuit it had somehow procured, ready to dive in.

Johan pulled its mouth open and started fishing around inside.

"Sharpedo's teeth? Gyarados scales? Golbat fangs? Weedle silk?"

Just how much stuff have you been hoarding?!

"Ka-kah..." (Listen, let me explain...)

Johan rolled his eyes. He knew Gengar's collection habits all too well.

"Where's my beach umbrella and chair?"

"Ka-kah!"

Gengar obediently rummaged through its mouth, pulling out a sun umbrella and reclining chair. It even set them up for Johan with a grin.

"Don't bother the Pokémon in the water too much, all right?" Johan said as he settled comfortably into the chair.

"Ka-kah!" (Of course! As long as they don't provoke me first, I won't touch their stuff!)

With Johan's permission, Gengar cackled gleefully and charged into the waves.

Nearby, Vulpix and Mew built sandcastles while Tinkatuff and Dragonair surfed the waves together.

The gentle warmth of the sun lulled Johan into a peaceful state.

Hmm?

Out of the corner of his eye, he noticed a faint black dot far off on the horizon.

It hovered in place, circling back and forth but never coming closer.

Johan squinted but ultimately shrugged it off, deciding it wasn't worth worrying about.

Hoenn, Dewford Town.

On the vast ocean, massive waves crashed like rolling mountains.

Atop one such wave, Dewford Gym Leader Brawly expertly rode his surfboard, grinning with exhilaration.

Beside him, Bea—her tanned skin glowing with health—matched his moves with ease, riding the surf like a pro.

"Do you feel it, Bea?!" Brawly shouted, clenching his fist.

"Feel what?"

"Look at my biceps! My pecs! My back muscles! Muscles never betray you! The effort you put in comes back to you tenfold—just like raising a Pokémon!"

"Yes, sir!"

Bea replied firmly, though her thoughts drifted to something else entirely.

It's been so long since I had cream cake...

Despite the challenge, Bea had quickly mastered Brawly's surfing techniques, impressing him with her talent.

For her, though, controlling the waves was no different from kneading dough—a skill she had honed long ago.

Did Johan send me here to prove the results of my training?

As the waves calmed, the pair paddled back to shore.

Just as they prepared for more training, Bea's phone rang.

When she saw the caller ID, her brow furrowed slightly, but she still answered.

A cheerful, familiar voice rang through the speaker.

"Ha ha ha! Bea, I heard you're training in Hoenn!"

"You're in Hoenn too?"

"Yup!"

"So...?"

"Uh... I think I'm... lost."

# 221: Champion-to-Be, Today Might Be Your Lucky Day

The sun shone brightly, and the weather was perfect. Johan lounged leisurely on a beach chair.

He sipped on a glass of freshly made Cheri Berry juice, chilled with ice cubes that Vulpix had just crafted.

In one hand, he held his phone, browsing through the notes Gengar had transferred about The Secrets of the Earth.

"Hm... so that's how it works..."

As Johan continued reading, he found that his initial impressions were correct. Beyond detailing the strategies for commanding, battling, and raising Ground-type Pokémon, the guide contained invaluable wisdom.

It was a treasure trove of Giovanni's accumulated experience in Pokémon training.

Of course, it also included insights into destructive moves like Earthquake and High Horsepower.

While not everything could be directly replicated, there was plenty to adapt and learn from.

The only downside?

Giovanni's training methods were extremely costly. Without significant financial resources, it would be nearly impossible to fully implement the guide's techniques.

Still, even a surface-level application would likely elevate an average trainer to an elite level.

As Johan scrolled, his phone suddenly rang.

It was Bea.

Upon answering, her voice came through, tinged with a bit of embarrassment.

"Johan-sensei, have you returned to Hoenn?"

"Yeah, I'm back. I'm in Rustboro right now," Johan replied, puzzled. "Why? What's up?"

"I have a... friend. I guess you could call him a friend. He's arrived in Hoenn—or at least, he should have by now."

Her hesitant tone made Johan even more confused.

"A 'friend,' huh? And what does she mean by 'should have arrived'?"

"Anyway," Bea continued, "he's lost. I was hoping you could help find him."

"Lost?"

Who still gets lost in this day and age? Don't they have GPS?

Wait a second...

Johan suddenly thought of someone. His expression grew strange.

"Your friend wouldn't happen to be Leon, would he?"

"Sensei, you know him?"

Johan sighed.

Know him? Oh, I know him all too well.

The so-called "Strongest Champion," famously known as the King of Ads, Master of Getting Lost, and Champion of Posing...

Even setting aside Johan's prior knowledge, Leon was a household name in the Pokémon world, constantly appearing in commercials and news reports.

"So, Leon is in Hoenn?"

"Yes. He said he's here to visit your former headmaster on behalf of his mentor."

That made sense. The former headmaster of Johan's school was a close friend of Mustard, Leon's mentor.

"But," Bea added, "I don't know exactly where he is now. He said he was over the ocean, and... well... he got lost."

"Over the ocean?"

Johan's eyes narrowed. He turned to gaze at the distant sea, where he had earlier noticed a small black dot aimlessly circling.

"Sensei, do you think you could ask Officer Jenny to help search for him?"

Asking Officer Jenny to find a Champion?

Wait, Leon wasn't technically a Champion yet.

"That won't be necessary," Johan replied. "I think I've already found him."

"Huh?"

"Let me confirm. I'll call you back."

After hanging up, Johan called out to Gengar, who was playing in the water.

"Ka-kah?"

Johan pointed toward the horizon. "Is that a person over there?"

Gengar shaded its eyes with a hand, squinting into the distance.

While it didn't spot anything, it took Johan's word for it and began floating toward the indicated direction.

Before long...

"Ka-kah!!"

Gengar zipped back like a mischievous gremlin, cackling wildly. Its tongue lolled out, and it darted across the water like a speeding jet ski.

Chasing after it was an orange Charizard, its wings flapping furiously but unable to close the distance.

Riding on Charizard's back was a young man with purple hair, a flowing cape, and stubble on both cheeks.

It was unmistakably Leon!

Though Leon looked like his usual self, both he and Charizard were clearly in distress.

"Ka-kah!"

Gengar reached Johan and handed him a peculiar-looking wristband.

Johan sighed, recognizing it instantly.

Of course, it's the Dynamax Band.

The device allowed Pokémon to undergo Dynamax transformations—essentially growing giant-sized during battles.

No wonder Leon and Charizard were in such a rush.

"Gengar..." Johan groaned, rubbing his temples. "You really do know how to find the good stuff."

When Charizard landed on the beach, kicking up sand, Leon hopped off its back.

As he took a deep breath of relief, Johan held out the Dynamax Band.

"Here. I assume this belongs to you."

Leon blinked before hurriedly accepting the band.

"Thanks, Gengar," he said with a sheepish smile.

"Ka-kah~"

Gengar waved nonchalantly and explained that Leon had intentionally handed it over as a breadcrumb trail.

Johan raised an eyebrow.

He really trusted Gengar with something so important?

"I didn't expect it to belong to your Gengar," Leon said, his tone warm.

Johan wasn't surprised that Leon recognized him.

"Leon, what brings you to Hoenn?"

"Well, I got a little lost," Leon admitted, scratching his head.

A little?

Leon revealed that he had left Johto three days ago.

Instead of flying straight to Hoenn, he had somehow spent two days circling aimlessly over the ocean.

"Three days?!"

Johan's jaw nearly dropped.

Johto wasn't that far from Hoenn. With Charizard's speed, the trip should've taken a few hours at most.

But Leon had managed to get lost for three whole days—and he'd flown west instead of east, landing on the farthest western point of Hoenn.

Classic Leon, Johan thought. The King of Getting Lost.

"Ha ha, I'm not great with navigation," Leon admitted with a laugh, brushing it off as normal.

"Charizard can't navigate either?" Johan asked skeptically.

"Roar!"

Charizard snorted, puffing smoke from its nostrils, its expression betraying a mix of pride and embarrassment.

"When it was younger, it could navigate just fine," Leon explained. "But now... well, not anymore."

Johan facepalmed.

So navigation skills are contagious?

He glanced at his own Pokémon.

My team hasn't picked up anything weird from me, have they?

"Hungry?" Johan asked.

At the mention of food, Leon and Charizard perked up immediately.

"Very! And Charizard says this is the best Pokéblock it's ever had!" Leon declared as they devoured several portions.

Johan watched them eat, silently noting the various sponsorship logos on Leon's cape.

Even the future Champion has to hustle for ad money, huh?

"Want me to take you to the academy?" Johan offered once Leon had finished eating.

"Really? Thanks, Johan-sensei!" Leon replied, his gratitude genuine.

Johan nodded but added, "But first, why don't we battle?"

Leon's eyes lit up at the challenge, his trademark enthusiasm kicking in.

"Of course!"

Leon struck a dramatic pose, pointing toward the sky with his cape billowing behind him.

"I challenge you, Johan-sensei!"

As the self-proclaimed strongest trainer in Galar, Leon's goal was to challenge trainers worldwide, learning from each battle to strengthen Galar as a region.

Johan, however, wasn't keen on the idea of fighting Leon directly.

His gaze shifted subtly to the Pokémon nearby.

Mew, who was perched invisibly on his shoulder, suddenly had a bad feeling.

"Mew?"

It blinked innocently, but Johan's smirk told it everything it needed to know.

Leon, you might've bitten off more than you can chew today.

# 222: "Mew Brand Eevee: A Must-Have!"

Leon maintained his signature pose, as though surrounded by a hundred cameras.

This wasn't just a habit but a product of his upbringing in Galar, where every battle was treated like a grand performance.

In Galar, the region's official matches—whether Gym Battles or major tournaments—were massive spectacles held in enormous stadiums, a necessity due to the Dynamax phenomenon.

Ordinary Gyms couldn't handle the destructive force of Dynamax battles, often crumbling under the strain.

This showmanship-driven culture significantly boosted Galar's economy, making it an essential part of the region's identity.

As Galar's future champion, Leon carried the responsibility of introducing Galar's unique culture to the world.

Years of promotion by Macro Cosmos and the Galar League had made his over-the-top gestures a natural reflex.

While Leon struck his dramatic pose, Johan, standing opposite, used his Aura powers to communicate with Mew, who had disguised itself as Eevee.

"Mew, I need a favor."

"Meow?"

"Help me fight this guy."

Despite being a Mythical Pokémon, Mew wasn't naturally inclined toward combat. It could fight, but it just didn't enjoy doing so.

As expected, Mew immediately shook its head.

"Meow~"

Johan chuckled, understanding its reluctance.

"Okay, how about this—I'll buy you two new toys afterward."

"Meow?"

Mew's eyes lit up at the mention of toys.

Still, it pouted for a moment before raising a tiny paw to indicate four toys instead of two.

Leon had no idea that his earlier ravenous eating had painted him as "pitiful" in Mew's eyes, warranting this negotiation.

"Deal," Johan agreed without hesitation.

"Meow~♪"

Mew swayed its tail happily.

"Just disguise yourself again," Johan reminded. "No need to attract unnecessary attention."

Mew nodded, understanding the potential dangers. Despite its power, it was still a target for those who coveted rare Pokémon—Team Rocket and Pokémon hunters, to name a few.

It wasn't that Johan distrusted Leon, but Macro Cosmos' chairman, Rose, was a different story.

Everyone knew that Leon owed much of his success to Rose's support.

With the deal struck, Mew transformed back into Eevee.

Meanwhile, Leon was still holding his pose, his muscles visibly straining. Only when Johan finally spoke did he relax.

"Alright."

Leon exhaled, his tension easing.

"Then let the battle begin!"

He tossed a Poké Ball high into the air.

From the burst of red light emerged a towering Haxorus, its olive-green armor gleaming in the sunlight. Its crimson blades and muscular limbs exuded raw power.

As a Dragon-type Pokémon, Haxorus radiated an intimidating aura, its crimson eyes focused and determined.

Johan was momentarily surprised.

Leon had chosen Haxorus instead of his usual Galarian starters like Cinderace, Inteleon, or Rillaboom.

Perhaps Leon intended this as more of a sparring session than a serious match.

But it didn't matter.

Mew was ready to dominate.

"Go, Eevee!" Johan announced.

A chubby little Eevee hopped out from behind Johan, landing on the battlefield with an adorable chirp.

"Bui~!"

Leon's serious expression didn't falter, even as Johan sent out an Eevee.

As a seasoned trainer, he knew better than to underestimate his opponent, no matter how cute their Pokémon seemed.

"Haxorus, use Rock Slide!"

"Roar!"

Haxorus roared and slammed its tail into the ground, sending a barrage of jagged rocks hurtling toward Eevee.

Johan smiled.

"Eevee, Misty Terrain."

"Bui~!"

A soft pink mist spread across the battlefield, enveloping the entire area in seconds.

The incoming rocks collided with the mist and crumbled harmlessly.

[Misty Terrain reduces the damage of Dragon-type moves by half and prevents status effects.]

Leon blinked in confusion.

Since when can Eevee learn Misty Terrain?

Haxorus, too, seemed momentarily disoriented as the mist dulled its focus.

"Dodge it," Johan commanded.

Eevee deftly leaped from rock to rock, avoiding Haxorus' attack with graceful precision.

"Haxorus, Close Combat!"

Leon quickly shifted strategies. In the Misty Terrain, Dragon-type moves were weakened, so a Fighting-type move was the obvious choice.

"Mew—Magic Shine!"

"Bui!"

The Eevee emitted a dazzling burst of rainbow light, blinding Haxorus and halting its advance.

The searing brilliance left Haxorus roaring in pain, its defenses crumbling under the relentless assault.

"Enough," Johan said, raising a hand.

The battle had lasted only moments, but it was clear that Haxorus was outmatched.

Leon withdrew his Pokémon, nodding solemnly.

"We lost."

Even with the type advantage, the Eevee's sheer power was overwhelming.

Leon's mind raced as he analyzed the fight. This Eevee's strength rivals that of an Elite Four Pokémon!

Eevee chirped cheerfully and hopped back to Johan, tail wagging happily.

Its reward came in the form of a delicious Pokéblock, which it accepted with gusto before scampering off to play with Vulpix and the others.

Leon was quiet for a moment before stepping forward and extending his hand.

"Johan-sensei, thank you for the battle. Next time, I hope we can have a full six-on-six match."

Johan chuckled and shook his hand.

"I only have four Pokémon right now."

Leon blinked in surprise, glancing at the Pokémon gathered around Johan. Including the Eevee, there were five.

Noticing Leon's confusion, Johan smiled and explained, "That Eevee isn't mine. It's a friend."

"Not yours?"

Leon's eyes widened.

Even without being officially captured, this Eevee had fought with such precision and trust—it was hard to believe it wasn't Johan's Pokémon.

Leon's admiration deepened.

Johan-sensei is truly incredible. Not only is he a brilliant tactician, but even wild Pokémon trust him implicitly.

Johan didn't bother correcting Leon's assumption.

Explaining that "Eevee" was actually a Mythical Pokémon in disguise would probably sound ridiculous anyway.

As Leon departed, Johan made a mental note.

If Leon ever challenges me again, I'll just let Steven handle him.

A smile tugged at his lips.

You want to fight me? Beat my student first.

# 223: "A Keen Eye for Talent!"

Kanaz Sea Beach.

Knowing Leon's legendary "sense of direction" (or lack thereof), Johan wasn't surprised when the headmaster quickly dispatched someone to pick Leon up. This small gesture spared Johan's precious (training) relaxation time.

While being driven to the academy, Leon called his mentor, Mustard.

"Yo, Lil' Leon!" Mustard greeted, expertly maneuvering his game controller on the Isle of Armor.

"Master, I met Johan-sensei today," Leon said earnestly.

"Oh? And how'd it go, hmmm?" Mustard chuckled knowingly, fully aware that Leon wouldn't pass up the chance to challenge Johan in a battle.

"I lost," Leon admitted with a sigh. "The gap was enormous."

Even though Johan claimed the "Eevee" wasn't his Pokémon, Leon couldn't entirely believe it.

"Hmm?"

Mustard paused his game, his interest piqued.

Leon wasn't one to exaggerate or back down. For him to openly acknowledge a significant gap in strength...

"Johan's that strong?" Mustard murmured, tugging on the brim of his hat as he rose to his feet. His tone shifted from curiosity to excitement.

"Yeah," Leon replied, his admiration clear. "Not just strength—his bond with his Pokémon runs incredibly deep. Johan-sensei respects their individual wills and focuses on raising a few exceptionally well-trained Pokémon. He only has four in total."

"Four?"

While it wasn't unheard of for trainers to focus on a select few Pokémon—quality over quantity—reaching Johan's level with such a small team was rare.

"By the way, Master, Johan-sensei agreed to your invitation," Leon added.

Hearing this, Mustard chuckled.

"Good, good. Tell Johan to come by soon, will ya? Ol' Mustard's getting bored here!"

Back at the café, Johan sneezed, rubbing his nose.

"Catching a cold from the sea breeze? That's odd."

With his cold resistance, he doubted even sleeping in the snow would faze him.

"Meow~~"

Mew happily played with the music box Johan had given it, a ceramic trinket adorned with a spinning Vulpix figurine that turned as it chimed.

It loved the little gift, and Johan had also bought three more trinkets from the beachside stalls just to keep Mew entertained.

Afterward, Johan took his Pokémon to the supermarket to stock up on snacks and dinner ingredients. It didn't take long for him to realize just how Mew had put on weight.

The moment they got home, Mew plopped onto the couch, cracked open a soda bottle larger than itself, and started guzzling it down.

Johan watched as its chubby belly swelled like a balloon, only for it to deflate moments later after Mew let out a contented burp.

"Hic—meow~~"

Johan pinched the bridge of his nose.

Great. He'd officially turned Mew into a couch potato.

While it was undeniably cute, the thought of Mew running into Mewtwo in this state...

Yeah. That'd require some damage control.

He also took precautions to ensure that Vulpix and the others didn't pick up Mew's habits. After all, if Vulpix got chubby enough to resemble a Glameow...

Dinner that night was a grand feast. Mew ate until it was practically rolling across the floor, thoroughly enjoying every bite.

Afterward, Johan decided to take the group for an evening stroll along the riverside to walk off the meal.

The moment they arrived, the Pokémon were captivated.

The gentle murmur of the river blended with the soft glow of fireflies flitting about, creating a scene straight out of a storybook.

"Meow~"

Mew was familiar with fireflies, having seen them often around the World Tree, but the others were mesmerized.

"Ka-kah~~"

Gengar, however, had other ideas. It gleefully flew toward the fireflies, snapping them up one by one like candy.

"Chu?"

"Draa?"

Tinkatuff and Dragonair tilted their heads in confusion, watching Gengar with curiosity.

Satisfied with its haul, Gengar put on its sunglasses and struck a pose, its body now glowing faintly from the consumed fireflies.

The sight sent the others into peals of laughter, including Mew, who rolled on the ground, clutching its sides.

"Meowmeow!"

Johan, though, couldn't resist bursting Gengar's bubble.

"Ahem. Gengar, there's an old saying where I'm from: 'Fireflies thrive on decay.'"

Gengar froze mid-pose, its eyes wide in horror.

"Ka-kah?! Σ(дlll)"

With a dramatic wail, it spat out every last firefly it'd eaten, sending the rest of the group into hysterics.

The next morning, Johan woke to find Mew gone.

He wasn't surprised. As much as Mew enjoyed visiting, its bond with the World Tree meant it couldn't stay away for too long.

He could only hope he'd made Mew's short visit enjoyable.

Later, Johan received a call from the headmaster.

"Johan, I heard you accepted Mustard's invitation?"

"Uh, yeah. Sort of," Johan replied.

"Great news!" The headmaster's laughter boomed over the line. "We've already booked you a first-class ticket to Galar."

Johan blinked.

First-class? Already booked?

"Don't worry about the costs," the headmaster added. "The academy encourages its staff to pursue opportunities like this. It's all in the name of improving our teaching quality!"

Hanging up, Johan couldn't help but marvel.

Publicly funded travel...

Maybe he could even snag a Dynamax Band while he was there and charge it to the academy.

After a long flight, the plane finally began its descent into Galar's Wyndon City.

The sprawling metropolis, filled with towering skyscrapers and bustling streets, rivaled Hoenn's Rustboro City in grandeur.

As Johan exited the airport, a Jeep screeched around a corner, drifting sharply before heading straight for him.

# 224: Sonia and Wings of Dawn

Crash!

A shaky, pink Jeep finally skidded to a stop, colliding with the roadside guardrail.

Amid the gasps and murmurs of nearby onlookers, a young woman with curled orange ponytails, stylish glasses perched on her forehead, and a chic brown coat staggered out of the car. Her makeup was impeccable, but her movements betrayed a hint of clumsiness.

"Jo...Johan-sensei!" she called out, heading straight for him.

"Sonia?" Johan blinked, slightly surprised.

The girl stopped, pointing to herself as her green eyes widened. "Wait, Johan-sensei knows me?"

"Leon showed me your picture," Johan replied casually.

At that, Sonia's pale cheeks turned a delicate shade of pink. "I-I see..."

Leon had her picture on his phone?

"What brings you here?" Johan asked, steering the conversation away from her embarrassment.

Snapping back to reality, Sonia explained, "Leon said this was your first visit to Galar and that you might not be familiar with the region. So he mentioned it to my grandmother, and she sent me to greet you."

"Oh, my grandmother is Professor Magnolia."

Why go through Magnolia instead of a direct call?

Clearing her throat, Sonia continued, "Please, Johan-sensei, hop in!"

Hop in?

Johan glanced at the battered Jeep, which looked ready to fall apart at any moment. Memories of Sonia's infamous driving skills resurfaced.

In Galar, two names were infamous for their travel mishaps.

The first was Leon, who couldn't find his way out of a Pokémon Center.

The second was Sonia, the "road menace" who was blissfully unaware of her lackluster driving.

These two childhood friends—partners-in-chaos—were a perfect match.

Johan shook his head, contemplating a fitting duo nickname for them.

"Maybe I should drive?" Johan suggested tentatively.

"Huh?" Sonia tilted her head.

Johan rubbed his chin thoughtfully. "I mean, as a first-time visitor, driving myself might help me get a better feel for Galar's culture."

A reasonable excuse.

Sonia hesitated but eventually handed over the keys. "Alright."

She stepped aside, carrying a Yamper in her arms as she climbed into the passenger seat.

Yamper was one of Sonia's key partners, an adorable Electric-type with boundless energy.

Relieved, Johan cradled his Vulpix as he slid into the driver's seat.

"Super cute!" Sonia gushed, spotting the icy Vulpix.

Her cartoonish enthusiasm lit up her emerald eyes like stars as she reached over to gently tug Vulpix's fluffy ears.

"Au...Oowu?"

The little Vulpix turned to Johan for help, but its soft, pleading gaze only charmed Sonia further.

Even Yamper wasn't immune. It hopped down from Sonia's lap and darted toward Vulpix, tail wagging furiously as it panted with excitement.

"Au-Oowu~"

The Vulpix shyly greeted its admirers.

With that, Yamper was utterly smitten.

Sparks of electricity crackled around its wagging tail as it eagerly tried to strike up a conversation.

"Yamper?" (Hungry?)

"Oowu~~" (Not really.)

Before the conversation could continue, Gengar suddenly emerged from the shadows in the back seat. Its long, crimson tongue lolled out menacingly as it loomed over Yamper.

"Ka-kah!" (I'm thirsty!)

Startled, Yamper froze, eyes wide as its legs stiffened. With a dramatic thud, it collapsed in fright, limbs twitching.

"Yamper!" Sonia cried, rushing to her Pokémon's side.

Johan pinched the bridge of his nose as Yamper lay limp, drooling foam.

Gengar scratched its head in confusion.

"Too scary?"

Thankfully, Vulpix blew a soft puff of icy air at Yamper, bringing it back to its senses. Johan wasted no time apologizing profusely to Sonia and her companion.

"You're quite the skilled driver, Johan-sensei," Sonia remarked as they set off.

That wasn't true—Johan's driving was cautious and slow, the kind one might expect from a grandparent.

It only highlighted just how terrifying Sonia's driving must be.

"Where to?" Johan asked.

"Um, well, let me check..." Sonia opened the glove compartment and pulled out a map, which she studied intently.

Johan glanced over, immediately spotting the issue.

"You're holding it upside down."

"Huh? Oh!"

No wonder Leon and Sonia were childhood friends.

"Here, let me handle it." Johan took out his Pokédex and activated its GPS.

"A Pokédex?" Sonia raised an eyebrow.

"Yeah, a gift from Professor Oak," Johan explained.

Although he rarely used it, its navigation features proved handy.

Their ultimate destination was the Isle of Armor, but Johan decided to make a brief stop at Wyndon's largest shopping mall.

"You can't visit empty-handed," Johan reasoned.

"Full Restores, Antidotes, Paralyze Heals, Max Potions... Gengar, don't grab too many—50 of each should do."

"What? They don't have enough stock? Fine, just give me what's available."

With their shopping complete, Sonia led Johan to Galar's Flying Taxi headquarters.

The Flying Taxi service, operated primarily by Corviknight, could take trainers almost anywhere in Galar.

Through Sonia's connections, they secured a seasoned pilot—a man bundled in a thick coat with goggles and a woolen hat.

"Is this the young man I'm picking up?" the pilot asked, smiling warmly.

He was one of the few people who knew the Isle of Armor's location—a trusted friend of Mustard's.

"Yes, please take care of him," Sonia replied, bowing politely.

"No problem."

The pilot excused himself to check on his Corviknight.

"Johan-sensei," Sonia suddenly said, her voice hesitant.

Johan turned to her, curious.

After a moment's pause, she blurted out, "I want to study in Hoenn!"

"Hoenn?" Johan blinked.

Sonia nodded. "Kabu told me so much about it—I'm really curious!"

Kabu, Galar's Motostoke Gym Leader, hailed from Hoenn.

"Plus... I want to study under you, Johan-sensei!"

Johan was taken aback.

Sonia, determined to grow stronger, wanted a fresh start. Hoenn seemed like the perfect place.

"Your grandmother doesn't mind?" Johan asked.

"She's fully supportive!" Sonia exclaimed.

"Well, then, you're always welcome in Hoenn," Johan assured her.

Recruiting Sonia to Rustboro Academy was a win. Martin owed him lunch for this.

Just then, a massive Corviknight descended from the sky, clutching an old-fashioned taxi in its talons. The pilot waved cheerfully.

"All set! Ready for liftoff!"

But Johan's smile froze.

The Luxury Ball at his waist trembled.

In a flash of red light, Tinkatuff appeared, hammer in hand, its eyes locked on Corviknight with a fiery intensity.

Picking up a rock, it tossed it into the air and swung its hammer with unrelenting precision.

A mighty clang echoed through the sky as Corviknight spread its wings wide.

The battle for the heavens had begun.

# 225: A Sigh of Relief, and Then... Despair

Mountains, rivers, towns, and plains swept past the horizon, blurring into one mesmerizing panorama.

"Calm down, or we're going to fall out of the sky," Johan said, gripping Tinkatuff's arm tightly.

"Chu~ Chu~~"

Tinkatuff had finally settled down, its earlier frenzy subsiding.

Despite being on a flying taxi, its competitive spirit was unshaken.

It had discovered something infuriating: this Corviknight, carrying the flying taxi, was fast. Too fast. The stones Tinkatuff had flung had missed completely.

Defeated, it resigned itself to sitting still, hammer in hand.

"Chu..."

It muttered under its breath, gripping its hammer tighter.

It needed to become stronger.

Meanwhile, the Corviknight hauling the taxi seemed to heave a sigh of relief.

Close call.

Now it understood why its cousins in the Paldea region often looked so miserable.

Tinkatuff's small, adorable, pink appearance was deceptive; the rocks hurled by its hammer were no joke.

Corviknight shuddered.

If one of those stones had hit its wing, it might've shed feathers. And if it had struck its head... well, the thought was too grim to entertain.

The driver, however, remained impressively calm, betraying no hint of alarm.

He seemed to be well-versed in the "complicated relationship" between Tinkatuff and Pokémon like Corviknight.

In their earlier stages, Tinkatink often became targets for metal-loving Pokémon like Corviknight and Bisharp, drawn to their crude, metallic hammers.

But as Tinkatink evolved into Tinkatuff and sought stronger materials to craft larger hammers, they'd turn the tables—attacking Bisharp clans to scavenge metal.

By the time they became Tinkaton, they often took aim at flying Corviknight for the same reason.

This rivalry was so infamous that in Paldea, the Flying Taxi service exclusively used smaller, faster Pokémon like Squawkabilly instead of Corviknight.

It wasn't hard to imagine why.

Luckily, Johan's Tinkatuff was strong—too strong.

If this Corviknight hadn't been skilled, it might have ended up grounded and plucked bald.

After what felt like an eternity, the driver called out, "We're almost there!"

Ahead, a sprawling island came into view.

The Isle of Armor.

The island's size was impressive, featuring forests, wetlands, dunes, caves, and a variety of wild Pokémon habitats.

Without adequate strength, wandering freely here could be perilous.

"Ka-kah!"

Gengar, perched against the window, pointed to the island.

Two towering structures loomed in the distance, one to the east and the other to the west.

Approaching from the west, the western tower was more visible. From afar, the sound of rushing water could be faintly heard.

The eastern tower, shrouded in black mist, was less distinct.

The driver chuckled, noticing Johan's curiosity.

"Those are the Towers of Two Fists. The western one is the Tower of Waters, and the eastern one is the Tower of Darkness. Each teaches a different martial art."

"The Tower of Waters practices the 'Rapid-Strike Style,' focusing on seamless, continuous attacks like flowing water or crashing waves, with each strike more powerful than the last."

"The Tower of Darkness emphasizes the 'Single-Strike Style,' favoring devastating, one-hit blows that end battles in an instant."

"Chu..."

Tinkatuff's eyes gleamed as it pointed toward the Tower of Darkness.

Johan gave a small nod of understanding.

The renowned Elite Four member Bruno had once suggested that Tinkatuff might benefit from studying the Single-Strike Style.

Johan, too, was familiar with the towers.

Each corresponded to a specific evolution for Kubfu—either the Rapid-Strike Urshifu from the Tower of Waters or the Single-Strike Urshifu from the Tower of Darkness.

Urshifu was one of Mustard's signature Pokémon.

Of course, Leon, Mustard's protégé, hadn't inherited his master's martial expertise.

Rumor had it Leon once got lost trying to find the towers with his Kubfu, derailing the entire training process.

Classic Leon.

"Ka-kah~"

Gengar suddenly produced its phone, swiping to a picture of a younger Mustard.

In the photo, Mustard stood proudly with two imposing Urshifu—one on either side.

"Oowu?"

The other Pokémon crowded around, expressions serious.

"Chu."

Tinkatuff muttered, seemingly impressed by the strength the Urshifu radiated.

"They're just bear paws," Johan joked.

The Pokémon turned to him with expressions that could only be described as judgmental.

Really? You're making food jokes right now?

"We're landing soon!" the driver announced as Corviknight descended toward a dojo-like building below.

The Isle of Armor—Mustard's Dojo.

Under a clear blue sky, Corviknight flapped its wings, landing smoothly on the sandy shore.

Ahead lay dense forests and rolling hills, while behind them stretched the open ocean, where a Drednaw emerged from the water with a menacing growl.

After the driver passed along his contact number, he quickly departed, clearly eager to leave the island as soon as possible.

"Chu."

Tinkatuff watched the retreating Corviknight with mild annoyance, grumbling about missed opportunities to "test" its hammer.

Johan surveyed the surroundings thoughtfully.

Not bad.

An entire island as a personal training ground.

No wonder Mustard, with his undefeated 18-year championship streak, was so accomplished.

Maybe one day, Johan mused, he'd buy his own island. A peaceful place to raise his Pokémon, complete with a cozy little house—and maybe even a partner to share it with.

The creak of the dojo door interrupted his daydream.

A woman in a bright green sweater, her makeup flawless, emerged, broom in hand.

"Are you Johan-sensei?" she asked with a warm smile.

Honey.

Mustard's younger wife and the dojo's co-founder, Honey was as sharp as she was charming. She managed the dojo alongside Mustard, balancing practicality with a touch of flair.

"Perfect weather for laundry, isn't it?" she remarked, shielding her eyes from the sun.

Johan returned the smile.

"You must be Honey. I see Mustard is a lucky man."

"Flatterer." Honey giggled, visibly pleased. "You're much smoother than Leon."

"Mustard's inside—probably gaming again. I'll take you to him."

"Thanks for the trouble."

As they entered the dojo, passing through a serene garden, they reached a central hall.

Inside, an older man with flowing white brows and a lively green jacket sat on a sofa, engrossed in a video game.

"Sorry about this," Honey said apologetically. "Mustard loses himself in games sometimes. But don't worry—it looks like he's about to lose anyway."

Sure enough, the words "GAME OVER" flashed across the screen moments later.

"Welcome, welcome!" Mustard said cheerfully, finally noticing his guests.

"Little Johan?"

Johan froze.

Little Johan?

He fought the urge to wince at the nickname.

"Yes, Mustard-sensei."

"Haha! You made good time."

Not really. His principal had practically launched him onto a plane overnight.

"Come on—I'll show you some Pokémon," Mustard said suddenly, clapping his hands.

"Pokémon?" Johan repeated, confused.

This wasn't part of the plan.

"Yup. I heard you only have four so far, right? You might find one of mine to your liking."

"Wait, I'm just here to train my Tinkatuff!"

But Mustard didn't seem to hear, leading Johan toward the back of the dojo.

"You'll need to pass a test to earn one, though," Mustard added with a grin.

A test?

Johan relaxed slightly.

Good. If he failed, he wouldn't have to take one home.

When they reached an open courtyard, Mustard turned, releasing two Pokémon onto the field.

A tiny Rookidee and a Rockruff appeared, both looking timid and inexperienced.

Johan's jaw dropped.

Noticing the eagerness in his Pokémon, he muttered in disbelief:

"Wait, you're holding back for me?"

What kind of Pokémon were so valuable that Mustard—the Mustard—would go this far to ensure he could catch one?

Suddenly, Johan's hopes of failure didn't seem so secure.

A breeze rustled through the grass, revealing a tuft of gray fur nestled among the foliage.

(－ω－) zzZ

# 226: Halt! This Tree Is Mine!

"Little Johan, don't underestimate them," Mustard remarked with a knowing smile, as though he'd already seen through Johan's thoughts.

Johan snapped back to attention.

Right.

No matter how they looked, these were still Pokémon raised by Mustard, the former Champion of Galar and a martial arts legend!

"Then..."

Before Johan could finish, Honey, Mustard's ever-charming wife, interjected with a smile:

"How about a no-holds-barred match? Both sides use two Pokémon each. What do you think?"

"Sounds perfect to me," Mustard agreed, sliding his hands into his pockets with an air of calm confidence.

"It's been a while since I've broken a sweat. Good time to get these old bones moving."

Johan hesitated briefly, his expression mixed.

No-holds-barred?

Unlike unrestricted battles, which only allowed Pokémon to clash and protected trainers from direct harm, no-holds-barred matches were different.

In this format, trainers and Pokémon could both engage in combat—trainers could even directly target one another!

Victory could be achieved by incapacitating both of the opponent's Pokémon or by rendering the opposing trainer unable to continue.

Such matches weren't common in Galar and were almost unheard of in official circuits.

But Mustard had traveled the world. It was no surprise that he'd encountered and embraced this battle style.

Not only was he the former Champion, but he'd also been a Gym Leader specializing in Fighting types and was the master who trained Bea, the human "Machamp" herself!

So this was the catch.

Fighting him... wouldn't get Johan killed, right?

Still, perhaps this battle was meant to evaluate his Pokémon's abilities and his own capabilities as a trainer.

With newfound resolve, Johan's eyes sharpened.

"Oh-ho! Look at that fighting spirit, Little Johan!" Mustard praised, his demeanor growing more focused.

Johan shouted his first command:

"Tinkatuff, use Ice Hammer!"

Tinkatuff, who had been quietly observing from Johan's side, immediately tightened its grip on its hammer. Without hesitation, it swung the frosted weapon in a powerful arc, sending shards of ice scattering toward Mustard.

Mustard, however, remained unfazed.

"Impressive force. This kind of raw power... has incredible potential if properly refined."

Even as he analyzed, his commands came swiftly and decisively:

"Rookidee, Brave Bird!"

Brave Bird?

Johan's eyes widened in disbelief.

Rookidee was Galar's humble early-game "starter bird." Who would bother teaching it something as demanding as Brave Bird?

But sure enough, the small avian's body ignited with pale blue flames as it flapped its wings and launched itself directly toward Tinkatuff.

"And Rockruff, Stone Edge!"

Rockruff's claws dug into the ground, summoning sharp stone pillars in a straight line—toward Johan.

Johan wasn't even surprised anymore.

If Rookidee could pull off Brave Bird, then why not a Stone Edge-wielding Rockruff?

"Gengar!" Johan commanded. "Shadow Ball!"

"Ka-kah! (Let's go!)"

From Johan's shadow, Gengar emerged with its signature mischievous grin.

This was where Gengar truly shined—its utility and unpredictability made it invaluable in wild fights and no-holds-barred modes.

With eerie precision, Gengar conjured a dark, crackling orb and hurled it directly at the incoming pillars of rock.

But then, something caught Johan's attention.

"This... this is..."

He glanced around at the field, his mind racing.

Rookidee's relentless assault, Rockruff's pinpoint strikes—this wasn't random chaos.

"Single-Strike and Rapid-Strike Styles," Johan muttered to himself.

Mustard, impressed, gave a small nod.

"Figured it out already, eh?"

The ice hammer collided with Rookidee mid-dive, sending the bird flying.

Even Tinkatuff paused briefly, its hammer still held aloft.

For such a small Pokémon, Rookidee had unleashed a surprising amount of force.

Before Tinkatuff could resume its attack, Rockruff pivoted, flinging pawfuls of sand directly into its face.

"Gengar, Hypnosis!" Johan redirected his focus.

Gengar flashed toward Mustard, its spectral hands glowing hypnotically.

However, Mustard simply shut his eyes and rolled forward, dodging the hypnotic waves effortlessly.

Gengar pouted.

If Johan hadn't forbidden its use of the "Johan Family Hypnosis Special" (a deliberately overpowered version of the move), Mustard wouldn't have stood a chance.

Meanwhile, Rookidee, despite being thrown back, launched itself from above again, wings blazing with Brave Bird.

"Gengar, Shadow Ball again! Tinkatuff, focus on the Stone Edge!" Johan barked his next set of orders.

Gengar's eerie laughter echoed as it hurled twin shadowy orbs—one at Mustard and the other at Rockruff.

Tinkatuff's hammer frosted over once more as it leapt into the air, smashing through the sharp rock spires Rockruff had conjured.

"Nice!" Johan grinned as the battlefield shifted in his favor.

But Mustard wasn't done yet.

Dodging Gengar's first Shadow Ball with an acrobatic twist, he clapped his hands together.

"Rockruff, Sand Attack, and Rookidee, one last Brave Bird!"

Before Rockruff could follow through, Tinkatuff smashed its hammer down, freezing the field and cutting off any chance of another attack.

Meanwhile, Gengar's second Shadow Ball hit home, knocking both Rookidee and Rockruff out of the fight.

It was over.

Or so Johan thought.

"Gotcha!" Mustard lunged forward, grabbing the haft of Tinkatuff's hammer mid-swing.

What followed could only be described as slapstick.

With an audible "whoosh," the small but deceptively strong Tinkatuff yanked Mustard off the ground, sending him tumbling into a nearby bush.

"Ka-kah! (That's what you get!)" Gengar teased as the dust settled.

"...This hammer... is heavy," Mustard groaned, lying flat on his back.

Victory belonged to Johan!

Honey approached the scene with an amused expression.

"Phew, what a fight!" She grabbed the hammer effortlessly, spinning it once before handing it back to a stunned Tinkatuff.

Johan blinked.

"Wait, you can lift that?"

Honey just smiled.

"There's a reason I keep Mustard in line, you know."

"Chu~"

Tinkatuff accepted the hammer gratefully, already thinking about how to make it even heavier.

"Great battle!" Mustard declared, dusting himself off. "You've passed the test!"

Johan was less enthused.

This "test" had been rigged from the start.

He hadn't wanted to win, but Mustard's relentless attacks had forced his hand.

Still, Johan tried to protest:

"Mustard-sensei, I don't just capture Pokémon on a whim. It has to feel... right."

"Oh, don't worry about that," Mustard reassured him. "Just wait until you meet the little fella I have in mind."

As Mustard led the group into a dense forest, Johan's curiosity grew.

"There," Mustard said, pointing to a small clearing.

Lying in the grass, a Pokémon snoozed peacefully.

Even after all the commotion, it hadn't stirred.

Johan scanned it with his Pokédex.

Beep!

"Kubfu. The Wushu Pokémon. Known for its determination and courage, it trains diligently to become stronger."

A Kubfu?

The little bear rubbed its eyes groggily, noticing Johan and his Pokémon for the first time.

It immediately perked up, tightening the band-like fur around its head and assuming a fighting stance.

Its gaze was intense, brimming with resolve.

"Ku!"

Halt! This tree is mine!

Johan blinked, turning to Mustard.

"What... did it just say?"

Mustard smiled slyly.

"It said, 'Don't damage the trees.'"

# 227: You Weren’t Like This a Moment Ago!

The gray-white fur, thick eyebrows like a martial artist's, intense eyes, and a perfect combat stance made Kubfu look like it meant business.

As Mustard observed the interaction, he grinned.

"It seems like it's challenging you to a fight, Little Johan."

His voice carried a hint of nostalgia.

This particular Kubfu was not an easy one to get along with.

Johan stroked his chin thoughtfully.

Kubfu left a favorable impression. It wasn't surprising that Mustard would offer such a Pokémon—it was a testament to his trust. Mustard wasn't the type to gift Kubfu to just anyone.

Only a select few who had earned his recognition ever received one of these rare Pokémon.

Even in Pokémon Journeys, two formidable trainers, Chase and Asahi, hailed from the Isle of Armor. Both wielded the evolved forms of Kubfu—Single Strike Style and Rapid Strike Style Urshifu.

Still, Johan couldn't ignore the "oddities" surrounding Mustard's antics earlier.

Could Kubfu have some hidden quirks?

Perhaps it had an enormous appetite, or maybe it was difficult to handle in other ways.

"Well," Johan mused, "there's only one way to find out."

"Tinkatuff," he called out.

"Chu~"

Tinkatuff stepped forward with a confident smile, hefting its massive hammer. It extended a hand toward Kubfu in a taunting gesture.

Bring it on, little bear.

Kubfu didn't flinch. Instead, it glanced at Tinkatuff's hammer and took a deep breath. Its entire body seemed to relax as it prepared to fight.

It had waited a long time for a worthy challenge.

This time, it was determined to observe the fight carefully and grow from the experience.

Suddenly, Kubfu charged forward with incredible speed, the wind roaring in its wake. Fallen leaves scattered in its path.

The sheer intensity of its presence surprised even Johan.

Kubfu approached with short, calculated strides before launching a precise punch.

"Rock Smash!" Mustard declared from the sidelines, his tone light yet brimming with pride.

"Such a spirited move!"

Johan smirked.

"Tinkatuff, Ice Hammer!"

Tinkatuff swung its hammer with frost gathering at the edges, meeting Kubfu's punch head-on.

Boom!

The collision sent shockwaves rippling outward.

Johan squinted, sensing something unusual.

"Was that... Aura?"

He could feel a distinct wave of energy emanating from Kubfu during the clash.

While Kubfu itself couldn't learn Aura Sphere, its evolved form, Urshifu, certainly could. Perhaps this was a sign of its potential?

The impact sent Kubfu flying backward, crashing into a nearby tree.

"Chu!"

Tinkatuff didn't pursue. Instead, it planted its hammer into the ground and nodded at Kubfu with approval.

Not bad, little one.

Kubfu struggled to its feet, its sharp eyes unwavering. It seemed to whisper in its Aura:

Even kings must fight to prove their worth.

Johan raised an eyebrow.

Where had Kubfu learned that line?

The battle paused briefly as Johan's Pokédex beeped, revealing Kubfu's details:

Kubfu

The Wushu Pokémon.

A serious Pokémon with a strong sense of justice. It diligently trains to strengthen its body and spirit.

Type: Fighting

Ability: Inner Focus

Known Moves: Leer, Rock Smash, Endure, Focus Energy, Aerial Ace, Scary Face, Headbutt, Brick Break, Detect, Coaching.

Mood: Determined (Even kings must prove their mettle!)

Status: Excellent (Though it seems a bit sleepy.)

Friendship Level: 10 (It likes your Tinkatuff's hammer.)

Johan chuckled.

Kubfu's skills weren't overly flashy—no Swords Dance or Close Combat yet. Its raw strength was impressive for its stage, but its personality was what stood out.

"Let's test something," Johan murmured, releasing a wave of his own Aura toward Kubfu.

Kubfu froze mid-step, visibly startled.

"That should do it," Johan thought.

He approached slowly, holding out a cube of Pokémon food.

Kubfu tilted its head, eyes darting between Johan and the food.

"Ku?"

For me?

"Yes, for you. Go on," Johan said, smiling.

After a moment of hesitation, Kubfu accepted the treat and devoured it in one bite.

"Hic!" Kubfu hiccupped, then let out a long yawn.

Johan's eyes lit up.

One treat was enough to fill it up? This was a trainer's dream!

Satisfied, Johan turned to Mustard.

"Sensei, it seems this little one and I are a good match."

Mustard grinned.

"Then don't wait—catch it, Little Johan!"

Without hesitation, Johan knelt and extended a Luxury Ball.

"Will you join me as my partner?"

Kubfu stared into Johan's eyes before nodding firmly.

The Luxury Ball clicked shut without resistance.

Inside, Kubfu curled up and promptly fell asleep.

As Johan held the sealed Luxury Ball, Mustard let out a dramatic sigh of relief.

"Sensei?" Johan asked.

Mustard beamed.

"Just excited for you, Little Johan! Let's head back for lunch—Honey's preparing a feast!"

Johan followed, puzzled by Mustard's enthusiasm. Something felt... off.

Before he could dwell on it further, a deep rumble shook the ground.

Boom!

A crimson light flared in the distance.

"Sensei, what is that?"

"Hmm," Mustard mused casually, as though the phenomenon was a minor inconvenience.

"Some Pokémon around here have been acting up lately. Probably nothing to worry about."

Nothing to worry about?! Johan thought, incredulous.

But before he could question further, Mustard turned and pointed.

"Bring Kubfu out, Little Johan. Let it stretch its legs."

Reluctantly, Johan released Kubfu from its ball.

"Ku~~ (Zzz...)"

Still asleep, Kubfu remained standing, swaying slightly.

But when a massive Drednaw emerged from the swamp, eyes glowing red with rage, Kubfu snapped to attention.

Its posture shifted fluidly into a fighting stance, its eyes focused despite the sleepiness lingering in its Aura.

"Sensei, are you sure—"

Mustard interrupted with a grin.

"Time to command, Little Johan."

Johan hesitated but gave a simple order:

"Kubfu, Brick Break!"

The moment he spoke, Kubfu burst into motion, its movements a blur.

The enraged Drednaw lunged with its massive jaws, but Kubfu sidestepped with effortless grace.

It pivoted mid-air, its paw glowing with fiery red energy, and delivered a devastating blow to Drednaw's shell.

Boom!

Drednaw collapsed with a pained roar, its tough armor cracking under Kubfu's strike.

Johan gawked.

"You weren't like this a moment ago!"

Kubfu exhaled deeply, its Aura settling.

"Ku~ (Zzz...)"

It returned to its upright, sleeping posture as though nothing had happened.

Mustard clapped Johan on the back.

"Impressive, isn't it? Looks like your Kubfu's got a knack for channeling Aura in battle."

Johan sighed, rubbing his temples.

"Sensei, you could've warned me about this."

Mustard only laughed.

"Good luck raising that one, Little Johan!"

# 228: "The Outlaw Squad" Welcomes a New Member! (BONUS)

The Drednaw wasn't completely defeated but had been jolted back to its senses by the intense pain. It shot a wary glance at the still-sleeping Kubfu before slinking toward the swamp, clearly intimidated.

But then Kubfu moved again.

It grabbed onto the Drednaw's tail, preventing its retreat.

Drednaw froze, turned its head, and stared nervously at Kubfu.

"Ku~~ (－ω－) zzZ"

Kubfu held out its paw toward the Drednaw.

Understanding the gesture, Drednaw rummaged around its body, eventually producing a Berry and handing it to Kubfu.

Once it had the Berry, Kubfu finally released the tail, and Drednaw fled into the swamp like its life depended on it.

Johan: "..."

This level of coordination—so practiced and seamless—what on earth is this?

Watching the scene unfold, Johan broke into a cold sweat.

Was that righteous expression from earlier all an act?

A soft pop sounded as the bubble of snot on Kubfu's nose burst. It yawned lightly and slowly woke up.

"Ku?"

Kubfu stared at the Berry in its hand, scratching its head in confusion.

Then its gaze sharpened, and a profound, solemn expression appeared on its face as if the weight of a great destiny had just been placed upon its shoulders.

Kubfu clasped its paws together and bowed toward the vast forest.

"Ku~~"

Thank you, Mother Nature, for this gift. I am the chosen one, destined to fulfill my great calling!

The surroundings grew quiet again, leaving only the sound of Kubfu munching on the Berry.

Johan's eye twitched.

So eating that one energy cube earlier satisfied it because... it wasn't actually hungry?!

He tilted his head back at a 45-degree angle and let out a long sigh.

Turning to look at Master Mustard, Johan found him cheerfully speaking into a phone with an overly joyful expression.

"Ah, yes, yes, it's already been taken in by Johan. Honey, let's prepare a couple more dishes for lunch to celebrate~"

The unrestrained glee radiating from Mustard made Johan's forehead vein twitch.

If Mustard weren't a martial arts legend, Johan might've thrown hands.

At that moment, Kubfu walked over and handed the Berry it had been nibbling to Johan.

"Ku~~"

It smiled innocently at him.

Want some?

It's really tasty.

Johan's tense expression softened.

At least Kubfu's aura and his own wave frequencies were compatible—that much was clear.

He crouched down slightly, rubbing the little bear's head.

To his surprise, its fur was incredibly soft and fluffy—almost as delightful to touch as Vulpix's coat.

"I'm not hungry," he said, initially refusing the Berry.

But noticing the look in Kubfu's eyes, he relented, took the Berry, and took a bite.

"It's pretty good."

"Ku~~"

Satisfied, Kubfu finished the rest of the Berry with a crunch crunch before letting out another yawn, its eyes tearing up slightly from sleepiness.

"Go ahead and rest for now," Johan said as he returned Kubfu to its Luxury Ball.

Honestly...

As long as Kubfu wasn't asleep, things still seemed manageable.

"Looks like it really likes you, Johan," Mustard said with a knowing smile after ending his call.

"Yeah," Johan replied, though his voice was laced with resignation. "Thanks for that, Master Mustard."

Waving it off casually, Mustard continued walking back to the dojo.

"But, Johan, no kidding—this Kubfu is highly talented. A lot of people tried to catch it before you, but none succeeded."

"Really?"

With how it dozed off at every turn...

Oh, wait—right. A sleeping Kubfu would be far harder to catch than an awake one.

"By the way, Johan, you can use Aura, right?" Mustard suddenly asked.

"A little," Johan admitted openly.

To a martial artist like Mustard, Aura wasn't a foreign concept at all. Many Fighting-type Pokémon, such as his Urshifu or Mienshao, were capable of using moves like Aura Sphere.

"That explains it," Mustard nodded knowingly.

"This little guy's Aura talent is exceptional too."

Johan had already noticed that himself.

"It's not just its Aura talent. Did you catch on to anything else?"

After a moment's thought, Johan hesitated before saying:

"Single-Strike Style and Rapid-Strike Style?"

Mustard's long eyebrows twitched with amusement. "I knew you'd pick up on that!"

"It picked them up itself from visiting the Towers of Waters and Darkness," he added.

"By itself?"

Normally, a Kubfu would evolve into Urshifu after visiting one of the two towers.

But Johan quickly connected the dots.

"It went there... while asleep?"

"Bingo," Mustard chuckled.

"It doesn't even remember what happened while it was there."

Training while sleeping?

Johan suddenly noticed the Luxury Ball in his hand shaking slightly.

Through the transparent window, he could see Kubfu... doing push-ups inside!

What?! So diligent?!

"But because it was half-asleep, its heart remained pure—focused solely on martial arts," Mustard explained with a smile.

...Is that even a thing?

Returning to the dojo, Mustard excused himself to check on Honey and left Johan to train with the other Pokémon.

Throwing out Kubfu's Luxury Ball, Johan watched as it appeared, promptly dropping to the ground and doing push-ups in its sleep.

"Everyone, come out and meet your new teammate," Johan announced.

Gengar, Vulpix, Tinkatuff, and Dragonair materialized in turn.

Tinkatuff and Gengar were already familiar with Kubfu and respected its combat prowess.

Vulpix and Dragonair, however, were curious.

"Draa?" (Is it asleep or awake?)

"Oowu~~" (Can it even hear us?)

"Ku! zzZ"

Kubfu suddenly stood up, nodded toward Vulpix, and went back to its push-ups.

"Oowu?"

Vulpix tilted its head in confusion, a question mark practically floating above it.

"Ka-kah~~" (Let's just wake it up already.)

Gengar laughed mischievously, looking at Dragonair.

Slightly hesitant but ultimately agreeing, Dragonair's antennae sparked with golden energy, delivering a jolt to Kubfu.

"Ku! Ku!"

Kubfu's entire body jolted as its fur stood on end, the snot bubble finally bursting.

"Ku~~" (That tickles!)

"Oowu~~" (Nice one, Dragonair.)

Johan was mildly impressed. "Great job, Dragonair. You're our official alarm clock now."

As everyone introduced themselves, Kubfu extended its paw.

"Ku! ╰(●''●)╮"

Let's team up and conquer the world together!

Gengar, being the group's self-appointed leader, shook its paw firmly.

"Ka-kah!" (Welcome aboard.)

Observing this scene, Johan rubbed his temples and sighed.

"Why does this feel like a criminal gang is forming under my nose?"

The Outlaw Squad now had a new member.

Leader: Gengar, master infiltrator.

Tinkatuff and Kubfu: one a cunning thief, the other a bold enforcer.

Vulpix and Dragonair: specialists in underground and aerial operations.

A group this versatile was destined to cause havoc.

Johan let out a long sigh.

"Well... at least they're getting along."

If it ever came down to it, he'd have to rely on his so-called "path of righteousness" to rein them in.

After all, they say Pokémon eventually reflect their Trainer's personality.

Someday... they'll all become as upright as I am.

# 229: For the Greater Good, Kindly Sacrifice Yourself! (BONUS)

"Hello, Johan? Why the sudden call?"

On the other end of the phone came Professor Oak's familiar, cheerful voice.

"Professor Oak," Johan began, taking a deep breath before continuing, "there's a problem with your Pokédex…"

[Pokédex]: (?)

"Oh? A problem? What kind of problem? Tell me about it."

Professor Oak's voice held a hint of surprise.

It wasn't unusual for older models to have bugs, but this was a new version.

Oh right, Johan was the first user. Well, that explains it.

"It's like this…"

Johan briefly described the Pokédex's analysis of Kubfu, especially emphasizing the inconsistencies around the word "justice."

This time, not only had he been tricked by Master Mustard, but he felt like the Pokédex had also deceived him.

"Ah, I see now," Professor Oak mused, sounding thoughtful. "Actually, I obtained the information on Kubfu from Old Mustard…"

Master Mustard had provided input for the Pokédex during his world travels, having crossed paths with Professor Oak along the way.

So, the source of the problem was Mustard after all.

At that moment, Johan heard a new voice on the line.

"Professor Oak, still working? You haven't eaten, have you? I brought some steamed crab—it's the perfect season for it."

It was a woman's voice.

"Oh, Hanako, how thoughtful of you…"

"And Professor, where's Green?" came another voice, youthful and slightly high-pitched, almost baby-like.

"Sorry to interrupt."

Johan ended the call with a curt farewell and a sigh.

Betrayed again.

Back at the dojo.

The small dining table was laden with a variety of dishes, showcasing Honey's impressive culinary skills. Johan couldn't find anything to criticize.

"Professor Johan, would you prefer to pay in cash or card?"

Honey approached with a tray, smiling sweetly.

Johan: "..."

He should've expected this.

The memories of being exploited in-game by Honey were resurfacing, bringing with them a pang of nostalgia.

But Johan quickly remembered something crucial—

This trip was funded by public expenses.

Pulling out a bank card, he declared boldly:

"Honey, charge it to the card!"

Honey's eyes curved into delighted crescents as she pulled out a POS machine, skillfully processing the payment.

POS machines on the Isle of Armor? Truly ahead of her time, Honey.

"Well then, I wish you all a pleasant meal."

While Johan and Mustard enjoyed their lunch, Honey also prepared food for the Pokémon, accommodating their appetites with commendable effort.

However, she had clearly underestimated how much Gengar and the others could eat, keeping her busy running back and forth.

Meanwhile, Mustard's face began to flush as he reached a pleasantly tipsy state.

"Little Johan, hic~~ let me tell you something. You gotta use your Aura properly—like this…"

He wobbled to his feet, hiccuped again, and assumed a forward-facing stance.

His eyes sharpened, and he delivered a punch so swiftly that the air itself seemed to crackle with a dull boom.

"And like this…"

Switching to a stance reminiscent of a one-legged crane, he unleashed a series of rapid punches and kicks at blinding speed.

It was the essence of Single-Strike Style and Rapid-Strike Style.

But…

Johan's expression turned odd.

Perhaps because of Mustard's drunken state, his movements bore an uncanny resemblance to something…

"Lightning Five Whips?" Johan murmured to himself.

"What's that?" Mustard froze mid-motion, having caught Johan's muttered words.

"Lightning Five Whips? Old Mustard's Lightning Five Whips? That's a great name!"

Mustard swayed over to a cabinet, retrieving two scrolls.

One bore a water droplet symbol, while the other featured a half-closed eye.

The secret techniques of Single-Strike and Rapid-Strike Styles?

"This is too valuable!"

Without hesitation, Johan expertly pried open Gengar's mouth and stuffed the scrolls inside for safekeeping.

Mustard waved a hand dismissively.

"Valuable? Not at all. They're just my personal interpretations of the principles of hardness and softness."

Johan had heard of these before—the two primary schools of martial arts philosophy in the Pokémon world.

For example, Bruno adhered to the philosophy of hardness, while trainers like Brawly and Chuck leaned toward softness.

"These aren't as profound as the teachings in the Towers of Two Fists, but they should still be useful to you."

Useful?

That was an understatement—they'd be invaluable for both Tinkatuff and Kubfu.

"Of course, how much you can learn depends on you," Mustard added. "Back in the day, Little Leon got these same scrolls, but he only picked bits and pieces to incorporate into his own style."

Johan nodded.

Every trainer had their own philosophy and approach when it came to raising Pokémon.

The insights he'd gained from texts like Fear Tactics, Dragon Mastery, and Earth's Essence were things he used for inspiration, not imitation.

As the saying goes, "Those who learn from me survive; those who mimic me perish."

"Consider these a token of thanks for taking in that little one."

Huh? A token of thanks?

Was Mustard that eager to get rid of Kubfu? Drunk words are sober thoughts, after all.

Nevertheless, Johan stood up and bowed deeply in gratitude.

"Thank you, Master Mustard!"

Snore~~

Mustard had already flopped onto the floor, snoozing peacefully.

In the afternoon, Johan sat under a beach umbrella, flipping through the scrolls while contemplating training strategies for Tinkatuff and Kubfu.

Kubfu's silky fur and bushy brows screamed martial arts prodigy. Johan wouldn't even be surprised if it suddenly pulled off a Night Guy. (Wait, that's the wrong reference.)

Under Gengar's influence, his thoughts wandered a bit too far.

In any case, whether it evolved into Single-Strike Urshifu or Rapid-Strike Urshifu, Kubfu was fundamentally a physical attacker.

With a base Speed stat of 72, and 97 after evolution, it was even faster than Dragonair and Dragonite.

Its evolution brought about the amazing ability Unseen Fist, which allowed contact moves to bypass protective barriers (except Dynamax shields).

Combine that with its signature moves—Wicked Blow for Single-Strike Style or Surging Strikes for Rapid-Strike Style—both of which had near-guaranteed critical hits.

Pair it with items like Choice Band or Scarf, and its offensive potential was through the roof.

"Maybe… it could even go dual-strike?" Johan mused.

While its Special Attack stat was only 63, it wasn't unheard of. Gengar's Attack stat was similarly low at 65, yet it managed to excel as a mixed attacker.

"Hmm, I'll let Kubfu decide its evolution path."

Bam!

A sudden noise snapped Johan out of his thoughts.

Looking up, he saw Tinkatuff swinging its hammer furiously at a rock.

In the sky above, a Corviknight twitched nervously, instinctively dodging.

But Tinkatuff's aim was true—a rock hurled with perfect precision hit the Corviknight square on the head.

The metal bird plummeted to the ground, dazed.

"Chu—!!!"

Tinkatuff raced forward with glee, pinning the fallen Corviknight with its hammer.

Moments later, Gengar, Vulpix, Dragonair, and Kubfu surrounded the scene.

"Ku~~"

Kubfu's eyes sparkled as it observed.

So that's how it's done!

My old methods of planting trees to claim territory were far too primitive!

This is the power of teamwork!

Kubfu approached the unconscious Corviknight. It clasped its paws together in a gesture of respect and solemnly bowed.

"Ku!"

For the greater good, kindly sacrifice yourself!

Gengar patted Kubfu on the shoulder.

"Ka-kah~~" (No need to sacrifice anyone; we're pacifists here.)

If Johan got mad, none of them would survive his wrath.

Kubfu's expression cleared with understanding.

"Ku!"

For the greater good, I'll pluck its feathers instead!

Rolling up its sleeves, Kubfu joined the others in their "raid."

Watching its new teammates at work, Kubfu smiled.

It already felt like a perfect fit in this squad.

# 230: I Swear Fealty, Chief! (BONUS)

Night fell. To minimize disturbance to others, Honey had arranged for Johan to stay in a small standalone house within Master Mustard's dojo.

Of course, this came at a steep price, which Johan dutifully paid.

Bang, bang—clang, clang— The sound of hammering iron filled the air without pause.

Tinkatuff squatted on the ground, its face glowing with enthusiasm.

In front of it lay its prized and weighty hammer, surrounded by a chaotic pile of Corviknight feathers.

These were trophies from its recent "hunts."

To be fair, Tinkatuff couldn't take all the blame.

Corviknights on the Isle of Armor had never encountered Pokémon like Tinkatuff or Tinkaton before.

Whenever they spotted Tinkatuff's hammer—a metal of extraordinary purity—they couldn't help but be drawn to it.

What they didn't know was that the real predator often masquerades as prey.

When the Corviknights set their sights on Tinkatuff's hammer, Tinkatuff set its sights on their feathers.

This was simply the natural law of the Pokémon world—a "survival of the fittest" scenario Johan couldn't interfere with.

At most, he had ordered Tinkatuff to exercise some restraint.

"Take half the feathers, no more. Let them fly away so we might encounter them again someday."

"I'm just so kind-hearted," Johan thought to himself, sighing.

Even so, in just one day, Tinkatuff's gains exceeded all expectations.

Here it was, hammering away even after dinner, tirelessly swinging for nearly four or five hours straight. A mountain of feathers still awaited incorporation into its hammer.

"Chu~~ Chu~~"

But Tinkatuff showed no signs of fatigue, repeating its mechanical movements with excitement.

This hammer isn't heavy enough! Not nearly heavy enough!

Johan, lying in bed, was slowly being lulled to sleep by the rhythmic clanging. Finally, he couldn't hold it in any longer.

"Tinkatuff, call it a day. You can finish tomorrow—it's bedtime," Johan said.

Although they were in a standalone house, the noise could still disturb others' sleep.

"Chu?"

Tinkatuff paused, wiping the sweat from its brow. After blinking a few times, it reluctantly set the hammer down.

Johan let out a sigh of relief. The world was finally quiet again.

"Gengar, lights out," Johan instructed.

"Ka-kah~~"

Gengar, holding the Scroll of the Wicked Fist, popped its head out of the ceiling and gleefully scurried off to turn off the lights.

"You really are the master of turning things dark," Johan muttered as he cuddled Vulpix. Soon, he drifted into slumber.

It had been an exhausting day—flying to the Isle of Armor, traveling from Wyndon to meet Master Mustard, battling him, and finally capturing Kubfu.

Before long, he was sound asleep.

"Gengar, no causing trouble at night…" Johan mumbled in his sleep.

Gengar floated down from the ceiling, scratching its head.

"Ka-kah?"

Seeing that Johan was fast asleep, Gengar smirked mischievously.

It clapped its hands.

"Ka-kah~~" (Who's up for a late-night adventure?)

"Draaa?"

Dragonair, ever the overachiever, tumbled off the bed.

"Oowu~~"

Vulpix kicked at the blanket but remained firmly trapped in Johan's embrace, reluctantly giving up.

"Chu!"

Tinkatuff stealthily picked up its hammer and climbed down from its bed.

Of course, it had to join. Why?

Because it and Gengar were the ultimate team!

Without Tinkatuff, Gengar's efficiency would drop by at least half.

"Ka-kah?" (Is that it?) "Ku! (－ω－) zzZ"

Kubfu, sound asleep with a bubble on its nose, suddenly stood up, flexing its fists.

Gengar waved a hand in front of Kubfu's face.

"Ka-kah?" (Can you even see me?)

"ZzZ"

Kubfu struck a formidable pose, exuding intense energy.

"Ka-kah!!"

Gengar gave a big thumbs-up.

It didn't fully understand Kubfu's odd behavior, but it had seen how terrifying Kubfu could be in its sleep.

"Ka-kah~!" (Move out!)

Morning sunlight filtered through the windows.

The Isle of Armor's climate was surprisingly similar to Hoenn's.

When Johan woke up without Gengar licking him awake, he knew—something must have happened last night.

"Oowu~~"

Vulpix licked Johan's face, fully rousing him from slumber.

After getting up and washing up, Johan headed outside.

Gengar was munching on a mushroom that looked like a shrunken Poké Ball in one hand, still clutching the Scroll of the Wicked Fist in the other.

As expected, Gengar had been busy overnight.

Dragonair was sunbathing, still full of energy despite recent improvements to its routine.

Tinkatuff was, unsurprisingly, the most hyper. It seemed dead set on single-handedly "plucking" every Corviknight on the Isle of Armor.

The only one that seemed relatively calm was Kubfu.

Yet even while snoring, it was doing push-ups—with a massive boulder on its back, no less.

"Training while awake, training while asleep—this little guy definitely lives up to its serious brows and piercing gaze," Johan muttered.

Wait a second.

An indefatigable Gengar, a sleep-training Kubfu…

Wasn't this the perfect duo?

A familiar sense of exhaustion washed over Johan as he shook his head.

"Vulpix, at least you're the most well-behaved," he said, cuddling the fluffy Pokémon tightly. After several deep breaths, he felt much better.

"O-Oowu~~ (((//Д//)))"

"Breakfast is ready!"

At the sound of Johan's call, Kubfu's nose bubble popped. It tossed aside the boulder and eagerly scampered over with the others.

Watching Kubfu's ravenous appetite, Johan wiped sweat from his brow.

"Calm down, calm down," he muttered.

Kubfu's increased training justified its increased appetite.

Or so he thought.

But soon enough, Johan witnessed Kubfu's truly voracious capacity—rivaled only by Gengar.

"It's fine, it's fine," Johan reassured himself, though his tone betrayed a hint of unease.

The profits from Meowth and Pichu should start rolling in soon.

Feeding one Kubfu was manageable—probably.

If worse came to worst…

He could always open his Pokémon Psychology Clinic to rake in cash from wealthy clients.

"Ku~ Ku~~ (PД`q.)·.'゜"

Mid-meal, tears streamed down Kubfu's face.

"What's wrong?" Johan gently patted its head, concerned.

"Ku~ Ku~~!"

The organization is too good to me!

Not only did they provide such delicious food, but last night's "spoils of nature" surpassed what it usually gathered in an entire month!

Surely, even the heavens approved of its decision to join.

With teary eyes, Kubfu looked at Johan with unwavering resolve.

"Ku~!"

I swear fealty, Chief!

Johan: "…"

Though he wasn't sure why, a strange heaviness settled in his chest.

But from Kubfu's gaze and its Aura, he could feel the little one's sincerity.

After breakfast, Tinkatuff resumed its bird-hunting campaign, aided by Gengar and the others.

Far away, a Corviknight eyeing Tinkatuff's hammer suddenly trembled as dark red particles gathered around it.

Above, ominous black-red clouds began to swirl.

The Corviknight's body swelled as crimson light enveloped it.

Johan, reading on the beach, stood up abruptly, narrowing his eyes at the sight.

"This is…"

The swirling red mist, the rapidly expanding figure—

"Gigantamax?!"

"Chu?"

Tinkatuff blinked, then broke into an ecstatic grin.

"Chu!!!"

What a big bird!
