# 231: So the Hammer Is the True Core?! (BONUS)

A Gigantamax Corviknight!

The concept of Gigantamax was introduced in the eighth generation of Pokémon and is unique to the Galar region.

Gigantamax essentially unleashes a special energy field from within the Pokémon, distorting the surrounding space and making the Pokémon appear much larger than it truly is.

Upon Gigantamaxing, a Pokémon's size grows exponentially, its HP increases, and all its moves transform into Max Moves.

Some Pokémon, like Corviknight, also have Gigantamax Forms, distinct from normal Dynamax, featuring unique appearances and exclusive moves.

In essence, however, Gigantamax is just a more specialized form of Dynamax.

Thanks to its highly marketable nature, Dynamaxing became a central feature of Galar's commercial Pokémon leagues, spurring the region's development into a hub of spectacular Pokémon battles.

Under the joint promotion of Macro Cosmos and the Galar League, this effort proved to be a huge success.

And Corviknight is one such Pokémon with a Gigantamax Form.

"Screeeech—!!"

The massive Gigantamax Corviknight hovered in the sky, its immense body gleaming with dark red highlights. Above its head, three swirling gray-and-red vortexes of energy orbited ominously—a telltale sign of its Gigantamax state.

Whoosh!

The colossal bird flapped its wings, generating terrifying gusts that swept across the land.

"Chu~ Chu~!"

Tinkatuff showed no fear at the sight of the now-enormous Corviknight. On the contrary, its excitement was palpable.

Such a big bird… Just imagine how glorious its feathers must be!

"Ka-kah" (*It's so big*)

Gengar and the others stared in awe at the towering Gigantamax Corviknight.

For most of them, this was their first time witnessing a Gigantamax Pokémon.

Kubfu, however, had seen something similar before and remained relatively composed.

But while they marveled, the Gigantamax Corviknight wasn't idle.

"Screeeeeech—!!"

The massive bird let out another piercing cry, flapping its enormous wings as an oppressive energy rippled outward.

Suddenly, with a forceful swing of its wings, a massive white tornado shot forth at incredible speed.

Gigantamax Windstorm Vortex!

This was the Gigantamax Corviknight's signature move.

[Max Windstorm Vortex: Inflicts damage while nullifying effects like Reflect, Light Screen, Spikes, and terrain-based conditions (Big, Big, BIG Tornado!).]

Johan, however, remained unfazed.

At its core, Gigantamax merely enlarges the Pokémon, amplifies its moves, and improves its accuracy and secondary effects. It doesn't fundamentally alter the Pokémon's inherent strength.

This particular Corviknight, even in its Gigantamax state, was still just that—a Corviknight.

"Vulpix, set up Aurora Veil!" Johan ordered calmly.

Amid the raging winds, flakes of snow began to fall, forming layers of shimmering auroral light.

Gigantamax Windstorm Vortex might have been able to clear Reflect and Light Screen, but it couldn't dispel weather-based barriers like Aurora Veil.

Meanwhile, Tinkatuff climbed onto Dragonair's back, gripping its hammer tightly.

Dragonair's white wing-like fins expanded as it began to ascend toward the sky.

"Ka-kah?" (Can I grow that big too?)

Gengar tilted its head in wonder.

If I could grow that big… just imagine how much I could eat!

"Gengar, Shadow Ball!" Johan's voice cut through Gengar's musings.

"Ka-kah!"

Snapping back to reality, Gengar grinned mischievously.

Raising its hands high, it conjured a massive sphere of darkness in mere moments and hurled it at the colossal Corviknight.

With such a massive target, missing was nearly impossible.

For the first time, Johan realized that Dynamax battles might actually suit him.

"Mom won't have to worry about my moves missing anymore!"

BOOM!

The enormous Shadow Ball struck the Gigantamax Corviknight squarely, eliciting a pained screech.

Simultaneously, Dragonair and Tinkatuff reached the sky.

Tinkatuff leapt high off Dragonair's back.

"Draaa~~!"

Dragonair unleashed a thunderous bolt of lightning from its horn, striking the Gigantamax Corviknight with pinpoint accuracy.

"Kubfu, Coaching!" Johan called out.

Kubfu nodded solemnly, cupping its hands around its mouth as it shouted toward Tinkatuff:

"Ku~~!"

Tinkatuff felt a surge of power course through its body.

But more importantly, Kubfu's shout conveyed strategic advice—combat tips honed through its own training.

One strike… decisive and lethal?

"Chu!!"

Tinkatuff's eyes gleamed as it gripped its hammer.

Let's try it!

Frost began to enshroud the hammer as it swung through the air, growing layer by layer until the hammer itself expanded dramatically.

Just as the Gigantamax Corviknight began to recover from the Shadow Ball and Thunderbolt, it found its vision dominated by an enormous hammer, rapidly descending toward it.

"Scree—scree?!"

The Gigantamax Corviknight's mind reeled.

Wait a second. I'm the one who's Gigantamaxed!

Why are your moves more overpowered than mine?! Are they even legal?!

But it didn't have time to think.

BOOM!

The massive ice-coated hammer struck its head with devastating force, like a freight train slamming into it.

The colossal bird flipped backward, its eyes rolling into its head as its consciousness faded.

As it plummeted to the ground, its body began to shrink.

Thud.

Tinkatuff landed gracefully, caught mid-air by Dragonair, before they both descended toward the fainted Corviknight.

Tinkatuff, however, bit its lip nervously as it stared at the shrinking bird.

"Chu! Chu~ Chu~!!"

Stop shrinking! Don't shrink anymore!

Meanwhile, Gengar floated toward the falling Corviknight, grinning deviously.

Its eyes glinted with a faint light as it activated Trick, temporarily gaining Tinkatuff's Pickpocket ability.

"Ka-kah~~"

A sly smirk spread across Gengar's face as shadowy duplicates emerged beside it.

With multiple clones working in unison, Gengar began rummaging through the fainted Corviknight's feathers and metallic body.

Johan, watching from below, stood slack-jawed.

So that's how Gengar exploits Tinkatuff's Pickpocket ability!

Normally, Pickpocket has only a 30% chance of stealing items.

But with sheer numbers and repetition, Gengar effectively bypassed this limitation.

"Gengar… you never cease to amaze me," Johan muttered.

Moments later, Gengar's eyes sparkled as it extracted a dark red orb from the Corviknight's body.

"Ka-kah?" (What's this?)

It inspected the glowing object curiously before stuffing it into its mouth.

Before Johan could even question Gengar, his attention was drawn to Tinkatuff.

The little hammer-wielding Pokémon had landed on the ground and closed its eyes, adopting an unusually serious expression.

Johan's heart skipped a beat.

Could it be…

Tinkatuff opened its eyes moments later, a look of realization dawning on its face.

Relieved, Johan sighed.

It seemed Tinkatuff had simply learned a new combat technique, likely inspired by Kubfu's coaching.

"Chu~ Chu~"

Tinkatuff waddled toward the fainted Corviknight, puffing its cheeks indignantly.

The once-massive bird was now far too small to be worth its time.

Johan couldn't help but comment:

"You've already collected so many feathers. Does your hammer even have room for more?"

Tinkatuff blinked.

That… makes sense!

Just as Johan began to relax, Gengar pointed at Tinkatuff with a wide-eyed expression.

Before Johan could ask why, he noticed a bright white light enveloping Tinkatuff.

It was evolving!

Tinkatuff's body grew slightly, its single ponytail splitting into twin tails.

But the real change was its hammer, which swelled to an enormous size—several times its previous dimensions!

When the light faded, the newly evolved Tinkaton stood proudly, hammer in hand.

"Cha~~!"

It ran toward the Corviknight, swinging its now-massive hammer like a child with a new toy.

Johan buried his face in his hands.

Evolution was just an excuse to upgrade its hammer… How much metal will it take to fill that thing?!

# 232: The Leader’s Career Ends Before It Even Starts! (BONUS)

The Steel-type tragedy on the Isle of Armor continues.

Johan sighed to himself.

This wasn't just about the size of the hammer.

With Tinkaton's evolution, the hammer's weight limit and its capacity for integrating materials had significantly increased. Additionally, the hammer's quality—or more accurately, its density—had improved dramatically.

Before, a fist-sized piece of metal could be compacted down to a fingernail-sized fragment. Now, to achieve the same effect, Tinkaton might need a basketball-sized chunk of metal.

The demand for materials had skyrocketed exponentially.

CLANG! CLANG! CLANG!

As Johan mourned for the local Corviknight population, Tinkaton's hammering sounds pulled his attention back.

Squatting on the ground, the little Pokémon was working diligently, its focus unmatched.

But the process had clearly changed.

Not only were the hammering sounds louder, but the rate at which Tinkaton was integrating metals had become ridiculously fast.

What once took it strenuous effort to embed a single Corviknight feather into its hammer was now accomplished in mere moments.

Johan took a deep breath.

The increase in efficiency also meant an increase in consumption.

And...

"Is that Gigaton Hammer?"

He squinted at the hammering movements.

[Gigaton Hammer: A 160-power Steel-type move that deals heavy damage to the target but cannot be used consecutively. (Big Hammer, 160 damage!)]

Gigaton Hammer was Tinkaton's signature move, boasting tremendous power, though its inability to be used back-to-back was a notable drawback.

Still, this limitation could be circumvented through strategies like Encore, Instruct, or Sleep Talk, enabling repeated use of Gigaton Hammer and unleashing devastating combos.

For now, though, Gigaton Hammer seemed to be Tinkaton's ideal tool for forging.

As Tinkaton worked tirelessly, Johan glanced at the updated information in his Pokédex:

[Tinkaton]

Type: Fairy/Steel

Ability: Pickpocket

Moves Known: Ice Hammer, Feint, Astonish, Fairy Wind, Baby-Doll Eyes, Metal Claw, Stealth Rock, Skill Swap, Swords Dance, Brick Break, Gigaton Hammer, Brutal Swing, Fake Out.

Mood: Excited, determined (Bring me ten tons—no, a hundred tons of heavy metal!)

Condition: Excellent (Its state is inexplicably superb.)

Friendship Level: 100 (Mom, I evolved again!)

With its evolution, Tinkaton had learned three new moves:

Gigaton Hammer – Its signature Steel-type move.Brutal Swing – A Dark-type move that deals damage to all nearby opponents.Fake Out – A high-priority Normal-type move that flinches the target if used immediately after entering battle.

Fake Out, in particular, was a favorite among competitive players. Its ability to force the opponent to flinch and disrupt their strategy made it an excellent tool for setting up momentum.

Johan's gaze lingered on Tinkaton's mood entry for a moment.

Let's just ignore that... If I don't look at it, it doesn't exist.

After offering the Corviknight population another silent moment of mourning, Johan turned his attention to Gengar.

"Gengar, what exactly did you pull off that Corviknight?"

"Ka-kah~~"

Gengar scratched its head, looking equally puzzled.

So, it opened its mouth wide and reached into its stomach.

But after much rummaging, Gengar still hadn't pulled anything out.

"Ka-kah..."

Weird...

"What's wrong?" Johan asked.

"Ka-kah!"

It's gone!

Johan frowned.

"Gone? What's gone?"

Gengar gestured excitedly, its hands waving and mimicking an explosion.

"You mean you grabbed something dark red and glowing off the Corviknight?"

"Ka-kah! Ka-kah!"

Gengar nodded eagerly.

Johan's expression turned thoughtful.

Could it have been Galar particles?

Galar particles were unique to the Galar region, closely tied to Eternatus, the source of Dynamax energy.

Items like the Wishing Star could absorb these particles, and the Dynamax Band that enabled Pokémon to Dynamax was also made from Wishing Stars.

The Gigantamax Corviknight earlier had likely undergone its transformation due to an abnormal accumulation of Galar particles.

But Gengar somehow managed to snag this cluster of particles and, for some reason, put it in its stomach...

"Did the particles dissipate?"

"Or... did Gengar absorb them?"

No, that's impossible. Only Eternatus can absorb Galar particles.

Probably just dispersed.

Still, Johan asked, "Gengar, do you feel anything unusual?"

"Ka-kah?"

Gengar tilted its head before clutching its stomach and collapsing dramatically onto the sand, its face contorted in mock agony.

Johan froze in alarm before catching a glint of mischief in Gengar's eyes.

He sighed heavily.

"You little..."

Walking over, Johan placed a hand on Gengar's shoulder.

Aura sense, activate!

Through his Aura, he checked Gengar's condition.

Flawless. Absolutely perfect.

Johan exhaled sharply and waved dismissively.

"It's hopeless. Bury it."

"Ka-kah?! Σ(дlll)"

Vulpix and Dragonair immediately caught on, smiling mischievously as they advanced toward Gengar.

Kubfu, however, was utterly convinced.

Tears streaming down its face, it began digging a grave in the sand.

"Ku~~!! (;Д｀)"

The boss's career ends before it even begins!

I'll carry on your will!

Unable to hold it in any longer, Gengar shot up with a startled gulp, frantically assuring everyone it was fine and that it was all a joke.

Johan smiled faintly before roaring, "THUNDEROUS OUTRAGE!"

Meanwhile, in Galar's Spikemuth Energy Plant...

Deep within the facility, a massive orb rested atop a platform, surrounded by scientists clad in dark uniforms.

The room buzzed with activity as data was collected and analyzed.

Suddenly, warning lights flashed, and an alarm blared.

"What's happening?!"

"The core is becoming active!"

"Could it be the expansion of the storage chamber?"

"Or the particles dispersed during the process?"

"But... Chairman Rose said the core was in a dormant state. Without its full power, there's no way it could..."

Inside the orb, a pair of piercing white eyes cracked open slightly.

For the first time in eons, the gaze flickered with faint curiosity before shutting again.

A deep, rumbling voice echoed faintly within the sphere.

Back on the Isle of Armor...

Gigantamax Corviknight's rampage had ended swiftly, and with no major disruptions, Tinkaton had resumed its "bird hunting."

Its newly upgraded hammer attracted even more Corviknight, and with its evolution, it now needed no assistance to bring them down.

Standing to the side, Kubfu diligently coached Tinkaton in mastering One Blow Style techniques.

Lying back on a beach chair, Johan retrieved the shimmering Rainbow Feather, giving it a light infusion of Aura to restore its radiant glow.

"Don't dim."

Satisfied with the feather's brilliance, Johan grinned.

"Who's more pure-hearted than me?"

"Ka-kah?"

Elsewhere, Gengar and Dragonair stumbled upon a Slowpoke basking lazily on the sand.

"Yadon?"

The Slowpoke slowly turned its head, its golden-tipped tail glinting in the sunlight, as if in a daze.

Gengar and Dragonair tilted their heads in unison.

What was with this Slowpoke?

It looked like any other Slowpoke... but something about it felt odd.

As they stared at each other, time seemed to stand still.

---

---

---

# 233: Ordinary Radish Seeds

Two days later.

A gentle breeze swept across the skies of the Isle of Armor as Johan sat in a flying taxi, holding a paper bag in his hands. His expression was slightly peculiar.

Kubfu leaned against the window, tears welling in its eyes as it bit its lip and waved goodbye to the island below.

"Ku~! (;Д｀)"

Farewell, my starter village! We will succeed in our endeavor someday!

On the ground below...

Master Mustard, Honey, and the dojo's apprentices couldn't contain their smiles of relief as they waved the group goodbye.

They were finally leaving! The Isle of Armor could finally have some peace!

The Pokéball on Johan's belt opened on its own as Tinkaton emerged into the taxi's cabin.

"Cha~~"

Seeing that it had no intention of going after the Corviknight below, Johan let out a small sigh of relief.

Even so, Tinkaton looked a little wistful.

"Cha~~" (I'll miss this place.)

But it had nearly plucked the entire Corviknight population of the island bare.

Just last night, Johan had spotted a group of Corviknight sneakily preparing to migrate under the cover of darkness.

Tinkaton had yelled at them to stay.

According to one particularly traumatized Corviknight, it had said:

"Kaah! Kaah! Kaah!!"

Which roughly translated to:

"You don't understand how terrifying that pink devil with the giant hammer is."

It may have looked harmless, but the sheer joy it showed while plucking their feathers sent shivers down their spines.

If only they could stay high enough in the air and avoid its hammer...

But who could have guessed that it had a flying dragon for backup?!

Johan glanced over at Tinkaton and Dragonair.

If they didn't leave now, the Corviknight population on the Isle of Armor might actually go extinct.

He had even seen it with his own eyes:

A group of nearly featherless Corviknight hopping cautiously on the ground in search of food, not daring to fly.

The moment they took off, they'd be instantly targeted.

To make up for it, Johan had handed out a substantial number of Pokéblocks as compensation.

At the very least, they needed proper nutrition to regrow their feathers.

"I really am too kind," Johan thought with a sigh.

The Alolan Vulpix curled up in Johan's lap stirred slightly, letting out a soft cry.

"Oowu~~" (It's nice not to feel sad during a goodbye.)

It hated parting, but this one didn't feel so bad.

"Sad farewells?" Johan twitched his lips slightly.

He looked at Gengar, who sat across from him, wearing an innocent expression as it nibbled on a poisonous mushroom.

Honestly, if it were only Tinkaton, it wouldn't have been so bad.

At most, it was just bullying the island's Corviknight population.

But Gengar... Gengar was the true terror of the island.

After swapping abilities with Tinkaton, Gengar had taken the sleepwalking Kubfu into the woods at night.

Who knew what horrors they had unleashed?

According to Gengar, it was simply:

"I'm a natural-born jungler!"

Kubfu, on the other hand, had described it as:

"The abundance of nature's gifts is overwhelming!"

In just a few days, they had gathered enough resources to match what Kubfu would normally collect in half a year.

There was even a rumor that Honey, on a routine mushroom-picking trip, had walked for miles without finding a single one.

The dojo's dinner that night consisted of plain bread rolls and pancakes.

Water was scarce, and the students had to fight each other to decide who got the bigger share.

Noticing Johan's gaze, Gengar quickly finished its mushroom and flashed a "harmless" smile.

"Ka-kah~~" (I really liked this place, too.)

The Isle of Armor was fantastic. The Pokémon here were skilled and friendly—truly a paradise for talents.

Johan chuckled darkly.

In truth, Mustard and Honey had given them a parting gift (or "thank-you gift").

It was the paper bag of seeds Johan now held.

At first, Johan had been confused.

Seeds? For him? He wasn't a farmer.

But Honey had explained:

"These seeds are quite old. Unfortunately, I couldn't get them to grow, but their origins are extraordinary—they were passed down from the ancient kings of Galar."

When Honey was still the head of a trading company, she had acquired the seeds by chance but had never been able to make them sprout.

When Johan received the bag, his first thought was:

Ancient seeds from Galar? After all these years, they're still viable? That would be the real miracle.

But then he opened the bag.

Inside, the seeds were plump and lustrous, with a faint glow.

Ancient seeds? Really?

Luckily, the Pokédex provided some clarification.

[Radish Seeds]: When planted in icy terrain, they grow into Ice Radishes. When planted in ghostly, deathly soil, they grow into Black Radishes. (You're welcome.)

The mention of "Ice Radish" and "Black Radish" immediately brought to mind one specific Pokémon:

Calyrex.

Calyrex, known as the "King of Bountiful Harvests," was a legendary Pokémon said to have ruled Galar in ancient times. Known for its extraordinary intelligence, it was said to oversee all things past, present, and future.

Calyrex also had two steed Pokémon: Glastrier and Spectrier, which loved Ice Radishes and Black Radishes respectively.

The problem?

Johan shook the bag, which still held quite a few seeds.

"Even if you gave these to me, I can't grow them."

The conditions for cultivating these radishes were far too specific.

Legends about Calyrex, Glastrier, and Spectrier were virtually forgotten in Galar.

He glanced down at the Vulpix in his lap and then at Gengar across from him.

Could it work?

If Alolan Vulpix created a patch of snow and Gengar conjured an eerie, ghostly "graveyard," perhaps they could recreate the conditions needed to grow these radishes.

It wasn't about luring Glastrier or Spectrier—both were fiercely proud, powerful Pokémon not easily subdued, even by Calyrex itself without the Reins of Unity.

Instead, Johan figured the radishes must hold immense nutritional value if they were so coveted.

If he managed to grow them, they'd make excellent snacks for Vulpix and Gengar, perhaps in the form of carrot mochi.

"Worth a try."

After arriving back in Littleroot Town following a long flight, Johan released his Pokémon from their balls to let them reacquaint themselves with home.

For him, it wasn't just about strength. It was about helping each Pokémon feel at home in the family.

Upstairs...

"Ku~~!" (Is this our new lair?)

"Ka-kah~~" (Stick with me, and I'll show you our kingdom tonight!)

The other Pokémon grinned knowingly as they watched Gengar wrap an arm around Kubfu.

"Ku~!"

Kubfu's eyes shone with determination.

This is what it means to be part of a team. I love it here!

The Pokémon began showing Kubfu their favorite things:

"Oowu" (You can climb these shelves as much as you want.)

"Cha~" (Feel free to hammer these metals.)

"Draaa" (The bathtub is for relaxing.)

Gengar adjusted its sunglasses.

"Ka-kah~~" (If you meet wild Pokémon out there, just drop my name—Fear Incarnate!)

Kubfu blinked, nodded solemnly, and puffed out its chest.

"Ku-ku!" (I already feel the pride of being part of this crew!)

# 234: The Great Garden Battle!

Kubfu adapted to its new environment much faster than Johan expected, which, to his relief, was a good sign.

It meant Kubfu was highly compatible with him and his team.

After lunch, Johan made a trip to the department store, then returned to the café with his Pokémon, heading straight to the backyard.

Although Honey's seeds probably wouldn't grow, it would be wasteful not to at least try.

He had sectioned off two square-meter plots in the garden's northeast and northwest corners, designated for planting Ice Radishes and Black Radishes respectively.

With winter approaching, most of the garden's plants had withered, making it an excellent time for cleanup.

Johan had gone to the department store specifically to buy winter-hardy flower seeds for the rest of the garden.

The Backyard

Dressed in an old outfit and a headscarf, Johan instantly transformed into a rugged farmer.

"How's it going, Tinkaton?" he asked, hands on his hips.

The clanging of metal came to a halt.

"Cha~ Cha~!"

Carrying a massive hammer in one hand and an assortment of tools in the other, Tinkaton proudly strutted over, its pink cheeks flushed as if seeking praise.

Johan chuckled, giving its twin-braided head a vigorous rub.

"Good job!"

Tools were essential for garden repairs.

Sure, he could've bought them, but why bother when Tinkaton could forge far superior custom ones?

Store-bought tools weren't tailored to Pokémon anyway.

"Cha~~"

Tinkaton beamed with pride.

Johan began assigning tasks.

"Kubfu, the weeds and dry grass in the garden are all yours."

"Ku~!"

Kubfu accepted a small hoe with a solemn nod.

Proper food reserves are the foundation of any operation, it thought. This is agriculture at its core!

Johan's eye twitched. He was getting used to Kubfu's obsession with "revolutionary plans," even if he still had no idea what—or who—it intended to overthrow.

"Tinkaton, you're in charge of pruning the trees in the garden."

Johan handed it a pair of shears and added:

"Just like plucking feathers from Corviknight—trim only half the branches. Too much, and the trees will look bare and ugly."

"Put the trimmed branches together with Kubfu's weeds for later disposal."

"Cha?"

Tinkaton glanced at the shears, then at its hammer, and hesitated.

Can't I just use my hammer?

"Absolutely not!" Johan vetoed immediately.

Using a hammer? The garden wouldn't have a single intact tree left!

"This is a test of your control over your strength," Johan said seriously. "Can you follow my instructions without damaging the trees?"

Tinkaton nodded, enlightened.

"Cha~!"

Why didn't you say so earlier?

It obediently took the shears.

"And Dragonair?" Johan called, looking around.

"Draaa~~"

Dragonair's voice came from behind him.

It had gotten stuck on a window ledge after flying down from the second floor.

Johan sighed. Dragonair's streak of bad luck was par for the course.

Rescuing Dragonair, he assigned its task:

"You're on watering duty. Just make sure you control the water flow, okay?"

Dragonair's ability to manipulate weather, combined with its water-type moves, made it perfect for the job—as long as it didn't turn watering into a flood.

"Draaa~!"

Finally, Johan turned to the two most critical Pokémon for his planting project: Alolan Vulpix and Gengar.

"Vulpix, I need you to build two raised flowerbeds out of ice. The ice must be sturdy enough not to melt easily."

Whether it was Ice Radishes or Black Radishes, both thrived in Crown Tundra-like conditions. Temperature wouldn't be an issue.

"Oowu!"

Vulpix nodded, grabbing the blueprint—a loose sketch at best—and began constructing in the corner.

That left Gengar.

Johan turned to the chubby ghost with a sly smile.

"Ka-kah?" Gengar gulped, instinctively stepping back.

What are you planning with that mischievous grin?

Johan motioned for it to relax.

"Gengar, let me ask you: if those radishes grow, who do you think will eat them?"

"Ka-kah~~" (Me and Vulpix, obviously.)

"Would you like to try them?"

"Ka-kah~~(＠￣￢￣＠)"

Drool trickled from Gengar's mouth.

Johan's smile widened.

"Then you understand that special seeds like these require fertile soil, right?"

Gengar nodded instinctively but froze as Johan continued:

"And how do we make the soil more fertile?"

Gengar's mind blanked.

"Ka-kah... (▽.)"

Crap. This feels like a setup.

Thinking quickly, Gengar offered, "Ka-kah?" (Should I bury some bodies?)

Johan's face darkened. Grabbing Gengar by its spikes, he cut to the chase:

"Enough nonsense! You pilfered so much on the Isle of Armor—hand over some of it, or else!"

Gengar yelped, "Ka-KAH!! ヽ(ｏ`皿′ｏ)" (Fine! Take it!)

Using its most menacing tone, it gave the most cowardly response.

It reached into its stomach dimension and pulled out a heap of items, leaving Johan speechless.

"No wonder Master Mustard and Honey wanted us gone so badly…"

The pile included mushrooms, toxic mushrooms, Galarica twigs, multicolored berries, various seeds, molted Pokémon materials, and more.

With such a haul, Johan no longer worried about soil fertility.

Wrapping the items in cloth, he said, "I'll turn these into compost. Gengar, help Vulpix with the flowerbeds."

Dashing into the house, he murmured, "There's enough here for cooking and making Pokéblocks later."

"Ka-kah~~"

Gengar sighed in relief. At least I only handed over a small portion…

When Johan returned, the garden repair and planting project officially began!

The Garden Battle

Dragonair hovered above, exhaling a fine mist. Gentle rain fell, creating a miniature rainbow under the sunlight.

Kubfu, holding its hoe, narrowed its eyes and focused.

"Ku~~(－ω－) zzZ"

Water. Hoe. Soil.

Rapid-Hoe Technique!

Dirt flew everywhere as Kubfu tilled the soil with blinding speed.

Thwack!

A wet clump of dirt splattered onto Johan's face.

He froze.

The Pokémon stifled their laughter, biting their lips.

Then a sudden gush of water from above drenched him completely.

Johan looked up at Dragonair, who smiled innocently.

"Draaa~~" (You're welcome.)

"Thanks…" Johan muttered through gritted teeth.

Gengar couldn't hold back and burst out laughing.

"Ka-kah!"

Its laughter triggered the rest—Vulpix, Tinkaton, and even Dragonair joined in.

Even the sleeping Kubfu twitched its head, as if laughing in its dreams.

"Funny, huh?" Johan smirked darkly, grabbing a handful of mud.

"Take this—Mud Style: Earth Splash Barrage!"

Splat! Splat! Splat!

Mud flew in every direction, covering every Pokémon.

Their laughter stopped abruptly.

Oh, it's war now.

Gengar and Vulpix exchanged glances.

Gengar: "Time to unleash my unbeatable ground techniques!"

Vulpix: "I know Dig, remember?"

Even Tinkaton gleefully patted its hammer.

"Cha~ Cha~~" (Didn't expect to use this again so soon.)

The backyard became a mud-slinging battlefield.

Dragonair, realizing what was happening, took a deep breath.

"Draaa~~" (Waterfall Barrage, activate!)

Johan and the Pokémon froze in panic.

"Dragonair, no!"

The Aftermath

Though chaotic, the planting plan was eventually completed.

One flowerbed shimmered with snow and ice, while the other emitted faint wisps of black mist, thanks to a small piece of Reaper Cloth Gengar reluctantly sacrificed.

Still, Johan scratched his chin.

"Feels like something's still missing…"

Without proper isolation, the conditions might affect other garden plants.

Drip-drip!

His phone buzzed.

The caller ID read "Forever Eighteen"—Lorelei.

Lorelei?

Curious, Johan answered.

"Johan, take a look at what I just sent you," Lorelei said.

"An Ice Sculpting Contest?"

# 235: "The Reimbursement King" Returns to Action!

Friday.

A gentle breeze drifted through the air as Johan lounged lazily on the couch, scrolling through his phone.

"Vulpix," he called softly.

The Alolan Vulpix, perched atop a nearby cat tower, perked up at his voice. It stretched luxuriously on the platform, then gracefully hopped down from tier to tier.

In no time, it was standing on the top of the couch behind him.

Johan smiled and patted the open space beside him.

Understanding immediately, the little fox leapt down from the couch's backrest and landed lightly beside him, curling up against his arm.

"Oowu~~" it purred softly.

Johan placed a hand on its soft, warm belly and gave it a gentle rub before pulling it into a deep embrace.

"Ahhh—!"

Vulpix squirmed with delight, its tail wagging in contentment.

Sometimes Johan thought it might be nice to live like this forever—simple, carefree days spent with his Pokémon.

Reading quietly with Gengar, cuddling with Vulpix, working out with Tinkaton, giving Dragonair a bath, and telling Kubfu stories before bed…

Recently, Kubfu had developed a fascination with Johan's stories—particularly The Romance of the Three Kingdoms and Water Margin.

But dreams were beautiful, and reality was harsh.

Johan might want peace, but his Pokémon didn't necessarily share that sentiment.

So, to keep things under control, he made sure they were busy enough during the day to sleep soundly at night.

Johan held up his phone for Vulpix to see.

"An ice sculpture competition. Interested?"

"Oowu?"

Vulpix blinked, suddenly alert. Its interest was immediately piqued as it stared at the intricate ice sculptures displayed on his screen.

Seeing its enthusiasm, Johan smiled.

He'd figured as much—this competition was right up Vulpix's alley.

Building snowmen was one of its favorite activities.

And to Vulpix, there wasn't much difference between sculpting and snowman-building.

"It's part of the winter festival in Snowbelle City, Kalos," Johan explained.

With winter approaching, all sorts of events were being held across the Pokémon world—skiing contests, ice sculpture competitions, figure skating championships, and more.

Each event offered attractive prizes, drawing trainers from around the globe.

This particular competition in Snowbelle City was no exception.

The grand prize was a high-quality Icy Rock sponsored by the Lysandre Lab, along with a generous supply of Figy Berries.

Even the runners-up and third-place winners would receive Figy Berries, an Ice Stone, and Never-Melt Ice—all rare and valuable items.

Johan already owned a piece of Never-Melt Ice, gifted by Lorelei.

But just like the Ice Stone he'd once obtained, that Never-Melt Ice had been mostly consumed by Vulpix.

According to Lorelei, the Never-Melt Ice in this competition was of much higher quality.

Besides, one piece wasn't nearly enough for Vulpix, who had a penchant for hoarding.

"An Ice Stone," Johan murmured thoughtfully.

He'd always wanted to give Vulpix an Ice Stone.

He didn't know the quality of the one being offered here, but even the worst Ice Stone would fetch a high price.

And even if they didn't win, participation alone came with a consolation prize of Figy Berries.

"Oowu!!"

Vulpix perked up, nodding enthusiastically.

It wanted to compete!

Johan chuckled softly, stroking its head.

He couldn't help but marvel at how far his little fox had come—from the shy and timid Pokémon he'd met to one ready to stand on a stage and showcase its talents.

"All right!"

If Vulpix wanted to join—and with such enticing prizes on the line—he had no reason to refuse.

Besides, he was planning a trip to Kalos anyway, at Professor Sycamore's invitation.

His only hesitation had been the competition's connection to Lysandre Labs.

But on second thought, it didn't matter.

If Lysandre had any interest in him, Diantha would've already mentioned it.

Since he'd heard nothing, it was safe to assume Lysandre wasn't concerned about a small-time Pokémon psychologist.

"Let's get ready," Johan said. "We're going to Kalos."

Perhaps Snowbelle City, with its perpetual snow and ice, would provide some insight into growing Ice Radishes.

That afternoon, however, he received a call from Professor Sycamore's lab—not from Alain this time, but the professor himself.

"I apologize for not being available last time," Sycamore said, his voice tinged with weariness and genuine regret.

"No problem, Professor. I was already planning to visit Kalos," Johan replied.

"Oh, perfect! When will you arrive? I'll send someone to meet you at the airport."

"No need for that, Professor. I'd like to take my time exploring Kalos."

Kalos was inspired by France, with Lumiose City modeled after Paris, the City of Light.

Johan wanted to soak in the romantic culture at his own pace.

Still, he wasn't in a rush.

As a teacher at Rustboro Pokémon Academy, he had administrative tasks to finish, even though classes were over.

And, more importantly, he needed to file his expense report.

Rustboro Pokémon Academy: Principal's Office

Martin stood awkwardly, holding Johan's reimbursement form, and passed it to the principal, whose bruises from the last incident still hadn't faded.

"This is Johan's expense report from his trip to Galar," Martin said hesitantly.

He didn't know how to explain the document.

The principal, cheerful as ever, waved off Martin's concern.

"A reimbursement form? Why so nervous?"

But as he scanned the document, his eyes widened.

"How much?!"

"Well… uh…" Martin cleared his throat. "Principal, maybe Johan made a mistake somewhere…"

Skipping over the smaller items, Martin read aloud:

"Eighteen Energy Roots. Twelve Full Restores. Twenty Max Potions…"

He coughed awkwardly. "I'll confirm the details with Johan later."

"No need."

The principal shook his head solemnly and placed the form on his desk.

Martin's heart sank. Oh no. Even the principal is mad this time.

BAM!

The principal slammed his desk, then waved his hand decisively.

"Approved!"

"Huh?" Martin was stunned.

The principal sighed wistfully.

"Johan beat Master Mustard."

"Wha—Master Mustard? That Master Mustard?"

Martin's jaw dropped.

"Yes," the principal nodded.

When he first heard the news from Master Mustard himself, he'd been just as shocked as Martin was now.

Mustard might be old, but he was an undefeated champion for 18 consecutive years!

"That level of victory requires unimaginable effort," the principal continued.

Johan's lengthy list of healing items only proved how much he'd endured.

Martin exhaled slowly. Something feels off about this… but if the principal says so, I'll let it go.

Still, one question lingered in his mind: What did Johan need 100 kilograms of coffee beans for?

On the plane

Johan sneezed unexpectedly.

He glanced at his phone, which displayed a notification for his approved reimbursement.

Smiling, he murmured, "The academy really does take care of its own."

Gengar, meanwhile, was cackling uncontrollably.

"Ka-kah~~"

It clutched its phone, tears streaming down its face, as it watched the latest episode of Meowth & Pichu.

Thanks to Matori and Team Rocket's efforts, the show had finally aired.

With the combined influence of Team Rocket and Devon Corporation, it premiered across all major networks and streaming platforms.

Feedback was overwhelmingly positive.

The series boasted a 9.5 approval rating, rivaling the popularity of The Adventures of Popplio!

And that was after just three episodes.

Matori had also gained Johan's approval to shoot scenes on location across different regions, aiming to make the series even more spectacular.

Johan, however, was more concerned about his share of the profits.

Outside the plane window, the weather was clear.

The outline of the Kalos region loomed in the distance.

Kalos, shaped like a five-pointed star, was divided into several distinct areas.

The plane was set to land in the heart of it all: Lumiose City.

Known for the Prism Tower and Lumiose Museum, Lumiose City was the largest and most populous city in the Pokémon world.

Its economy thrived on tourism, supported by cafés, shops, and restaurants scattered throughout the city.

One of Johan's goals was to scout potential new hires for his café—whether human or Pokémon.

"Dear passengers, we are now approaching Lumiose City…"

"Ka-kah!" Gengar stowed its phone and fastened its seatbelt.

Johan glanced at the ghost-type, feeling an ominous premonition.

Is Gengar's Mega Evolution about to get out of hand?

# 236: Mega Stone and Key Stone!

Kalos, Lumiose City.

From a distance, the iconic Prism Tower stood tall, piercing the sky.

Bathed in sunlight, the tower's surface refracted a soft, radiant glow, showcasing a unique architectural beauty.

Inside the Prism Tower lay Lumiose Gym, making the structure not just a landmark of Lumiose City, but also a hub for aspiring trainers.

In Johan's memory, the Lumiose Gym Leader was Clemont, an expert in Electric-type Pokémon and a man fondly nicknamed "The Zap Genius."

He was known for creating bizarre gadgets, which often exploded, giving their users an unintentional "perm."

Clemont had a younger sister, Bonnie, an adorably bubbly girl who often accompanied him.

But considering that Ash was still just a seven-year-old kid at this time, Clemont was likely still in school himself.

Fortunately, Johan wasn't here to challenge gyms, so the identity of the current leader wasn't of much consequence to him.

Still, he couldn't help but chuckle at a curious trend: every region seemed to have an Electric Gym Leader who excelled in engineering and mechanics.

From Hoenn's Wattson, who built the underground "New Mauville," to Sinnoh's Volkner, who transformed Sunyshore City into an energy-efficient marvel, these innovators shaped their regions.

Even Kalos's Clemont showed promise, despite being young.

Only Kanto's Lt. Surge stood out—a rugged military man with little interest in invention.

"Ka-kah~~"

Gengar held up a tourist brochure provided during the flight, comparing the map to their current location.

Lumiose City was laid out in a radial pattern, with Prism Tower at its center, roads fanning outward like spokes on a wheel.

According to the brochure, Professor Sycamore's Lab was situated on South Boulevard, on the city's southern outskirts.

Nearby were three popular cafés: Café Soleil, Café Lysander, and Café Introversion.

Lumiose City was renowned for having the most cafés of any city in the Pokémon world, a testament to its thriving coffee culture.

"Good thing there's no river of sewage running through the city," Johan muttered, glancing at the map on his Pokédex.

Imagine if there were some kind of boat parade—people might end up seasick rather than charmed.

Thankfully, while Lumiose City drew inspiration from Paris, it avoided mimicking its messier aspects. The streets were clean, with no trace of litter or unsightly piles.

And since the Ice Sculpture Contest wasn't being held in Lumiose City itself, they could enjoy their time here without feeling rushed.

Gengar waved down a man in a blue uniform leading several Skiddo.

"Ka-kah!!"

The man approached with a friendly smile.

"Hello, sir. Do you need a ride on a Skiddo?"

Skiddo were docile Grass-type Pokémon with remarkable endurance. They could sense a rider's emotions through their horns, making them ideal for leisurely sightseeing.

"Yes, please," Johan said, smiling.

"Just one?"

Johan glanced at Gengar, whose eyes sparkled with excitement.

"Better make that two," he said with a chuckle.

Johan mounted one Skiddo with Vulpix in his arms, while Gengar climbed onto the other.

The cobblestone streets of Lumiose City stretched out before them, flanked by charming, sun-dappled buildings.

Sycamore trees lined the avenues, their branches swaying gently in the breeze.

With every step, the tranquil beauty of the city enveloped them, from its laid-back architecture to the warm glow of the afternoon sun.

Clip-clop.

The rhythmic sound of Skiddo hooves filled the air.

Vulpix sat nestled in Johan's lap, its curious eyes darting from one sight to another.

Not far away, a woman carrying a parasol strolled leisurely, leading her Pokémon—a Furfrou styled in an elegant Matron Trim.

"Look over there," Johan said, pointing in the opposite direction.

A gentleman in a crisp white suit and round hat walked alongside a Furfrou sporting the debonair Dandy Trim.

"Oowu~!"

Vulpix's eyes sparkled with wonder.

"If you'd like, we can get you styled like a queen," Johan teased.

"Oowu~ oowu~!"

The little fox's eyes gleamed at the thought of being a queen, but it quickly shook its head, blushing furiously.

It couldn't handle being that fashionable!

Johan burst out laughing, ruffling its head affectionately.

"Don't worry. I'd never make you look ridiculous. How about a cute little princess dress instead?"

Vulpix snuggled closer, relieved.

"Wait, where's Gengar?" Johan suddenly realized.

The answer came in the form of rapid hoofbeats.

Clip-clop-clop-clop!

Gengar rode his Skiddo at full gallop, a look of sheer terror on both their faces.

Skiddo's horns allowed it to sense emotions—and Gengar's chaotic thoughts had overwhelmed the poor creature.

Visions of Johan becoming a ghost, Gengar conquering Lumiose's underworld, world domination, and ghostly pirate adventures had flooded Skiddo's mind.

Eyes wide with panic, it raced down the street, carrying Gengar further and further away.

"Ka-kah—!!"

The ghost cackled as the two vanished into the distance.

Johan & Vulpix: "..."

They exchanged a glance.

"Well," Johan said finally. "That's going to be a problem."

"Oowu~?"

"With Gengar? Nah, I'm worried about the Skiddo."

After much apologizing, Johan managed to calm the traumatized Skiddo with some energy cubes.

Its trembling eventually stopped, and it returned to the nearby Skiddo station.

"Sorry about that," Johan said sheepishly to its handler.

Then he shot Gengar a glare.

The ghost Pokémon scratched its head, mumbling excuses about the Skiddo's lack of mental fortitude.

Grabbing Gengar by the horns, Johan dragged him toward Sycamore's Lab.

Located on South Boulevard, the lab was an elegant building with white stone pillars and an inviting, open design.

As they approached, the automatic doors slid open.

"Mr. Johan?"

A boy in a white lab coat greeted them with a bright smile.

"Alain?" Johan asked.

"Yes! Welcome!" Alain said, bowing deeply. "Professor Sycamore asked me to wait for you."

"Thanks," Johan replied with a grin.

Inside, Sycamore's Lab was smaller than Oak's but no less impressive.

The glass walls of a long hallway gave a clear view of a simulated forest habitat, home to Pokémon like Floette, Diggersby, and Heliolisk.

Among them were numerous starter Pokémon from Kanto and Hoenn, brought here to support Sycamore's research on Mega Evolution.

Finally, they reached Sycamore's main lab.

The professor was examining data on a Garchomp when he turned and greeted them warmly.

"Johan! It's been a while."

"It has, Professor," Johan replied with a smile.

While Johan and Sycamore caught up, Gengar wandered over to the Garchomp.

"Ka-kah~~"

"Garchomp?"

Confused, the dragon retreated a step, unsure why the ghost was grinning so mischievously.

In the lab, Sycamore handed Johan a box.

Inside were a Mega Stone and a Key Stone.

"This is a Mega Stone for Gengar," Sycamore explained. "It's paired with this Key Stone. Please take them—it's my gift to you."

Johan was stunned.

A Mega Stone for Gengar?

But… it didn't look like the one he already had.

And the Key Stone was unmistakably genuine.

"Ka-kah?"

Gengar peeked through the wall, its eyes widening.

Had it just heard "Key Stone"?

# 237: Gengar, Mega Evolution!

Three chapters today due to patreon being 21 chapters ahead instead of 20.

------------

"Professor Sycamore, this is a bit much, don't you think? It's too valuable," Johan said hastily.

Mega Evolution, still in its early stages, was understood and utilized by only a handful of trainers. Even if it became widely known, both Mega Stones and Key Stones would remain prohibitively expensive.

Sycamore chuckled and explained, "It's not a gift, per se."

He continued, "I hope you can use this Key Stone and your Gengar's Mega Evolution to help me gather some data."

For years, Professor Sycamore had dedicated himself to the study of Mega Evolution. However, due to limited data and resources, much about the phenomenon remained a mystery.

He still lacked a comprehensive understanding of Mega Stones, Key Stones, and the bond between Pokémon and their trainers that facilitated Mega Evolution.

Even basic data on the types of Mega Stones and their compatibility with specific Pokémon was incomplete.

Johan stared at the shimmering crystal in the box, lost in thought.

He wasn't one to accept such a significant favor lightly. Mega Stones were incredibly costly, and even Key Stones—though more general in their usage—were no trivial matter.

Finally, after a brief silence, he reached into the box and picked up the Key Stone, saying, "If that's the case, I'll accept just the Key Stone for now."

Sycamore blinked in surprise. "Johan, the Key Stone needs to be used with a Mega Stone to—"

Johan smiled and cut him off. "Don't worry, Professor. I already have a Mega Stone."

He turned to Gengar, who grinned mischievously and extended its tongue. Nestled on the tip was a glittering stone it had been toying with.

"Bla-bla-bla," Gengar teased as it flicked the Mega Stone with its tongue.

Sycamore stared, dumbfounded.

"This is…"

"If I'm not mistaken," Johan said, "this is Gengar's Mega Stone. The one you analyzed might belong to a different Pokémon entirely."

Sycamore scratched his head in embarrassment. "Ah, I see... I must have made a mistake in my data analysis."

Still, he couldn't help but marvel. "To think you already had a Mega Stone for Gengar, Johan. That's remarkable."

Johan glanced at Gengar, who was happily tossing the Mega Stone into the air.

"It was... a lucky find," he admitted.

After all, the Mega Stone had been discovered near Gengar's ancient resting place—a fortunate coincidence.

Sycamore felt a twinge of guilt. He had invited Johan all the way to Lumiose City, only to misidentify a Mega Stone. Now, he couldn't even follow through on his promise of a Mega Stone.

After a moment's thought, Sycamore said, "How about this: you help me collect data on Mega Evolution for now, and in the future, if you need another Mega Stone, I'll make sure to get you one."

"Professor, there's no need for that. The Key Stone alone is already—" Johan began.

But Sycamore interrupted with a grin. "Actually, I have another favor to ask."

Johan paused, raising an eyebrow. "You're full of requests today, huh? Well, let's hear it."

He decided to accept the arrangement. After all, he didn't have other Pokémon ready for Mega Evolution at the moment.

Besides, he reasoned, the Key Stone was already a priceless gift.

Sycamore nodded approvingly. "Excellent. Then we're settled."

At last, Johan slipped the Key Stone into his pocket.

With this, Gengar's Mega Stone and Key Stone were finally complete!

[Key Stone: A stone imbued with the power of bonds. It is the key to unlocking the Mega Stone, enabling a Pokémon to Mega Evolve.]

As Sycamore prepared to discuss Mega Evolution in greater detail, Johan suddenly remembered something.

"Professor, actually, I have a favor to ask as well."

Sycamore, intrigued, turned to him.

"It's about a café I run back in Hoenn…"

Johan explained how his café was currently closed due to a staff shortage. Although the Rocket Trio was expected to return after their film project, he needed temporary employees to keep the business running smoothly.

Being in Lumiose City, a place with a thriving coffee culture, Johan hoped to find suitable candidates.

"I didn't know you were a fellow coffee lover," Sycamore said with a laugh. "I can definitely help. I know just where to look for the right employees."

He paused. "But if some of the staff are Pokémon...?"

Johan's eyes lit up. "That would be perfect! It might even make communication easier."

Sycamore chuckled and promised to help find employees for the café.

With that matter resolved, Sycamore's expression turned eager.

"Well then, Johan, shall we give Mega Evolution a try?"

The professor's enthusiasm was infectious. Johan hesitated for a moment but found himself nodding.

As he pondered the logistics, Johan felt a sudden chill.

Turning, he found Gengar staring at him with sparkling eyes, the Mega Stone glowing faintly in its mouth.

At the same time, the newly-acquired Key Stone in Johan's pocket began to emit a soft vibration.

Johan frowned. "Wait a second... Are we about to initiate Mega Evolution already?!"

The synchronization between him and Gengar was undeniable. A faint energy thread seemed to link them, shimmering with the power of their bond.

The bond was so strong it left Johan momentarily speechless.

"Isn't this a bit sudden?" Johan asked nervously.

"No better time than now," Sycamore said. "And I'll be your opponent."

Sycamore's partner Pokémon, a Garchomp, stood at his side. Although it wasn't as formidable as Cynthia's, it was still a capable battler.

"Let's do it!" Sycamore declared confidently.

Despite his reservations, Johan knew this was a unique opportunity.

"Alright," he sighed.

In the battle arena at the back of the lab, Johan and Sycamore stood at opposite ends of the field.

In the middle were their partners: Gengar and Garchomp.

Spectators included Sycamore's assistants Alain and Sophie, who were recording data, as well as a third guest: Gurkinn, the Gym Leader of Shalour City and a Mega Evolution mentor.

Gurkinn, arms crossed, observed Johan intently.

"Hmm… That energy… Is it Aura?"

While Gurkinn couldn't use Aura himself, his partner Lucario was an expert in its manipulation, allowing him to sense traces of it in Johan.

"Battle... start!" Alain raised his hand.

Sophie activated her instruments. "Recording Gengar's first Mega Evolution data… ready."

At the center of the arena, Johan and Gengar locked eyes.

The sounds of the outside world faded away, leaving only the synchronized thrum of their heartbeats.

Ba-dum. Ba-dum. Ba-dum.

"Are you ready, Gengar?" Johan asked softly.

"Ka-kah!!" Gengar clenched its fists, baring a feral grin.

A radiant energy surged between them, their bond manifesting in the air like a shimmering thread.

Johan raised his hand, the Key Stone glowing in response.

With a commanding voice, he shouted, "Gengar, Mega Evolve!"

# 239: Mega Gengar!

"Ka-kah!!"

Gengar let out a bone-chilling roar.

A brilliant white light erupted from the Key Stone in Johan's hand. Simultaneously, the Mega Stone on Gengar's tongue began to emit a dazzling spectrum of colors, cascading like waves to envelop and transform its form.

The battle arena, and even the room itself, seemed to tremble under the intensity of the energy. The radiant light filled the space, leaving the onlookers awestruck.

Within the blinding glow, Gengar's body underwent a dramatic transformation.

Its legs sank into the ground, seemingly merging with the shadows beneath it. The spikes on its back and ears elongated, becoming sharper and more menacing. Its arms rested on the ground, poised and powerful.

At the center of its forehead, a golden, eye-like emblem—or perhaps a symbol resembling a coin—slowly opened.

A dark crimson aura rose from below, painting its lower half in eerie hues.

Moments later, the pulsating energy of Mega Evolution coalesced into the iconic Mega Evolution symbol in front of Gengar before dispersing into a burst of brilliance.

Mega Gengar had arrived.

"Ka-kah!!"

An overwhelming presence filled the room, emanating waves of raw, ghostly energy.

Sycamore's face turned pale as he struggled to process the scene. Though he had witnessed Mega Evolution before—his friend Gurkinn had shown him the transformation of a Lucario—it had never been like this.

Lucario's Mega Evolution was calm and controlled, almost elegant in its intensity.

But this...

The sheer malevolence of Gengar's aura and the oppressive atmosphere it generated sent chills down his spine.

The arena seemed to quake under the weight of this transformation, leaving Sycamore unsure whether this was the result of Gengar's extraordinary bond with Johan or something even more profound.

Across the room, Alain and Sophie stood frozen, mouths agape.

Yet none were more shaken than Gurkinn, the seasoned Mega Evolution mentor himself.

As the only trainer besides Johan with experience in Mega Evolution, Gurkinn was well aware of the bond required for such a transformation.

But the raw magnitude of what he had just witnessed left him stunned.

"This bond... It's beyond extraordinary," Gurkinn murmured. "Given time, they might not even need the Mega Stone for this."

"B-Battle... start!"

Alain finally snapped out of his daze, his dry lips fumbling to announce the match.

He glanced at Johan and Mega Gengar, his eyes brimming with admiration and a hint of envy.

This... This is Mega Evolution?

When will I have my own Mega Evolution and become the strongest Mega Evolution trainer?

"Garchomp!" Sycamore called out to his partner.

The Dragon-and-Ground-type Pokémon froze.

It wasn't just the dazzling spectacle that left Garchomp shaken—it had felt the full force of Mega Gengar's oppressive presence.

This wasn't a friendly sparring match; this was pure intimidation.

"Dragon Rush!" Sycamore commanded, trying to rally his Pokémon.

"Gaaaaarrr!!"

Johan and Gengar, however, seemed to be operating on an entirely different wavelength.

Their connection had deepened during the Mega Evolution process, allowing them to communicate without words.

They could feel each other's thoughts and intentions as though they were one.

Johan was unaware that during this transformation, Gengar was burning through something deep within its core—a power both unfamiliar and thrilling.

"Ka-kah!!"

Gengar cackled maniacally, reveling in the incredible strength coursing through its form.

Johan spoke softly, his voice steady:

"Gengar, use Shadow Ball."

Despite its boundless excitement, Gengar followed Johan's command without hesitation.

It let out a low laugh, its hands lifting slightly as a massive, jet-black sphere began to form.

Though it was just a Shadow Ball, the energy it contained far surpassed any attack Gengar had unleashed before.

With a swift motion, Gengar launched the Shadow Ball.

BOOM!

The entire arena shook as the attack made contact. Glass shattered, the floor cracked, and a shockwave rippled outward, forcing Alain and the others to stumble.

Dust and debris filled the air as the room descended into chaos.

Gurkinn, recovering from the blast, muttered hoarsely, "This... this is the power of their bond."

He had always known that the strength of Mega Evolution was directly proportional to the bond between trainer and Pokémon. But this? This was unprecedented.

When the dust settled, Garchomp stood trembling in the center of the battlefield.

The Shadow Ball had missed, but the sheer force of its passage had left cracks in the arena floor.

A bead of sweat rolled down Garchomp's face as it realized:

I can't survive another one of those.

Johan exhaled deeply. "That's enough, Gengar."

The rainbow energy surrounding Mega Gengar dissipated, returning it to its original form.

"Ka-kah~~"

Gengar slumped forward, hands on its knees, panting heavily.

While its boastful exterior remained, it was clear that the transformation had taken a toll.

Reaching into its dimensional pocket, Gengar pulled out a Revive root and several poisonous mushrooms, stuffing them into its mouth with a dramatic flourish.

Johan himself felt the strain as well.

Though he had been training diligently, focusing on improving his Aura and physical stamina, the effort required for Mega Evolution left him drained.

"It's exhausting," Johan admitted to himself.

He glanced at Gengar, who was busy munching on its snacks, and chuckled. "How are you holding up?"

"Ka-kah~!"

Gengar waved a dismissive hand, feigning strength.

In the corner, Garchomp's claws trembled as it thanked the stars the battle was over.

It was alive... but barely.

The battle had ended, but the aftermath lingered.

Johan noticed that the spectacle had drawn the attention of his other Pokémon. Tinkaton, Dragonair, and Kubfu had all emerged from their Poké Balls, drawn by the immense energy of Mega Evolution.

Even Vulpix had come to watch, its eyes wide with admiration.

Though proud of Gengar's display, Johan couldn't help but sigh.

His Pokémon were clearly inspired, each determined to grow stronger.

But that also meant...

"Damn," Johan muttered. "My expenses are about to skyrocket."

Tinkaton and Kubfu immediately began training, flexing their muscles in a comical display of determination.

Even Dragonair and Vulpix joined in, mimicking their movements.

Gengar, ever the show-off, turned to Johan and puffed out its chest.

Look at this! I'm inspiring a culture of effort!

Johan rolled his eyes. "Sure you are."

As Johan considered the practicalities of Mega Evolution, he realized he needed a way to carry the Key Stone.

While Gengar could store its Mega Stone in its dimensional pocket, Johan needed a reliable and stylish way to keep the Key Stone on him at all times.

Many trainers used accessories like pendants, rings, or wristbands, but Johan wanted something unique.

"Maybe... a monocle?" he mused.

With the battle concluded, Sycamore and his assistants busied themselves analyzing the data.

Johan, meanwhile, wandered through the research facility with his Pokémon, enjoying the various exhibits and interacting with the other Pokémon on-site.

It wasn't long before Sycamore returned, his face alight with excitement.

"Johan, thank you! The data we collected today is invaluable."

Johan smiled. "Glad I could help."

"Oh, and about your café employees," Sycamore added, "I've asked Sophie to help scout potential candidates. Some of them might even be Pokémon."

Johan's eyes brightened. "That's perfect. Thanks, Professor."

# 240: The Coffee Manor

At the Lumiose City Pokémon Center, Johan and his team were greeted by the ever-energetic Audino.

"Dew-da~~!"

Audino gave Johan a sweet smile as he stepped inside.

Unlike other regions where Joys are typically accompanied by Chansey or Blissey, Kalos Pokémon Centers are usually supported by Wigglytuff or Audino.

This particular Center featured Audino, which is a Normal-type Pokémon with exceptional hearing capabilities.

Audino can detect even the faintest sounds and vibrations, making it an invaluable asset for medical and rescue work. Its ability to sense heartbeats allows it to monitor physical and emotional states with unparalleled precision.

"Ka-kah~~(^^)"

Gengar enthusiastically waved at the blushing pink Pokémon. Audino shyly tilted its head and returned an equally cheerful smile, its demeanor soft and warm.

"It seems to like you, Johan," Nurse Joy remarked with a chuckle.

Johan laughed. "It seems like everyone likes me a little too early."

By now, Johan was used to being recognized by the Joy family across regions. He had long suspected that they had a private group chat titled "Joy Worldwide."

As usual, after a brief conversation, Nurse Joy requested a signature and a photo.

"Gengar is in your hands, Nurse Joy," Johan said with a smile.

Although Gengar had insisted it felt fine after its first attempt at Mega Evolution, Johan wanted to ensure there were no adverse effects.

Moreover, staying in the Pokémon Center felt safer than staying at Sycamore's research facility—Johan had a hunch that Gengar might "redecorate" the lab's facilities overnight.

While Gengar was undergoing its check-up, Johan sat in the lounge, sipping on coffee brought by Audino.

"Thank you," Johan said, nodding in gratitude.

Nurse Joy approached and casually asked, "Johan, are you in Kalos to challenge the Kalos League?"

Johan blinked in surprise, a wry smile crossing his face. "No, I'm just here for the ice sculpture competition in Snowbelle City."

He wasn't like Ash, running off to each region to start anew, catching and training Pokémon from scratch.

"Oh, the Ice Sculpture Competition!"

Their conversation drifted to casual topics until a news broadcast caught Johan's attention.

On the television, Lysandre appeared, dressed in his signature black attire. Beside him stood a regal Pyroar as reporters swarmed him for interviews.

He declared, "The latest invention from Lysandre Labs, the Holo-Caster, will be available to the public soon."

The reporter conducting the interview was none other than Malva, one of Kalos's Elite Four and a news anchor known for her sharp wit.

"He's changed a lot," Nurse Joy observed. "But Lysandre is such a kind person."

She recounted how Lysandre had once provided aid to struggling communities and Pokémon in need, a kindness she had witnessed firsthand.

Kind, huh? Johan mused, choosing not to comment.

Lysandre's transformation wasn't just physical—his worldview had shifted dramatically.

Still, Joy's comment gave Johan an idea.

Lysandre's descent into extremism stemmed from the ingratitude of those he helped, people who exploited his generosity without remorse.

This disillusionment painted the world in a "survivor's bias," leading him to focus solely on its flaws.

But the truth was, countless others genuinely appreciated and respected him. Perhaps this angle could be a way to reason with Lysandre in the future.

"Johan," Nurse Joy's voice interrupted his thoughts, "Gengar's check-up is complete."

She handed over the report with a warm smile.

"Gengar is exceptionally well-trained. Its stats far exceed the standard benchmarks for its species. Truly remarkable."

"Ka-kah~!"

Gengar, now back at Johan's side, stood with its arms crossed, a smug grin plastered across its face.

However, Nurse Joy added, "That said, it's a bit drained from overexertion. I recommend at least a day of rest."

Johan sighed and gave Gengar a pointed look. "Hear that? Rest."

"Ka-kah…"

Gengar scratched the back of its head sheepishly, disappointed that it wouldn't be ruling Lumiose's "underworld" that night.

The Next Morning

Johan woke to the familiar sensation of Gengar licking his face—a routine that, surprisingly, he no longer found annoying.

Sitting up, he ruffled Vulpix's fur, earning a soft "Oowu~~" from the sleepy Pokémon as it pouted and tried to fix its disheveled coat.

Nearby, Kubfu snored softly, drooling as it dreamt of training. Even in its sleep, its little arms moved in a push-up motion.

"Dragonair," Johan called out as he headed to the bathroom. "Wake up Kubfu and get ready; we're heading out."

"Draaa~~"

A soft hum and a tiny jolt of electricity later, Kubfu startled awake, its nose bubble popping with a loud "plop!"

As the team assembled, Tinkaton proudly presented Johan with a gleaming gold monocle.

"You made this?" Johan asked, impressed.

"Cha~~ Cha!"

The monocle wasn't just stylish; it featured a fine gold chain with a slot at the end designed to hold the Key Stone securely. It was functional and sophisticated—a perfect blend of utility and charm.

Dressed in a black suit, monocle in place, Johan's appearance blended seamlessly with Kalos's romantic atmosphere.

When he caught a glimpse of himself in the mirror, he muttered, "Looks like I'm one trench coat away from being a classic villain."

After breakfast at the Pokémon Center, Johan strolled into a nearby café to meet Sycamore.

When the professor saw Johan, he paused for a moment before laughing.

"That look suits you, Johan."

The two shared a cup of coffee before heading toward their destination: The Coffee Manor.

The Coffee Manor

Located in eastern Lumiose City, the Coffee Manor was an old estate with a storied history.

Sycamore explained, "This manor is run by an elderly couple who take in stray or abandoned Pokémon. Over the years, it's become a haven, akin to a daycare, but with a twist."

The Pokémon here weren't trained for battles. Instead, they were taught life skills: cooking, cleaning, brewing coffee, and even gardening.

Many Kalos cafés sourced their staff from this manor, giving the Pokémon fulfilling roles outside of combat.

"I recruited several of my own Pokémon helpers from here," Sycamore added.

Soon, their taxi pulled up to the grand gates of the Coffee Manor.

Standing at the entrance was an Indeedee, bowing politely as if it had been expecting them.

"Dee-dee~~"

# 241: Hiring New Staff

The Indeedee standing at the gate had upward-facing horns, its purple and black coloration giving it an air of elegance. Its half-open eyes exuded a blend of cuteness and seriousness. Clearly, this was a male Indeedee.

Known as the "Emotion Pokémon," Indeedee thrives on positive emotions, often lending its abilities to assist humans and Pokémon alike. In the Galar region, they're typically partnered with Nurse Joy in Pokémon Centers, though they can be found in other regions too.

"Thanks for helping us, Indeedee," Professor Sycamore said with a smile.

"Dee-dee~~"

The Indeedee responded with a polite bow, folding its hands neatly as it guided Johan and Sycamore into the estate.

As they passed through the gates into the front yard, Johan couldn't help but marvel at the scenery.

Even in late autumn, the estate remained lush with greenery. White cobblestone paths stretched between perfectly symmetrical rows of trees and bushes, with not a single stray leaf on the ground.

Hidden among the flowers and shrubs, Johan spotted several Pokémon peeking out shyly to observe the visitors—Scatterbug, Vivillon, Flabébé, and Aromatisse all curiously eyed the newcomers.

Johan adjusted his monocle and shot a warning glance at the shadow where his Gengar was lurking.

Behave yourself.

Although mischievous by nature, Gengar understood Johan's expectations. It only peeked out slightly, greeting the Pokémon with cheerful waves.

The Indeedee at the lead lightly twitched its horns, as if sensing the emotional states of Johan, his Vulpix, and Gengar. Whatever it perceived made its expression soften noticeably.

Passing a central fountain, several Ducklett hurriedly waddled into the water with nervous splashes.

"This estate rivals Steven's mansion," Johan murmured to himself.

Professor Sycamore chuckled, noticing Johan's reaction.

"It's impressive, isn't it? I was equally stunned my first time here. But don't worry—the owners are incredibly kind."

Johan nodded. The sheer size and upkeep of the estate suggested considerable wealth, yet the couple who owned it seemed to dedicate their resources selflessly to caring for Pokémon.

Indeedee led them past the main building and into the backyard, where Johan spotted an elderly man tilling the soil and an older woman knitting peacefully on a chair.

"Dee-dee~~"

The Indeedee called out softly, catching the attention of the couple.

"You're here," the man said warmly, wiping sweat from his brow.

"Mr. Coffee, Mrs. Coffee," Sycamore greeted them with familiarity, smiling.

Few people knew the couple's actual names; even Sycamore addressed them as Mr. and Mrs. Coffee. The nickname, derived from their shared passion for coffee cultivation, suited them well.

"Welcome," Mrs. Coffee said with a gentle smile.

"This is Johan, a teacher and café owner," Sycamore introduced.

Johan bowed slightly in greeting. "Mr. and Mrs. Coffee, it's a pleasure to meet you. I run a small café alongside my teaching work, but due to my schedule, I've been struggling to manage both..."

"Sit, sit," Mrs. Coffee interrupted kindly. "There's no need to stand on formality."

Another Indeedee approached, carrying trays with two cups of coffee and small plates of cake.

This Indeedee, a female with downward-facing horns, had a calm demeanor and a soft, comforting smile.

"Dee-dee~~"

As Johan and Sycamore sat, Vulpix sniffed curiously at the two Indeedees.

"Oowu?"

Though they looked similar, Vulpix seemed fascinated by their subtle differences.

"Ka-kah~~"

Gengar popped out from Johan's shadow, waving amicably. Today, it was unusually well-behaved—a fact that didn't escape Johan's notice.

Receiving permission from the Coffee couple, Johan released the rest of his Pokémon from their Poké Balls.

"Feel free to play," Johan said, adding a quick reminder, "but be on your best behavior."

The female Indeedee brought snacks for the Pokémon, who quickly warmed up to her.

A Taste of Perfection

"Try the coffee," Mr. Coffee said, gesturing to their cups.

Johan and Sycamore exchanged a glance before lifting their mugs.

The rich aroma hit Johan first, a blend of chocolate bitterness with a hint of citrusy acidity. The flavor was smooth and velvety, with a lingering sweetness that bloomed on the palate.

"Delicious," Sycamore said, smacking his lips appreciatively.

"It's... the best coffee I've ever had," Johan admitted.

Mr. Coffee beamed. "We grow and roast the beans ourselves. I'm glad you enjoy it."

Johan's thoughts wandered—perhaps he could source beans directly from the Coffee couple for his café.

"Try the cake, too," Mrs. Coffee encouraged.

Johan's plate held a slice of chocolate mousse cake, while Sycamore had a slice of cheesecake.

Both looked exquisite, and the taste did not disappoint.

"Did the Indeedees make these?" Sycamore asked curiously.

"Not these two," Mr. Coffee replied, smiling mysteriously.

Johan followed their gazes toward a window, where two more Indeedees peeked out—a bold older sister and a shy younger brother.

"There are four Indeedees here?" Johan murmured in surprise.

Mrs. Coffee explained that the siblings were rescued during a trip to Galar, where they had been bullied by a group of Thievul and Nickit.

The siblings were brought back to Kalos, where they flourished under the Coffee couple's care.

"The brother prepares coffee, and the sister bakes," Mr. Coffee said proudly.

Johan was impressed by their story and their skills.

"What do you think of them, Johan?" Mrs. Coffee asked.

Johan hesitated, glancing at Sycamore. Weren't the Coffee couple supposed to test him first?

Seeing his confusion, Mrs. Coffee laughed. "Sycamore speaks highly of you, and the Indeedees already trust you. Their instincts never lie."

Indeedee, known as the Emotion Pokémon, could sense and evaluate a person's character with remarkable accuracy.

Still, Johan wanted to ask the siblings directly.

"It's important to me that they want to join willingly," he said.

The Coffee couple exchanged approving glances.

"Good. But there's one thing you should know," Mr. Coffee added.

"These two cannot be captured in Poké Balls—they were likely stolen from poachers and escaped before being sold."

Hearing this, Johan's expression darkened.

Inside, Johan approached the Indeedees.

"Dee-dee~~" the sister greeted warmly.

"Dee... dee..." the brother peeked out shyly.

Johan smiled gently, extending a hand with energy cubes.

The sister took one immediately, savoring it with delight.

The brother hesitated, eyeing Johan warily, until his sister grabbed another cube and stuffed it into his mouth.

"Tasty, right?" Johan said with a grin.

The brother's cheeks reddened as he nodded.

"Would you like to help at my café?" Johan asked.

The sister nodded enthusiastically, and though the brother was hesitant, he eventually agreed as well.

Their skill sets surprised Johan further—the sister's hidden ability, Psychic Surge, created a Psychic Terrain, while the brother's Synchronize trait allowed him to share status conditions.

Such abilities would be invaluable for both café work and Johan's Pokémon therapy services.

With the siblings officially on board, Johan promised to return for them after his trip to Snowbelle City.

As he and Sycamore left the estate, the Coffee couple watched fondly.

"They made a good choice," Mrs. Coffee said.

"I hear Johan is quite the trainer," Mr. Coffee added.

The sister clenched her tiny fists determinedly.

"Dee-dee!"

She levitated fallen leaves and branches into a tidy pile, a testament to her impressive psychic power.

Her brother, watching nervously, marveled at his sister's strength.

"Dee..."
