# 242: Become a Master of Insider Secrets?

The next day, Johan, Alain, and Professor Sycamore set off for Snowbelle City.

Originally, Johan had planned to go alone, but Professor Sycamore suggested they travel together, citing the Ice Sculpture Competition as an event worth witnessing. Additionally, Sycamore wanted to collect more data on Gengar during the trip for his research.

Naturally, Alain joined as the professor's assistant.

"Off to Snowbelle City!" Professor Sycamore exclaimed, sliding into the driver's seat of the jeep and donning a pair of stylish sunglasses.

"Ka-kah!"

Not to be outdone, Gengar pulled out a pair of sunglasses from its dimensional pocket and put them on. The two exchanged a glance before bursting into simultaneous laughter.

Gengar took the passenger seat, while Johan sat in the back with Vulpix, accompanied by Alain.

The jeep smoothly rolled out of Lumiose City.

Having Professor Sycamore as the driver? That was a rarity the Pokémon world wouldn't often see.

"Johan, here's the information about the Ice Sculpture Competition," Alain said enthusiastically, passing over a tablet. "It includes the event details, the list of contestants selected by the organizers, and profiles of the judges."

Johan accepted it with a puzzled expression. "Contestant selection?"

Alain explained with a smile, "The organizers screened participants based on their skills, achievements, and reputation. Naturally, Johan, you wouldn't need to worry about being eliminated, so you might not have noticed."

"Ah, that makes sense."

Alain continued, "I've identified a few contestants who are likely to be your strongest competition for the championship."

"Championship?" Johan raised an eyebrow.

Although he had confidence in Vulpix, aiming for the championship wasn't his main goal. He was here primarily because Vulpix wanted to participate, and he simply wanted to let it try. Winning wasn't a necessity; there would be prizes regardless of placement.

However, as Johan reviewed the judges for the Ice Sculpture Competition, he found himself raising an eyebrow in amusement.

The judging panel consisted of Nurse Joy, Officer Jenny, Siebold of the Kalos Elite Four, Wolfric, the Snowbelle City Gym Leader, and Adam, the mentor of Wallace, the Sootopolis Gym Leader.

With these five making up the panel, Johan couldn't help but think, Why does this judging panel feel oddly familiar?

He muttered under his breath, "I wonder if the panel could be... bribed?"

"Hmm?" Alain tilted his head.

"Nothing," Johan replied quickly, shaking his head.

Scrolling through the contestant list, Johan spotted several familiar names.

Lorelei stood out at the top of the list. Known as the "Ice Queen," she was destined to become Kanto's Ice-type Elite Four member. She was undoubtedly a strong contender for the championship.

Further down, there was Candice, the future Gym Leader of Snowpoint City in Sinnoh.

From Galar, there was Nessa...

Wait, Nessa?

Johan's mind conjured an image of a tall, striking woman with a deep tan and a fit, athletic physique. She was Sonia's close friend, a rising model, and would one day be known as the "Tidal Beauty" and the Gym Leader of Hulbury. It seemed her affinity for Water-types extended naturally to Ice-types.

Finally, Johan saw another familiar name from the distant Paldea region: Grusha.

Grusha, the future Gym Leader of Glaseado Gym, was a skilled Ice-type specialist. Johan recalled being surprised when he first encountered Grusha, having initially mistaken him for a girl due to his delicate features.

Clearing his throat, Johan refocused. The contestant lineup was truly stellar. Between future Elite Four members and Gym Leaders, the competition promised to be intense.

"Thank you," Johan said, handing the tablet back to Alain.

Alain's attention to detail was impressive, which made it all the more regrettable that he would one day be manipulated by Lysandre.

"You're welcome, Johan," Alain said with a slight blush.

"Johan, do you think you'll win?" Sycamore asked from the front seat, turning around briefly to glance at him.

Johan laughed softly, stroking Vulpix's fur.

"Oowu!" Vulpix straightened confidently, its eyes gleaming with determination.

Seeing Vulpix's resolve, Johan replied, "If we give it our all, we might have a small chance."

But then Johan froze, realizing something unusual. Sycamore was sitting in the passenger seat.

"Wait... who's driving?!"

"Ka-kah!" Gengar grinned at him from the rearview mirror, hands firmly on the wheel.

"Gengar's driving?!" Johan's voice rose in disbelief.

Sycamore chuckled. "Gengar said it's great at driving, so I let it give it a try. Honestly, it's doing a fantastic job."

And to Johan's surprise, the jeep was indeed cruising smoothly.

"Let's just hope we reach Snowbelle City without any surprises," Johan muttered.

When they finally arrived in Snowbelle City without incident, Johan couldn't help but marvel. "Looks like our little Gengar is all grown up."

Snowbelle City, located in the northeastern corner of Kalos, was a snow-covered wonderland. The competition had transformed the town into a lively hub, brimming with tourists and vendors.

The event itself was scheduled to begin at sunset on a scenic hill overlooking the city. Local lore spoke of how the setting sun cast a golden glow over the snowy landscape, creating a mesmerizing rainbow effect.

Upon checking in at the competition venue, Johan was surprised to encounter Lorelei, seemingly waiting for him.

"Johan," she greeted warmly, accompanied by her Jynx.

Johan stiffened slightly at the sight of Jynx. Wasn't that Lorelei's overly confident Smoochum?

"Lu-lu-lu!" Jynx cooed, waddling over to Vulpix with hearts in its eyes.

"Oowu?!" Vulpix recoiled, startled by Jynx's sudden enthusiasm.

Seeing Vulpix's discomfort, Johan muttered, "It's Lorelei's Smoochum. You've met before."

"Oowu?!"

Realizing the connection, Vulpix froze in disbelief. While it recognized traces of Smoochum in Jynx's features, the transformation was... jarring. Any thoughts Vulpix had about evolving into a Ninetales were promptly shelved.

Fortunately, Jynx's affection stemmed from admiration, as Vulpix had always been a role model for it.

"I see Jynx's attitude has improved," Johan noted.

Lorelei nodded. "Thanks to you. Your advice to gather information on Jynx's cultural significance in Galar really helped."

Johan felt a sense of pride. Helping Pokémon overcome their challenges was always rewarding.

At that moment, a smooth, magnetic voice interrupted, "You must be Johan."

# 243: Ice Sculpture Competition!

A man wearing a black leather jacket, his hair resembling a lion's mane, and a kind smile approached.

"Lysandre, the representative."

It was Lysandre, leader of Team Flare and a representative of the Lysandre Labs.

Everyone present knew him, especially Professor Sycamore, who shared a seemingly friendly relationship with him.

The crowd greeted him, and Lysandre responded with a polite smile, his gaze lingering a little longer on Johan and Alain.

However, he didn't stay long. As one of the sponsors of the competition, he was busy. Before leaving, he invited Johan and the others to have coffee after the event.

Johan initially considered declining, but recalling Diantha's earlier request, he decided to accept.

Afterward, Lorelei suggested they check out the venue. Johan agreed, and the two entered the contestant area while Sycamore and Alain went to greet others they knew.

The competition was held on a snowy mountainside platform. The arena, which seemed to be a battle stage, was repurposed for the event. Three sides were surrounded by spectator stands, while the backdrop was a towering snow-covered mountain.

At the center of the arena stood numerous identical stone pedestals, roughly one meter tall, each serving as a workstation for a contestant and their Pokémon to create their ice sculpture.

On one side of the arena, a team of photographers was busy filming. A bronze-skinned girl stood near one pedestal, appearing distracted.

"Would you like to take a break, Nessa?" one of the photographers suggested, noticing her lack of focus.

"Oh, sure. I'm sorry," Nessa replied, bowing apologetically to the crew before jogging away.

Her path inadvertently brought her near Johan and Lorelei.

As she approached, she realized she might be intruding. Just as she was about to apologize, her light blue eyes lit up.

"Are you… Johan?"

Seeing Nessa dressed so lightly in the cold, Johan resisted the urge to ask, Aren't you cold? Instead, he smiled and greeted her, "Hello, Nessa."

"Oowu~!" Vulpix chirped with a wag of its tail.

"You know me?" Nessa asked, surprised.

"I've heard about you from Sonia," Johan replied casually.

Sonia and Nessa were close friends, and Nessa nodded in understanding.

She also realized Johan likely knew more about her than others did. For example, she knew from Sonia that Johan had defeated Mustard, the former Champion of Galar, who had remained undefeated for 18 years.

She also knew Sonia planned to attend school in Hoenn next year because of Johan.

"Is something wrong? You seem distracted," Johan asked politely.

Nessa hesitated, unsure how to begin, but remembering that Sonia had described Johan as an excellent psychologist, she decided to confide in him.

Lorelei, noticing Nessa's hesitation, suggested, "Why don't we talk over some coffee?"

The contestants had a dedicated lounge area stocked with coffee, a must-have in Kalos.

Once in the lounge, Lorelei excused herself to get coffee, leaving Nessa with space to speak.

Nessa explained her situation. Recently, she had lost a battle against what she considered her rival, Milo.

The loss itself was frustrating enough, but Rose, the Galar Pokémon League Chairman, had confronted her afterward. He told her she needed to choose between being a Gym Leader or a model, as balancing both roles might prevent her from achieving her full potential in either.

Being a Gym Leader was still new to her, and she hadn't yet fully adapted to the role.

Johan silently noted the timing. So this is during the events of "Twilight Wings."

He remembered that Nessa later vented her frustrations to Sonia, which is likely how she first heard of him.

"What do you think?" Johan asked gently.

"I…" Nessa hesitated. If she had the answer, she wouldn't have come to Kalos for a distraction.

"I don't know."

"No, I think you do," Johan replied, his voice calm and reassuring.

Nessa looked up, confused.

When their eyes met, she felt as though Johan could see directly into her soul, and it made her nervous.

Johan smiled again and said, "I had a student once. He's about your age and faced a similar, if not greater, dilemma."

Nessa's curiosity was piqued.

Johan stroked Vulpix's fur, gazing out the window as he continued. "He's a skilled Pokémon Trainer, but also the heir to a major company. His personal passion, though, is collecting stones."

Of course, Johan was talking about Steven.

"He once asked me what he should do."

"What did you tell him?" Nessa asked eagerly.

"I asked him to imagine giving up one or even both pursuits. Could he accept such an outcome?"

Johan turned to Nessa and smiled. "Ask yourself, Nessa. Could you give up being a model?"

The thought made Nessa instinctively shake her head.

"And what about being a Gym Leader?"

She shook her head again. Her goal was to become the strongest Gym Leader.

"See? You already have your answer," Johan said with a shrug.

Nessa paused, processing his words.

"Or do you think you're not capable of excelling at both?" Johan asked, raising an eyebrow.

Am I capable?

"I'm just an ordinary person. Can I really do it?" Nessa murmured.

"Everyone's just an ordinary person."

With a flash of light, one of Johan's Poké Balls opened.

Kubfu appeared, clenching its tiny fists in determination. "Ku~!"

Its expression seemed to say, Why not me? If others can, I can too!

"Even you're giving motivational speeches now?" Johan muttered, glancing at Kubfu.

Nessa couldn't help but laugh. "It's adorable."

Johan sighed, cradling Vulpix in one arm while lifting Kubfu by the scruff of its neck with the other. He carried them both out of the lounge, giving Nessa some space to think.

As he left, he offered her one final piece of advice.

"Talk to your Pokémon. After all, you're in this together."

Nessa didn't know how long she sat there, staring at her reflection in her coffee. Eventually, she let out a small sigh.

By evening, the competition was ready to begin.

Vulpix rested on Johan's lap while Gengar and Kubfu worked together to give it a final "massage."

"Ka-kah~! (Just have fun out there. Tonight, we'll conquer the world!)" Gengar cheered.

"Ku~! (Strike fast, strike true!)" Kubfu added.

Johan could only shake his head at their antics.

Vulpix, however, appreciated their encouragement.

When the time came, the participants entered the arena.

Johan stood in line behind Nessa, who seemed less troubled than before, though not entirely at peace.

"Johan," Nessa said earnestly, "after the competition, can we have a battle?"

Before Johan could reply, the host's booming voice announced her entrance, and she stepped into the arena to thunderous applause.

"Next, we welcome Johan from Rustboro City in the Hoenn region!"

Carrying Vulpix, Johan entered the arena to enthusiastic cheers.

Though startled by the attention, Vulpix took a deep breath and climbed onto Johan's shoulder. Its small voice called out to the crowd:

"Oowu~~!"

The competition began with a flourish. Together, Johan and Vulpix worked to carve their sculpture. Slowly but surely, an image emerged: Johan surrounded by his Pokémon—Vulpix in his arms, Gengar posing playfully, Dragonair soaring gracefully, Tinkaton wielding its mighty hammer, and Kubfu sleeping peacefully.

In the sunset's light, the transparent ice sculpture reflected vibrant colors, bringing their creation to life.

Above the arena, snowflakes began to fall.

# 244: Articuno!

"Articuno?!"

A gasp came from the audience.

"It's really Articuno!"

"The legendary Pokémon, Articuno!"

To have an Articuno appear during the Ice Sculpture Competition was unprecedented in the event's history.

The crowd stood up collectively, gazing at the majestic figure gliding through the air, its movements summoning a light snowfall.

Even the contestants stopped what they were doing to marvel at the sacred silhouette bathed in the golden hues of sunset.

Lorelei's eyes sparkled as she admired the beauty of Articuno.

Nessa, too, watched the legendary Pokémon in awe, feeling her worries dissolve like melting snow.

"Oowu~~"

Vulpix tilted its head upward, its light blue eyes shimmering with wonder.

So beautiful…

"Don't let it distract you," Johan reminded Vulpix.

"It's just Articuno."

The crowd was stunned by his casual tone.

Articuno, after all, is a mythical Pokémon.

But Johan had seen Ho-Oh before—and that was while it carried a camera crew.

Back then, even his Tinkaton nearly brought it down.

Sure, Articuno was a legendary Pokémon, but members of its species weren't exactly unique. The one above them was likely just an ordinary Articuno, not one of the Legendary Trio.

And besides, Johan thought, after seeing the Galarian Birds… this one doesn't even compare to that ridiculous walking chicken.

Vulpix nodded obediently and redirected its focus back to their sculpture.

Johan, however, noticed something unusual. He reached into his pocket and felt the Rainbow Wing. The feather, which usually glimmered with vibrant hues, had grown dim.

"It's just Articuno, not Ho-Oh. What are you getting worked up over?" Johan muttered under his breath, infusing his aura into the feather.

The Rainbow Wing brightened immediately, returning to its resplendent state.

"Caw?"

The Articuno above seemed to notice something. It turned mid-flight and soared back toward the arena.

"It's coming back!"

"Is it looking for something?"

"A feather? Could it be one of its own feathers?"

Articuno surveyed the arena with a swift glance before locking its gaze on Johan and Vulpix.

Under the astonished eyes of the audience, contestants, and judges, a snow-white feather floated gently from the sky, spiraling down to Johan and Vulpix.

"Oowu?"

Vulpix blinked, wrapping its six tails around the feather.

As an Ice-type Pokémon, Vulpix felt an unexpected chill emanating from the feather.

Johan was taken aback. Was this feather drawn here by the Rainbow Wing?

The Articuno's feather wasn't as fussy as the Rainbow Wing—it emitted a faint, soft glow. Its cold energy spread outward, but Vulpix quickly tucked it into the thick fur of its tail, suppressing the chill.

The feather seemed to invigorate Vulpix as it shivered slightly, looking refreshed.

"Caw—!"

Satisfied, Articuno nodded and flapped its wings powerfully, disappearing into the horizon.

The audience erupted into chatter.

"Did Articuno choose them?"

"It's a sign of recognition!"

"That feather must be a blessing from Articuno!"

As the spectators marveled, some began to notice the nearly finished ice sculpture crafted by Johan and Vulpix.

"It's incredible!"

"That looks just like them—it's so lifelike!"

The ice sculpture depicted Johan and his team in stunning detail. Every Pokémon, from Gengar's mischievous pose to Tinkaton's steadfast strength, looked alive. Even the smallest nuances of their personalities were perfectly captured.

"It's like they're real!"

"Articuno must have been admiring this sculpture!"

Johan and Vulpix exchanged glances.

"So, do we still need to pull any strings?" Johan joked.

Well, in a way, they had already pulled a "string"—the Rainbow Wing.

The feather in Johan's pocket seemed to shimmer faintly as if acknowledging the thought.

Vulpix, however, appeared a bit conflicted. It wasn't sure if the attention was truly for their efforts.

Was their hard work being overshadowed by a single feather?

Johan noticed Vulpix's unease. Carefully picking it up, he reassured it, "No, this is just Articuno showing us recognition. The judges won't let a feather sway their judgment."

"Oowu?" Really?

"Of course," Johan smiled.

"This panel includes an Elite Four member. Do you think Articuno's presence would intimidate someone like Siebold?"

Johan added with a smirk, "And there's also Wallace's mentor, Adam. He's no pushover either."

"Besides," Johan said, giving Vulpix a confident look, "have you considered that maybe our sculpture was already the best?"

Vulpix's eyes lit up. It had put in countless hours of practice, sculpting snowmen every day. With its innate talent and dedication, there was no reason to doubt its abilities.

The judges unanimously agreed: Johan and Vulpix's sculpture was the best.

Articuno's appearance may have brought attention, but the artwork's quality was undeniable.

Other contestants had created extraordinary pieces:

Lorelei and her Jynx sculpted a Lapras.Wulfric and his Walrein shaped an Altaria.Nessa and her Pelipper created a Milotic.Candice and her Sneasel depicted an Abomasnow.

However, the complexity and detail of Johan and Vulpix's piece—featuring a Trainer and five Pokémon—set it apart.

Johan and Vulpix were awarded first place, receiving a "Never-Melt Ice" and a large batch of Aspear Berries sponsored by Lysandre Labs.

But for Johan, the trophy was secondary.

The real reward was Vulpix's newfound confidence. The little Pokémon finally realized it wasn't just any Ice-type—it was extraordinary.

Second and third place went to Lorelei and Wulfric, respectively.

Although Nessa didn't make the top three, her experience—from meeting Johan to seeing Articuno—helped her find clarity in her goals.

"Congratulations, Johan. And Vulpix, too," Siebold said as he handed over the prizes.

"Thank you."

"Your sculpture was the most remarkable I've ever seen!" Siebold added warmly.

"Oowu~!"

Vulpix puffed out its chest proudly.

Articuno's appearance turned the Ice Sculpture Competition into a legendary event, becoming a hot topic throughout Snowbelle City and the Kalos region.

Afterward, Johan joined Lysandre for a coffee meeting.

That night, Johan and Lysandre sat in a quiet café. The snow-covered streets of Snowbelle City glistened under the moonlight.

The two exchanged pleasantries but refrained from diving straight into their agendas.

Johan knew Lysandre's ideals well, thanks to his memories of another life. Similarly, Lysandre had researched Johan extensively through Diantha and Sycamore.

After a prolonged silence, Lysandre finally spoke.

"May I call you Johan?"

"Of course," Johan replied.

"What do you think of this world?"

Johan stroked Vulpix's fur as it purred contentedly.

"Beautiful, mysterious, and full of life," he answered.

Lysandre's eyes narrowed slightly.

"Of course," Johan continued, "there are imperfections, too. Things that could be better."

This last remark struck a chord with Lysandre, who leaned forward, his tone more earnest.

"And if I told you there was a way to create a new, pure world—one without flaws?"

"Not an easy feat," Johan replied.

"But if it were possible?"

Johan smiled faintly. "Then why not try?"

This response pleased Lysandre, though Johan knew better.

Lysandre wasn't someone easily swayed by a single conversation.

Still, Johan decided to play along, using the opportunity to subtly steer the discussion.

As they parted ways, Lysandre gifted Johan a TM for Dark Pulse.

Standing outside the café, Johan stared at the TM in his hand, musing aloud, "Maybe Giovanni would be better suited to deal with someone like Lysandre…"

After all, I'm too much of a nice guy for this.

# 245: A Snowy Night Brawl and the First Hints of the Cocoon of Destruction

The biting cold wind blew under a velvet-black sky as thick clouds obscured most of the moonlight. In the dim glow of the night, a shadow darted swiftly through the forest, heading straight for the Pokémon Center in Snowbelle City.

Inside a cozy, heated room...

"Ka-kah~~"

The mischievous Gengar leaned over Johan's sleeping figure and unceremoniously ran its tongue across his face.

"Mmph!"

The familiar numbing sensation jolted Johan awake. He instinctively reached for the bedside table, rubbing his face groggily.

"Ka-kah~~"

Gengar, ever the prankster, pulled a Cheri Berry out of its mouth and plopped it into Johan's hand.

Crunch!

Still half-asleep, Johan sat up, squinting at his phone.

1:30 a.m.

"Gengar... what now? It's the middle of the night—"

"Ka-kah! Ka-kah!"

But Gengar didn't waste time explaining. Instead, it tugged insistently at Johan's arm, urging him to get up.

Recognizing the urgency in Gengar's actions, Johan quickly shook off his drowsiness. Although Gengar was often a troublemaker, it knew its limits. If it was waking him up like this, something serious must have happened.

Johan glanced around the room.

Aside from Vulpix curled up on his arm, none of his Pokémon were anywhere in sight.

"Something happened?" Johan asked, a hint of tension creeping into his voice.

"Ka-kah!"

Gengar nodded emphatically.

Johan sprang into action, swiftly throwing on his clothes and scooping up the still-sleepy Vulpix. He followed Gengar, leaping out the window and landing in the snow.

The icy air hit him like a slap to the face, instantly clearing the last remnants of sleep from his mind.

"Oowu?"

Still half-asleep, Vulpix opened its eyes and looked around in confusion. It quickly realized they were now in a snowy forest.

Gengar floated ahead, motioning for Johan to follow.

As they ran, Gengar explained the situation.

Earlier, it had taken the rest of the team out for their usual nightly "meet-and-greet," a playful way of asking local wild Pokémon for "gifts." Everything was going fine until they stumbled across something unusual—a group of poachers, taking advantage of the recent influx of people in Snowbelle City, had set their sights on a herd of Abomasnow.

When Johan arrived at the scene, he found his other Pokémon hiding in a thicket, observing the situation in a nearby valley.

The rustling of trees and occasional grunts echoed faintly through the forest.

Tinkaton clutched its hammer tightly, glancing at Johan with determination.

"Cha!"

But Johan held up a hand, signaling for it to wait.

Abomasnow, known as the "Ice Monster," typically lived reclusively in snow-covered mountains, rarely venturing into human territory. Their peaceful existence made them prime targets for greedy poachers.

Johan guessed the recent snowfall, brought on by Articuno's earlier appearance, had lured the Abomasnow out of hiding, making them vulnerable.

Johan closed his eyes, focusing his aura.

Aura... expand!

A wave of energy rippled outward, revealing the presence of several Abomasnow facing off against two poachers. The situation was tense; the poachers held a small Snover captive, using it as leverage to keep the larger Abomasnow at bay.

Johan breathed a quiet sigh of relief.

This wasn't an overwhelming force—it was just two poachers with their Pokémon: a Heatmor and a Bisharp.

"This might actually be manageable," Johan muttered.

He quickly devised a plan and signaled his team to move into position.

"You'd better be careful with that Snover," said the taller of the two poachers.

"We need it alive to sell, and we don't want these Abomasnow going berserk."

"Relax," the shorter one replied, tightening his grip on the struggling Snover.

"Let's just capture the rest of them before they get any ideas."

Before the poachers could act, a sharp crack echoed through the forest as Kubfu slammed its fist into a nearby tree, snapping it clean in half.

"Who's there?!"

Both poachers spun around, momentarily distracted.

In that instant, Gengar materialized behind the shorter poacher, its menacing grin glowing faintly in the dark.

"Ka-kah!"

The poacher froze, terror coursing through his veins as a large boulder shot out of the bushes, slamming into Bisharp and knocking it backward.

At the same time, Dragonair unleashed a torrent of water, sweeping Heatmor off its feet and dousing its flames.

"Oowu!!"

Vulpix fired an Ice Beam, freezing the poacher and the captive Snover in a block of ice.

The taller poacher barely had time to react before Johan appeared, landing a solid punch to his jaw.

Thud!

The poacher crumpled to the ground as Johan flexed his fist.

"All that martial arts practice finally paid off," he muttered with a grin.

The defeated poacher groaned, reaching for another Poké Ball, but Gengar's leering face appeared before him.

"Ka-kah!"

The poacher's eyes rolled back, and he collapsed in a heap.

With the immediate threat neutralized, Johan released the frozen Snover and handed it a few Pokéblocks to calm it down. The Abomasnow herd quickly gathered around, their leader embracing the rescued Snover.

"Go on," Johan said gently, watching as Snover nuzzled its mother.

The Abomasnow leader stepped forward, bowing slightly in gratitude before retreating into the snowy woods with its herd.

Before leaving, the Abomasnow left behind several gifts: berries, small chunks of ice, and crystalline snowflakes.

Johan examined the icy crystals, recognizing them as fragments of Everstone—rare materials often used for evolution.

"They're such genuine creatures," Johan murmured, watching the Abomasnow disappear into the forest.

"Poachers like these don't deserve to exist."

Not long after, Officer Jenny arrived on a snowmobile, flanked by a small team of officers. To Johan's surprise, she wasn't alone.

"Johan, meet Looker," she said, introducing the weary-looking man beside her.

"Hello, Johan. I'm Looker, with the International Police."

Johan raised an eyebrow, smirking slightly. "Nice to meet you. I'm also a 'looker.'"

The detective froze for a moment before stammering, "Er, I mean—that's my codename!"

Despite the awkward exchange, Johan was well aware of Looker's reputation. Known for his relentless pursuit of justice, Looker had dismantled numerous criminal organizations, including remnants of Team Galactic and the Seven Sages of Team Plasma.

"I've been tracking these poachers for a while," Looker explained, glancing at the unconscious men.

"They're part of Argus Steel's crew—the airship thieves."

Argus Steel?

The name jogged Johan's memory.

Wasn't that the father-daughter duo from Diancie and the Cocoon of Destruction?

Johan handed the situation over to Looker and Officer Jenny, eager to avoid entangling himself further.

"Thanks for your help, Johan," Looker said sincerely.

"Don't mention it," Johan replied, gathering his team.

"We'll leave the rest to you."

As they left, Gengar lingered for a moment, staring into the darkness.

"Ka-kah?"

Something in the shadows caught its attention—a faint, green, cell-like entity watching silently.

Unnoticed by all, the tiny green creature observed them with a mix of curiosity and recognition before vanishing into the night.

# 246: Reopening and the Sprouting of the Radishes

Two days later, on Thursday, under clear skies.

Having wrapped up the Ice Sculpture Contest, Johan did not linger in Snowbelle City or even the Kalos region. After a brief stop in Lumiose City to pick up the Indeedee siblings and exchange contact information with the Coffee Couple, Johan flew back to Rustboro City in the Hoenn region.

From now on, all coffee beans for the "Ray of Sunshine" Café would be exclusively sourced from the Coffee Couple's estate. Thanks to their appreciation of Johan's character—and their financial independence—the beans were offered at an exceptionally fair price.

Today marked the first day back at home and the grand reopening of the café.

Johan woke up to find Gengar sprawled on the ceiling, fast asleep.

Even Gengar, it seemed, had wrapped itself in a blanket—though in this case, the blanket was a massive, tattered "Spell Tag."

There probably wasn't another Pokémon in the entire world who could boast such an oversized and peculiar sleeping companion.

Relieved to see Gengar behaving for once, Johan let out a small sigh.

The previous night, he'd dreamed of Mega Gengar punching Yveltal and stomping Groudon—a "super" scene that made him worry he might unconsciously yell, "Gengar, Mega Evolve!" in his sleep.

Such a command might truly turn Gengar into a "Great Demon King."

After all, Mega Gengar's ability changes to Shadow Tag, a trait with significant tactical implications.

[Shadow Tag]: Prevents all non-Ghost-type Pokémon without this ability from fleeing or switching during battle.

While not flashy on the surface, this ability synergizes perfectly with Gengar's combat style, much better than its usual Cursed Body.

Still... Johan couldn't help lamenting:

"Bring back Gengar's Levitate!"

If Mega Gengar ever went on a nighttime stroll, encountering it would be a nightmare for wild Pokémon:

"I want to flee, but I can't."

Add Kubfu's opportunistic toll collector nature and Tinkaton's sticky fingers, and it wouldn't just be impossible to escape—hiding possessions would be futile, too!

The New Unlawful Trio would make their grand debut.

Fortunately, Johan hadn't sleep-talked, and Gengar had remained unusually well-behaved for the night, likely drained from recent travels.

Thinking about that night with the poachers, Johan couldn't help but praise Gengar and the others for their outstanding performance.

"If you guys could bust poachers every night, I'd have no reason to stop you from roaming around."

However, Mega Evolution still required a tremendous amount of energy. Johan needed more practice, but finding someone experienced to learn from was difficult—hardly anyone in the world even knew about Mega Evolution yet.

Once Professor Sycamore's research progressed further, he planned to publish his findings.

At that point, the era of Mega Evolution would officially dawn.

Still, Johan had another lingering question:

Mega Evolution, Dynamax, and Terastalization were all distinct transformations. They seemed incompatible and couldn't coexist.

But... what about Z-Moves? Could they work alongside these transformations?

The thought was both thrilling and terrifying.

Shaking off these musings, Johan stretched and got out of bed. Pulling open the curtains, he was greeted by the chilly embrace of winter.

Frost sparkled on the streets, and a hazy condensation fogged the windows. Though snow hadn't fallen, the growing chill hinted that it was only a matter of time.

"Stepping out of bed feels warmer than staying in," Johan muttered as he glanced at Vulpix.

Vulpix, for its part, seemed oblivious to the fact that it was an Ice-type Pokémon. Despite its natural resistance to cold, it loved burrowing under the covers at night.

Since the Ice Sculpture Contest, its confidence had soared. The feather from Articuno further bolstered its aura of frosty determination. Johan simply hoped the little fox's icy tail wouldn't freeze him completely before spring arrived.

"Oowu~~"

Rubbing its eyes, Vulpix hopped off the bed and joined Johan by the window to enjoy the view.

"You can take another nap if you want," Johan said with a smile, scratching its head.

"Oowu~"

The obedient fox nodded before curling back up.

Downstairs, the house bustled to life as the rest of Johan's Pokémon woke up. Amid the activity, he turned his attention to the newest members of the household: the Indeedee siblings.

The two had been given their own room as part of their employee benefits.

Upon waking, the Indeedee siblings were already donning the new uniforms Johan had prepared for them—gifts he'd promised back in Snowbelle City.

Both siblings seemed delighted with their outfits, especially the sister, who twirled happily in front of the mirror.

"Good morning," Johan greeted them.

"Indeedee~~ (｡⌒⌒｡)"

"Inde... Indeedee~~ (o////o)"

Before Johan even got out of his room, the siblings had already cleaned both the upstairs living quarters and the café downstairs.

Indeedee's Psychic powers made household chores a breeze:

Brooms swept floors on their own. Cloths wiped windows effortlessly. Mops danced across the tiles as if enchanted.

With two Psychic Pokémon in the mix, the café was spotless in record time.

Yet, Johan couldn't help noticing something peculiar.

The sister's Psychic power was leagues ahead of her brother's.

In fact, Gengar and Vulpix privately confided to Johan that her Psychic strength might even surpass Vulpix's.

The realization left Johan slightly dazed.

Another "special case"?

He quickly dismissed the thought.

Unlike his battle-ready Pokémon, the Indeedee siblings were here to "work," not fight.

Still, a fleeting idea crossed his mind:

If I can't assemble a full team for the Indigo League, could I ask the sister to stand in?

Of course, she wouldn't need to fight—just fill the sixth slot.

Ultimately, Johan shook off the thought. The siblings already handled so much: baking, serving, cleaning, assisting with therapy...

"Better not push my luck," he sighed.

After breakfast, the café reopened to a stream of curious customers.

While some regulars were initially wary of the staff changes, their doubts vanished the moment they tasted the new coffee and pastries.

"Wow, this cake is amazing!"

"The coffee tastes different but just as good!"

"I love it! Everything feels so fresh!"

Meanwhile, the Indeedee siblings became crowd favorites. Customers couldn't stop gushing:

"So cute! Can I hug them?"

"They're absolutely adorable!"

As Johan watched the siblings handle their work with grace, he nodded in satisfaction.

Outside in the backyard, Johan tended to his special radish seedlings.

"Ka-kah!"

Gengar crouched near the bed containing the "Spirit Cloth" soil, excitedly pointing at a sprout poking through the earth.

"Oowu..."

On the frosty side, Vulpix carefully examined the snow-covered soil, spotting a tiny green nub barely peeking out.

The radishes were sprouting, albeit slowly.

But with a little help—such as adding the "special snowballs" gifted by the Abomasnow—growth would surely accelerate.

"It's working," Johan murmured.

As his Pokémon busied themselves watering the plants, Johan couldn't help feeling proud of their efforts.

"Let's just hope no wild Pokémon comes along to ruin all this progress," he joked.

Inwardly, however, he began contemplating ways to further enhance the crop's growth rate.

"Maybe... I need a little help from a certain crown-wearing Pokémon."

Far away, in Galar's Crown Tundra, a peculiar Pokémon resembling a white rabbit sneezed, rubbing its nose before settling back into a deep slumber.

# 247: The Sleepless Hypno

5 bonus chapter's coming out at 11 AM EST. So I ask that you withhold you're powerstones until then.

-----------

Kanaz City Pokémon Academy, Pokémon Psychology Consultation Room

Johan sipped his coffee while scrolling through the latest news, particularly articles related to poachers.

At the same time, he continued answering questions from people seeking help with Pokémon psychology online.

By now, Johan's reputation as an expert on Pokémon psychology was growing steadily. While not yet a world-renowned authority, he was undoubtedly gaining recognition.

Common issues, in particular, had become second nature to him.

Clang! Clang! Clang!

A sudden noise drew his attention. Without looking, Johan knew the source—Tinkaton.

The sound was identical to what he'd grown accustomed to hearing on the Isle of Armor when it hammered away at Corviknight feathers every day.

Turning toward the noise, Johan's suspicions were confirmed.

In the corner, Tinkaton was crouched over its hammer, tinkering with something. Beneath the massive mallet lay several smooth, sharp blades.

What caught Johan's eye was how easily the blades—made of some unknown material—bent and deformed under Tinkaton's hammer.

Wait...

Johan blinked, his brain catching up.

Are those... Bisharp's blades?

When had Tinkaton gotten those?

Piecing it together, Johan remembered that one of the poachers they encountered in Snowbelle City had owned a Bisharp.

Nearby, Gengar cheerfully pulled another blade from its mouth and handed it to Tinkaton.

"Ka-kah~!"

Tinkaton wiped its forehead with a tiny hand and gave Gengar an appreciative nod.

The two were getting along swimmingly.

It became clear that during the chaos, Gengar must have swapped Tinkaton's ability and stealthily pocketed Bisharp's blades.

Johan silently mourned Bisharp's misfortune for about three seconds.

Knock, knock.

A knock on the door broke his train of thought, followed by someone entering without waiting for an invitation.

"Johan."

It was Steven, his face adorned with a friendly smile. "Congratulations on winning the Ice Sculpture Contest!"

"Kubfu~!"

Kubfu, who had been dozing on a single-seater couch, jolted awake.

Sensing an opportunity, it leaped to its feet, planted itself in Steven's path, and struck a battle-ready pose.

"Ku! Ku~!"

"Halt! This path is mine to guard!"

Steven: "???"

Johan pinched the bridge of his nose, exasperation evident. He walked over, grabbed Kubfu by the thick fur on its back, and gently moved it aside.

Steven watched, dumbfounded, as Johan effortlessly lifted the sturdy little Pokémon with one hand.

The rumors were true—Johan really was a top-tier martial artist.

No wonder Hoenn's Elite Four member, Bruno, had invited him to spar.

"Ku?"

"This is one of my students," Johan explained, giving Kubfu a meaningful look.

Realizing its mistake, Kubfu bowed apologetically to Steven.

"Ku~!"

My sincerest apologies!

Turning back to Johan, Kubfu's eyes sparkled with admiration.

Its trainer wasn't just talented—he was nurturing students left and right.

Surely, greatness was all but assured!

"Johan, was that...?"

"He's been listening to too many stories about ancient warlords," Johan replied offhandedly.

Recently, not just Kubfu but the entire group—including Gengar—had grown obsessed with Johan's nightly storytelling sessions.

Johan waved dismissively. "Don't mind him. It's his first time in the consultation room."

Steven nodded in understanding, though he couldn't help but feel curious about the new addition.

"By the way, how's your Lileep doing?" Johan asked, changing the subject.

Steven's expression softened into a smile. "Thanks to your guidance, it's slowly adjusting to its new environment and has started training."

"That's great to hear," Johan replied. Reviving ancient Pokémon and helping them adapt to modern life was no easy task.

"Coffee?" Johan offered.

"No, thanks." Steven shook his head, suddenly remembering the real reason for his visit.

He explained that he wasn't there for himself but on behalf of a family friend—specifically, an old associate of his father.

"Who is it?" Johan asked.

Steven elaborated.

The family in question, the Parkes, had a Pokémon suffering from a unique issue—it had stopped sleeping altogether, day or night, causing considerable distress in the household.

After seeking help from various sources, they eventually turned to Steven, who immediately thought of Johan.

"A Pokémon that doesn't sleep?"

Johan rubbed his chin thoughtfully. This was definitely solvable.

After all, whether through hypnosis, soothing techniques, or other methods, Johan—or even Gengar and Vulpix—could handle most sleepless Pokémon.

If push came to shove, Tinkaton's hammer would surely provide a permanent solution.

Still, Johan asked, "What kind of Pokémon are we dealing with?"

"Hypno."

Johan froze. "...What?"

Hypno, known as the Half-Awake Pokémon, was a Psychic-type with a tapir-like appearance. It spent most of its time dozing and was known for feeding on the dreams of humans and Pokémon alike.

Given its sleepy nature, a sleepless Hypno was as concerning as it was baffling.

Sitting in Steven's car en route to the Parkes' rural home, Johan reviewed what he knew about Hypno.

Steven also provided some context about the Parkes family.

The family patriarch, Mr. Bend Parkes, had worked alongside Steven's father, Joseph Stone, during Devon Corporation's early days.

As one of the company's major shareholders, Mr. Parkes had played a crucial role in its foundation. However, ten years ago, he had retired from corporate life to enjoy a quiet existence on a small farm outside Rustboro City.

Their house came into view—a cozy wooden cabin nestled among snowy hills.

As the car pulled up, a pair of elderly figures emerged to greet them.

Standing beside Mr. Parkes were several Pokémon:

A Haxorus and Darmanitan flanked him, exuding a quiet but imposing presence.

Meanwhile, his wife, Mrs. Shannon Parkes, was accompanied by an Ariados, Charjabug, Joltik, and Farfetch'd.

The couple warmly welcomed Johan and Steven, leading them inside the cabin.

The interior was cozy, warmed by a Magcargo nestled within the hearth.

Mr. Parkes explained Hypno's situation in detail, his voice tinged with concern. Mrs. Parkes looked equally worried.

"Can I see it?" Johan asked.

The couple led him upstairs to a closed door, warning him to be cautious.

"It's been irritable from lack of sleep," Mr. Parkes said.

"No problem," Johan reassured them, smiling as he placed a calming hand on the door.

He opened it slowly.

Inside, a Hypno hovered midair, surrounded by thick black smoke that twisted and churned like a living nightmare.

Johan froze.

The smoke's shifting shapes... looked unsettlingly familiar.

Hypno noticed them and turned sharply, its bloodshot eyes glowing with fury. The smoke around it surged violently.

Gengar stepped forward, flashing its signature grin.

"Ka-kah~!"

"Want to experience true terror?"

# 248: The God of Nightmares!

5 bonus chapter's coming out at 11 AM EST. So I ask that you withhold you're powerstones until then.

-----------

Buzz—

In an instant, a surge of Psychic energy erupted from Hypno, rushing toward Johan and Gengar.

Psychic!

"Oowu~~!"

Perched on Johan's shoulder, Vulpix's eyes glimmered as it unleashed its own Psychic power, forming a protective barrier for Johan.

Meanwhile, Gengar grinned wickedly as it slipped further into the room. Its hands rubbed together before conjuring a pitch-black orb.

However, Johan quickly intervened.

"Gengar, don't use Shadow Ball here!"

After all, they were inside someone's home—a wooden cabin, no less. A Shadow Ball would leave them with a hefty repair bill, not to mention some very unhappy homeowners.

Moreover, Mr. Bend and his wife had explicitly avoided harsh methods for subduing Hypno. Otherwise, with his Haxorus, Mr. Bend could have dealt with the situation himself.

Clearly, they had called Johan to resolve this issue in a more gentle and understanding way, while also uncovering the root cause of Hypno's distress.

"Ka-kah?"

Gengar scratched its head, bewildered.

"So, no brute force, huh?"

It glanced at the Shadow Ball in its hands, then shrugged and tossed it into its mouth.

Boom!

A muffled thud echoed as Gengar's body briefly expanded. The orb vanished entirely, leaving Gengar to let out a satisfied burp.

Bend: "???"

Watching this unfold, Mr. Bend was utterly dumbfounded.

"Is… is this some new advanced technique for canceling moves?"

"Lick!" Johan commanded.

Sluuurp—

Gengar's slimy tongue lashed out, grazing Hypno's body. The move's peculiar effect left Hypno temporarily paralyzed.

Gengar had perfected the move, Lick, to an almost absurd degree.

Within its repertoire, this move shone brightly—thanks in no small part to Johan's contributions.

With Hypno briefly immobilized, Vulpix leapt into the room. Snowflakes swirled around it as it cast a shimmering protective veil.

Aurora Veil!

Since Johan had ruled out brute force, his team's options were limited. Otherwise, summoning Tinkaton for one well-placed hammer strike would've been enough to subdue Hypno.

Instead, Johan focused on restricting Hypno while seeking the root of the problem.

However, Hypno remained restless, its Psychic energy pounding relentlessly against the Aurora Veil.

The black smoke rising from Hypno's head twisted and morphed into unsettling shapes, unaffected by the barrier.

In Johan's vision, Hypno's Pokédex entry appeared:

[Hypno]

Type: Psychic

Ability: Forewarn

Moves Known: Moonlight, Moonblast, Psybeam, Imprison, Stored Power, Yawn, Defense Curl, Wonder Room, Magic Coat, Hypnosis, Zen Headbutt…

Mood: Agitated (Plagued by a terrible nightmare, it is too afraid to sleep. This has left it highly irritable.)

Condition: Poor (It desperately needs rest.)

Friendship: -10 ("Do you want to share my nightmare too?!")

A terrible nightmare?

Johan quickly honed in on the crucial detail.

"So the root cause is a nightmare?"

But what kind of nightmare could leave even a Hypno too terrified to sleep?

"Woo~!"

"Watch out!" Mr. Bend called out urgently.

Hypno's glowing red eyes suddenly radiated a familiar aura.

Hypnosis!

Johan paused briefly before holding up a hand to stop Gengar and Vulpix from intervening.

"Hypnosis? Let's see what kind of dream you want me to see."

He murmured softly, a small smile tugging at his lips.

Johan had exceptional resistance to Hypnosis, especially after awakening his Aura abilities.

After all, both Gengar and Vulpix had learned their own Hypnosis techniques from him.

Having served as their primary test subject, Johan had built up an impressive immunity.

If anything, he was practically a "natural anti-sleeping saint."

Still, for safety's sake, Johan released Dragonair from its Poké Ball.

"If I don't wake up soon, make sure to bring me back," he instructed.

Dragonair nodded, though a flicker of concern flashed in its eyes.

As Hypno's Psychic power enveloped him, Johan's vision blurred momentarily before the scenery shifted.

He now stood amidst towering, mist-covered mountains beneath an ink-black sky.

A foreboding darkness seemed to swallow everything below. Every shadow, every gust of wind hinted at an unseen menace.

The oppressive weight of the unknown pressed in from all sides.

Despite the eerie atmosphere, Johan remained calm.

He rested his chin on his hand, smirking. "Hmm… not bad, but Gengar's nightmares are way better."

Though the setting capitalized on fear of the unknown, it lacked Gengar's finesse in crafting truly spine-chilling illusions.

Buzz—

A distant mountain trembled.

From its peak, a blood-red moon began to rise, casting an ominous glow that stained the dark mist crimson.

In the faint light, a shadowy figure emerged, its silhouette becoming clearer.

"Wait..."

Johan's eyes widened in surprise.

The figure resembled a dark, shadowy Pokémon. Its white, wispy "hair" floated eerily above its head, while its red, jagged collar framed its silhouette.

Darkrai.

The legendary Nightmare Pokémon.

So this was the source of Hypno's nightmares?

Realization dawned on Johan.

Darkrai's presence explained everything. A Hypno facing Darkrai's power in a nightmare? No wonder it was too terrified to sleep.

Still, he couldn't help but wonder.

"Shouldn't Darkrai be on Newmoon Island in Sinnoh?"

Before he could ponder further, the shadowy Darkrai turned sharply and lunged at him.

Boom!

The moment it struck, its form shattered like smoke, dissipating into nothingness.

The dreamscape around Johan began to crumble, fragments breaking away into a void.

Johan opened his eyes, adjusting his glasses as he returned to reality.

The sight of his calm demeanor instantly reassured Gengar, Vulpix, and Dragonair.

He gave them a reassuring nod before stepping further into the room.

"It's time for Hypno to rest," he said softly.

"Ka-kah~~!"

Gengar cackled, swirling its hands to release invisible waves of hypnotic energy.

"Oowu~!"

Vulpix leapt onto Johan's shoulder, its eyes glowing as it added its own power to the mix.

Together, they executed the legendary "Johan Family Hypnosis" technique.

Johan's voice, warm and comforting, resonated in Hypno's mind through his Aura.

Now that he understood the source of its distress, the solution was simple: confront and overcome the nightmare.

That was Gengar's job.

Vulpix's role was to follow up with a soothing dream, ensuring Hypno could finally rest peacefully.

"Woo… woo…"

Hypno's glowing red eyes slowly dimmed and shut. The dark smoke around it lightened, transforming into pink, wispy clouds before dissipating entirely.

Its breathing steadied as it floated gently to the floor, fully enveloped in sleep.

"Is it… cured?" Mr. Bend asked hesitantly, watching the now peacefully slumbering Pokémon.

Johan nodded, wiping his brow. "It should be fine now."

"Still, I'd like to know more about its recent activities. Where has it been, and what might it have encountered?"

As he spoke, Johan gently closed the door to Hypno's room.

Back downstairs, Mr. Bend recounted the events leading up to Hypno's strange behavior.

"...That day, I was out chopping wood with Haxorus when Hypno floated out the window. It wasn't unusual for it to collect dreams—it has a natural sensitivity to them, after all…"

From the description, Johan pieced things together. Hypno's Forewarn ability must have allowed it to glimpse something—perhaps even a fragment of Darkrai's domain—triggering the nightmares.

Far away, on the secluded Newmoon Island, a shadow stirred.

Darkrai's red eyes snapped open.

"What...?"

It wiped its forehead, puzzled.

"Was that… a dream? I could've sworn I saw Giratina!"

# 249: Bankrupting for an Ice Stone (BONUS)

After listening to Mr. Bend's account, Johan had pieced together the cause of Hypno's nightmares. However, when it came to dealing with the Nightmare Pokémon, Darkrai, he had no concrete solutions.

For now, he advised the couple to monitor Hypno closely and promised to assist again if the problem persisted.

At the invitation of Mr. Bend and Mrs. Shannon, Johan and Steven enjoyed a simple yet delicious homemade meal.

Johan couldn't help but appreciate the slow, idyllic pace of rural life.

On the way back…

Johan's phone vibrated with a notification.

[Your account: +500,000 Pokédollars. Remaining balance: 4,036,723 Pokédollars.]

A single session of Hypno therapy had earned him half a million Pokédollars.

The generosity of Mr. Bend and Mrs. Shannon left Johan secretly astonished.

It also reinforced a sobering realization: to enjoy such a carefree lifestyle, one first needed financial freedom. Otherwise, it wouldn't take long before the idyllic dream turned into collective hunger.

Still, Johan admitted to himself that he enjoyed "worldly" patients like this.

If only every client could be as "worldly."

With his bank balance crossing the four-million mark for the first time, Johan began to contemplate his next steps.

"Steven," Johan asked from the car, "you're knowledgeable about stones. Do you know where I could find a high-quality Ice Stone?"

Steven looked up from his phone, pondering for a moment before replying:

"The best Ice Stones are rare and expensive. But I do have connections. If you want, I can gift you one—"

"Stop." Johan raised a hand, cutting him off mid-sentence.

At that moment, Johan regretted being Steven's teacher.

If he weren't Steven's mentor, he wouldn't have to refuse such a tempting offer. But as his teacher, Johan couldn't bring himself to accept something so extravagant.

"Just tell me the price," Johan insisted, suppressing his inner turmoil.

"For the very best Ice Stone," Steven hesitated, knowing Johan's pride, "it would cost around four million Pokédollars."

Four million?!

How did Steven know exactly how much was in Johan's bank account?!

If Johan bought the Ice Stone, he'd be left with just over 30,000 Pokédollars—a devastating blow to his finances.

But Johan also understood that Steven's price was likely the best in the entire Pokémon world.

Seeing Johan wrestle with the decision, Steven hesitated before suggesting, "I could cover part of the cost for you—"

"No need," Johan interrupted firmly, gritting his teeth.

"Buy it!"

Four million Pokédollars? Small change.

This Ice Stone wasn't just for Vulpix's potential evolution—it was an investment.

Even though Vulpix was hesitant about evolving, Johan wanted to be prepared for the moment it decided.

"Oowu~~"

Vulpix blinked its bright blue eyes, letting out a soft cry of approval.

The next morning…

A package arrived via Pelipper Delivery.

To Gengar and Tinkaton's disappointment, it wasn't delivered by Pidgey or Pidgeotto.

The Pelipper dropped the package, then flew off at top speed without even waiting for a tip.

"Ka-kah…" Gengar withdrew its outstretched hand, sulking.

However, the thrill of opening packages quickly lifted its spirits.

"Ka-kah~~!"

With nimble hands, Gengar ripped through the packaging, revealing a finely crafted wooden box.

Johan carefully opened it.

Whoosh—

A chilling aura filled the room as soon as the box was opened.

"Oowu~~!"

Vulpix's eyes sparkled, and it excitedly wagged its six tails.

Inside was a perfectly polished, diamond-like Ice Stone, its pale blue surface gleaming brilliantly.

Holding the Ice Stone, Johan sighed.

"So this is what bank-breaking looks like."

A single stone worth four million Pokédollars… Collecting stones was an even more expensive hobby than collecting antiques.

Johan's thoughts drifted briefly to the Pokémon Special manga.

"In Vermilion City's port waters, there were Eternal Evolution Stones for fire, water, thunder, and grass. I wonder if they're still there—or if they even exist in this world."

Eternal Evolution Stones were legendary items capable of inducing evolution without being consumed. Priceless treasures by any standard.

Johan shook his head, refocusing on the present.

Looking at Vulpix, who gazed at the Ice Stone with conflicted eyes, he asked gently:

"Have you made up your mind, Vulpix?"

Vulpix hesitated, its eyes glinting with hope.

But after a moment, it stepped back and shook its head.

"Not yet, huh?" Johan smiled knowingly, carefully putting the Ice Stone back in its box.

What Johan didn't know was that Vulpix's hesitation wasn't out of fear or indecision about evolution itself.

It worried that evolving would mean losing the ability to cuddle on Johan's shoulder, curl up in his arms, or snuggle in his lap.

"Ka-kah!"

Gengar suddenly shouted, pointing at the wooden box.

It turned out the box had a hidden compartment containing two smaller, darker stones.

These were Mini Dusk Stones, similar to the Mini Ice Stones Johan had received before.

Dream Eater had gifted a Mini Dusk Stone once, but Gengar had long since consumed it.

"Looks like these are from Steven," Johan mused, touched by his thoughtful student.

"Ka-kah!"

Gengar wasted no time popping one of the stones into its mouth, savoring it like candy.

With the Ice Stone in hand, Johan felt a sense of relief.

To celebrate, he announced, "Feast tonight! Dragonair, go ask the caretakers what they want to eat."

"Draaa~!"

Dragonair gleefully slid down the staircase, eagerly relaying the message.

Seven peaceful (and frugal) days passed…

During this time, Johan prepared the final exam papers for his Pokémon Psychology class and submitted them to the administration.

With most of his responsibilities handled, his winter vacation had unofficially begun.

One day, in the middle of writing training plans for his team, Johan received a call from Matori in Kanto.

"Johan, we've wrapped up this phase of filming. Would you like to review the edited episodes?"

"That fast?" Johan was surprised.

He had written a thirty-episode script, and only three episodes of Meowth & Pichu had aired so far.

"Not only that," Matori continued, "we need you to weigh in on sponsorship deals, advertising, and broadcast rights."

"Alright, I'll come to Kanto," Johan agreed.

He also realized something else:

The Indigo League registration was approaching.

Johan glanced at his team. He still needed a sixth Pokémon, and he was one badge short.

If he wanted that lucrative prize money…

"Matori, book me a ticket to Kanto," Johan said decisively.

"Understood."

Hanging up, Johan turned to see his team gathered behind him.

"Ka-kah?"

"Are we challenging the final Gym?" Gengar asked excitedly.

"The strongest Gym?"

The others leaned in expectantly.

Johan scratched his cheek, hesitating.

"Well… something like that."

The team erupted in cheers, each eager to prove itself.

"Ka-kah!"

"Draaa!"

"Ku!"

Kubfu even tugged Johan's pants, declaring itself ready to take on Cerulean City's Gym Leaders.

Johan sighed.

"Let's hope the Cerulean Sisters don't cry…"

With that, Johan packed his bags and flew to Kanto's Saffron City.

Unbeknownst to him, the Rainbow Wing tucked in his pocket began to glow faintly, as if sensing something extraordinary on the horizon.

# 250: The Embodiment of the North Wind (BONUS)

December 2nd, Friday, Cloudy.

A gentle breeze swept across Cerulean City's port.

As a mid-sized city in Kanto, Cerulean didn't have an airport, but its bustling harbor compensated for it.

After stepping out of a taxi, Johan decided to visit the Pokémon Center first to check on his team's condition.

After all, a week of continuous training might have hidden effects that needed attention.

At the Pokémon Center…

To Johan's surprise, the place was unusually crowded with trainers and injured Pokémon.

Did Lt. Surge conquer Cerulean Gym?

This level of chaos seemed more fitting for the Thunder Lieutenant's antics than the Cerulean Sisters' calm demeanor.

The sight of so many wounded Pokémon even unsettled Gengar and the others.

Looks like the last Gym Leader's strength is no joke, Johan thought.

"Mr. Johan, are you here for that Pokémon as well?"

Nurse Joy's cheerful greeting caught Johan off guard as he reached the counter.

"If anyone can capture it, it might just be you," she said with a smile.

"Pokémon?" Johan blinked. "I'm just here to challenge the Cerulean Gym."

"The Cerulean Gym?" Joy tilted her head, surprised.

As a beginner-friendly gym, Cerulean was known for its simplicity, often serving as a first stop for new trainers to build confidence.

But Nurse Joy quickly added, "Unfortunately, the Gym is closed today."

"Closed?"

"Fridays are their usual maintenance days to prepare for the weekend water ballet shows."

Of course it is, Johan thought, exasperated. Classic Cerulean.

"So, why are there so many injured Pokémon?"

"It's because of a certain Pokémon."

Nurse Joy began explaining:

"Rumor has it that a powerful and mysterious Pokémon has appeared near Cerulean City. Many trainers have flocked here, hoping to capture it."

The wounded Pokémon in the Center were casualties of failed capture attempts.

This wasn't an unusual phenomenon in the Pokémon world.

For instance, the Meteor Falls in Hoenn drew crowds after reports of trainers capturing Bagon there.

Similarly, stories of wild Dratini in Kanto attracted hopeful trainers to specific river areas.

Johan nodded but didn't dwell on it.

Since the Cerulean Gym was closed until tomorrow, Johan decided to visit the film crew instead.

Coincidentally, the shoot location was near the coastline between Cerulean City and Vermilion City—an ideal opportunity to multitask.

Outside the Pokémon Center…

"Where's Gengar?"

Johan noticed his mischievous companion was missing.

"Oowu!"

Vulpix, perched on Johan's shoulder, pointed toward a nearby street.

Moments later, Gengar floated toward them, waving cheerfully.

"Ka-kah!"

"Where were you?" Johan asked suspiciously.

Gengar didn't even attempt to hide its actions.

"Ka-kah~~"

I went to deliver a challenge letter.

A mental image of Gengar squatting in front of Cerulean Gym, licking an envelope shut before slipping it under the door, popped into Johan's mind.

You're really eager for this Gym battle, aren't you?

1:00 PM.

Johan rented a small motorboat at Cerulean's port, planning to sail downstream to the film crew's location.

The boat wasn't large, but it was spacious enough for Johan and his Pokémon, and its slow speed made it easy to control.

Gengar donned sunglasses and positioned itself at the helm, gripping the wheel confidently.

The gentle breeze ruffled its grin as it declared, "Ka-kah! Watch me pilot this boat like a pro!"

SPLASH!

Out of nowhere, a Magikarp leapt out of the water near the boat.

With its usual vacant stare, it flicked its tail once and surged ahead, leaving the boat in its wake.

Gengar: "..."

Is this really a motorboat? Even the school daycare's old Granbull could swim faster.

Frustrated, Gengar glanced at the sluggish Magikarp splashing water in front of them.

A mischievous grin spread across its face as it activated Thunder Punch.

Electric currents surged through the boat's motor.

WHIRRR!

In an instant, the boat sped up, overtaking the Magikarp and leaving it behind.

"Ka-kah~~" Gengar taunted, waving at the Magikarp. So long, loser.

Meanwhile, Johan and the others were seated at the back of the boat, playing a game of Ludo.

Kubfu, stuck at the start for several turns, eventually gave up and began practicing punches on the deck.

When it was Johan's turn to play, he found himself on the receiving end of smug grins from Vulpix, Tinkaton, and Dragonair.

"Let me show you all why they call me the 'Ludo King,'" Johan declared confidently.

90 minutes later…

"Six, six, six! I need a six!" Johan yelled.

Desperate, he added, "I'll sacrifice Gengar's lifespan for a six!"

"Ka-kah!!" Gengar protested, feeling its imaginary years slipping away.

Finally, Johan got his first piece onto the board, triumphantly declaring, "The comeback begins now!"

Unfortunately, by this point, Vulpix had already dozed off, and Tinkaton was only a few moves away from victory.

Even Dragonair, typically unlucky, was well ahead with three pieces in play.

Suddenly, a loud bang echoed from the rear deck.

Turning around, Johan saw smoke and flames rising from the engine.

"Did… the motor just explode?"

Everyone turned to Gengar, who was quietly trying to sneak into the cabin.

"Gengar," Johan said coldly, grabbing its spike, "care to explain?"

"Ka-kah~~" Gengar chuckled nervously. It… um… exploded on its own?

Sitting by the railing, paddling with a wooden board, Johan muttered, "I should've known better than to let Gengar handle this."

"Ka-kah~~" Gengar tried to defend itself. But wasn't that Magikarp's fault too?

Johan shot it a withering glare.

Facts aside, what's your point?

The stranded boat drifted aimlessly in the middle of the river. Returning to Cerulean or pushing onward to Vermilion seemed equally impossible.

"Can you fix it?" Johan asked Tinkaton.

Tinkaton examined the motor and nodded confidently. "Cha!"

But some parts were beyond repair, and they needed replacement materials.

Suddenly, Dragonair glided down from above, its tail pointing toward the riverbank.

"Draaa!"

It excitedly called out, indicating a structure nearby.

"How far is it?" Johan asked.

Dragonair hesitated, unsure how to estimate the distance.

"Let's head that way," Johan decided. "Maybe we'll find something useful to repair the motor."

Whoosh!

A cold gust swept across the river, propelling the boat forward.

"Oowu!"

Vulpix leapt up, pointing toward the misty riverbank.

Amid the dense fog, a majestic silhouette emerged.

Johan's eyes widened as he recognized the figure.

"The embodiment of the north wind… Suicune?"

Suicune, the Aurora Pokémon, was one of Ho-Oh's legendary trio. Known as the incarnation of the north wind, Suicune often appeared where clear waters flowed.

Johan instinctively reached into his pocket, pulling out the Rainbow Wing.

The feather's glow pulsed softly, catching Suicune's attention.

Its gaze softened, though it seemed puzzled by the artifact.

Johan channeled his Aura into the feather, causing it to radiate with divine light.

Suicune nodded approvingly, its elegant movements exuding a calm authority.

"Draaa…"

A melodic call echoed as waves rippled across the river.

Suicune's north wind pushed the boat forward, guiding them downstream.

"It's leading us somewhere," Johan murmured, understanding Suicune's intent.
